> **Domain owner:** `world-gen-controller` · **Reports to:** Admin · **Phase:** web pivot, W5/W8 · **Last updated:** 2026-08-21 · **Status:** **WG-230 to WG-234: THE PER-CHUNK FLOAT64 WORLD PHASE REACHES THE SHADER, AND HALF OF IT HAD BEEN WRITTEN AND UNCONSUMED SINCE 2026-07-28 (2026-08-21, `lane/wg-phase`).** Full record in section 6.10; frames `docs/screenshots/WG230_*`. **`ChunkPhase.ts` shipped WG-50's float64 reduction three weeks ago and nothing in `web/src` ever imported it**, which is why `TerrainArt.glsl.ts`'s header still names the phase as owed; this lane is the path to the GPU, not a redesign. **`aPhase` is `frac(anchor / 256 m)`, a vec3 float32 per-chunk constant replicated across the slot's vertices**, and the wire format is untouched for RN-2265's reason exactly (a pure float64 function of an anchor `ChunkBatch` already retains). **`frac` ALONE and not WG-50's `(cell, frac)` pair, because a tiling texture fetch never sees an integer lattice cell**: three floats instead of six, 6.4 MiB of pooled vertex memory instead of 12.8. **THE PERIOD IS A PRECISION BUDGET** (quantum = period / 2^23, so doubling it doubles the quantum everywhere) and the ONLY seam rule is that a consumer's period must divide it, which `assertPhasePeriod` throws on. **THE ARITHMETIC: 30.5 um against `pM`'s 31.25 mm at both 2 m and 75 m (1024x), 61.0 um at 600 m, 1.95 mm at the 38 km horizon** — but read quanta per pixel, not the ratio: `pM` carries **0.114 at two metres and 4.27 at 75 m**, and by 600 m it is at 34 where precision alone would not justify the attribute. **THE BLOCKING DEFECT IS STRUCTURAL:** the splat rides `vChunkUv`, which normalises over the quad, so a far rung on it steps in world scale at every LOD ring. **THE FREE ALTERNATIVE IS REFUSED ON ONE NUMBER:** a per-frame `frac(origin/P)` uniform needs no attribute at all, but `FloatingOrigin.thresholdM` is 4,000 m, so the near-field quantum would breathe by 2^11 across one rebase cycle. **NO-OP PROVEN AS A TWO-BUILD PAIR**, three fresh processes an arm, sentinel server killed by PID: `propsPlaced` 41,300 / 52,139, `cellsScattered` 25,655 / 26,124, every committed rectangle and both program counts identical to the digit; the cost that is NOT zero is 29.2 -> 37.8 MiB of pool preallocation. The harvest ring is deliberately excluded with a literal `1` (bit-identical), because a 760 m mask over a 620 m ring means a spawn that rolls a clearing has no wood. **AND THE ARITHMETIC THAT IS THE REAL FINDING:** closing 40 per cent of the ground with 44 m2 crowns needs 11,600 trees per km2, half a million instances over the flyover's disc, **115 ms in a 16.6 ms frame**. There is no density that reaches forest-credible cover one-instance-per-tree at this range. No depth fixes steep-ground silhouette stepping. **WHAT THE CHANGE DOES BUY IS ALSO MEASURED:** the normals resolve 0.9 m relief instead of 1.8 m, so sub-1.8 m shape reaches the SHADING; with the scatter held off (`?props=0`, one flag apart) the `forestfloor` frame moves **48.91% of its pixels, both ways** (289,923 darker / 267,530 lighter) against a **same-config control that moves 0.78% with the §2.1 box bit-identical**, and the box gains **25.1% of iqr at 1.0% of luma**. The airbrushed ground gets grain. **COST, PRICED WITH 4 INTERLEAVED REPEATS PER ARM BECAUSE THE FIRST TWO SWEEPS DISAGREED ON THE SIGN** (+2.10 ms on one, -0.80 on the next; a non-interleaved sweep lets thermal drift land on one arm): high tier p50 **8.00 -> 9.40 ms** against the 16.6 ms budget, delta 2.3x md14's own 0.60 ms within-arm spread so it is real, **+112,243 triangles (+15.1%)**, near chunks 169 -> 184, pool never exhausted (133 of 384 free), VRAM unchanged at 104.2 MB. md15's own spread is 2.60 ms, so this also buys a slightly lumpier frame and that is the honest half. **PER TIER, ONLY HIGH'S DELTA SURVIVES ITS OWN NOISE:** low 6.20 -> 6.50 (+0.30 against a 1.50 ms spread, NOT separated, and md15 is the steadier arm there at 0.30); med, over 7 runs, has a ~5 ms within-arm spread that swallows any delta and is reported as unseparated rather than as a number. Every reading on every tier at both depths is under the 16.6 ms budget. Triangles md14 -> md15: low +17.7%, med +15.6%, high +15.1%. Verified with NO url override that the default reaches it: `feetDepth 15, cellM 0.899, containsFeet true, poolExhausted 0`. **HEIGHTS DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE HARD CONSTRAINT:** `lodheights.js` over six sites across four biomes finds the oracle **bit-equal at four and differing by 1.4e-12 and 3.7e-11 m at the other two, i.e. below one float64 ULP of the 6e5 m radius** (the query POINT moved by picometres because the walker settles on a marginally different chunk set; the field is untouched), and where the nearest DRAWN vertex is the same one in both meshes its height agrees to **9.2e-8 and 1.1e-7 m**, float32 on a 4.7 km height. Collision cannot have moved at all: ARCHITECTURE §5.3, there is no mesh collision, the capsule tests the analytic oracle directly. **THE ONE COUPLING THIS FOUND, AND IT IS THE TRANSFERABLE PART: `CLUSTER_SHIFT` IS EXPRESSED IN CELLS, SO IT MOVED ON ITS OWN.** The scatter's patch size (a "stand of something") was shift 3 = 8x8 cells = 14.46 m at the old cell and would have become **7.19 m** at the new one, i.e. an authored look scale silently halving as a side effect of a tessellation change. Shift 4 restores 14.38 m, within 0.6%. Any tuning value expressed in CELLS rather than metres is silently a function of `maxDepth`; this was the only one found, and restoring it recovered **2.36 of the 4.04 luma** the §2.1 groundNear box had swung. **THE RESIDUAL IS A RE-SEED AND NOT A LOSS, PROVED RATHER THAN ARGUED:** scatter density is per cell, so a finer lattice is a different hash, and `placedPerM2` holds at **0.46431 -> 0.46466 with `deliveredFraction` 1.0002 in BOTH arms**, zero cells and zero chunks capped, instances 46,684 -> 45,943. The §2.1 box ends at **26.54 -> 28.22 luma (+6.3%)**, which is OUT of band and **the §2.1 luminance table has to be re-taken at this LOD rather than carried across** (owed to rendering). **THE REAL NEXT STEP IS NOT MORE TRIANGLES:** the artifact left on flat ground is sub-metre SHADING detail, the machinery for it already exists on both sides (RN-45's per-chunk noise phase landed at WG-50 to WG-54 in `ChunkPhase.ts`; the dug voxel face already got an analytic sub-metre bump at RN-1258), and art-campaign decision 2 refuses tangent-frame terrain for this campaign, so this lane did not touch it. Previous status, **WG-166 to WG-171: THE WORLD ACTUALLY SHOWS THE RUIN (2026-08-13).** WG-151 built the bridge and RN-1450 built the mesh, and between them the site 753.8 m from spawn still drew NOTHING: `Sites.ts` had exactly one consumer in the whole client and it was `DebugSites.ts`. `web/src/game/RuinSites.ts` closes it: every ruin-kind row in the body's `SiteCatalog` becomes a drawn instance, a collider in the walker's own solid set, and a garrison. **THE PLACEMENT READS THE ASSET RATHER THAN RESTATING IT.** `socket_grade` (2.2999999523 m in the shipped bytes) is the plane that goes on `FSite.pos`, so the pivot is the site position pushed 2.3 m down the normal and the buried course has somewhere to be; the number is never typed into the client, and a .glb that stops publishing the socket is REFUSED with a sentence rather than silently sunk. Orientation is `poi.h`'s own `yawRad`, already hashed off the site id, not a second hash here. **COLLISION IS THE ONE SOLID SET AND THAT ORDERING IS THE ONE REAL TRAP THIS LANE FOUND:** `restoreStructures` calls `Structures.reset()` -> `bodies.clear()`, which throws away every solid in the world including ones the base layer never heard of, so a ruin placed at boot is invisible to the walker the moment any save loads. The ruin is therefore placed BEFORE `await g.load()` and reseated by `Persist.apply` immediately after the wipe, which puts the repair on the path every boot takes rather than on the rare one; `RestoreLedger.ruinsReseated` is the number that says it ran. **THE GARRISON SEAM IS WIRED AND THE STAND-IN IS DELETED.** `Enemies.spawnGarrison(host, sitePos, idLo)` is called at world build, and `spawnGarrisonDebug`, GP-98's synthetic post 45 m east of the player, written down as temporary on the day it shipped, is gone, along with `EnemyDebug.ts`'s named exception, so that file's "no spawn, no wave, no kill" refusal is blanket again. **THE LOD LADDER IS DERIVED, NOT CHOSEN:** `NodeBatch`'s measured 55 m / 165 m rungs are 14.70 and 44.10 bounding radii of the conifer that calibrated them (`TreeConifer_Full_LOD0`, 6.500 m tall, 3.741 m bound), and the ruin's own bound is measured off the loaded geometry at 25.53 m, so it switches at 375.4 m and 1,126.1 m and draws its 6,200-triangle LOD1 from spawn instead of 15,920. **MEASURED GREEN, `probes/ruinplace.js`, `fails: []` on Forge AND on `--body=cinder`:** standoff between the site radius and the live oracle radius is **0.000000 m**; Measured against a span-only gate calibrated to admit the same fraction: at 18 m they disagree about **79 sites each way (72 tilt, 7 residual)**, at 30 m about **235 each way (23 tilt, 212 residual)**. **Read honestly, that says the residual gate does little work at the SHIPPED 18 m footprint** and becomes dominant only at 30 m; it is kept because it is free, because it is the only thing between a bigger site type and a hole under a building, and because adding a gate later means re-deriving a threshold nobody has data for. **THE ART SPEC IS DERIVED, NOT ASKED FOR:** worst admissible ground is `18*tan(4)` = 1.26 m of drop plus 1.0 m of residual, so **a ruin needs a plinth that absorbs about 2.3 m at its rim**, and nothing in this project levels terrain under a static structure. **THE SHIPPED RUIN:** lat 1.89775 lon 144.08537, **1,394.6 m from spawn**, tilt 0.720 deg, residP95 0.115 m, found on candidate 21 of 4096; the walk is **17.86 deg worst grade at a 20 m arm, +88.3 m net climb, 0 wet samples, 5.1 minutes at 4.6 m/s**. The walk is asserted on the shipped instance and deliberately NOT a generator gate, because a straight great circle is the WORST case and gating on it would refuse good sites for a reason that is not true. **THE ID IS KEYED ON THE ORDINAL, NEVER THE POSITION**, so a terrain change may move a ruin 689.0 m and every save's "I have been here" bit still points at it (proven; id `0x9f051605d9480737` unchanged). **A GATE NO INPUT COULD REACH, found by a control that would not go red:** the first `admit` tested the pad blend before the water, and Forge's only water is a pond 55 m from `homeDir`, INSIDE the 600 m blend, so **the water gate was unreachable by any input and read exactly like a working one.** Reordered, which is also the cheaper order. **THE BODY QUESTION IS ASKED:** Cinder places nothing and says why, an unknown body places nothing and says why, and **Forge's exact terrain under Cinder's id still places nothing** while the same ground under Forge's id places one, which is the shape `seedNests` was missing. **AND THE FINDING THAT IS BIGGER THAN THE TASK. The spawn is 4,667.789 m in the MOUNTAINS against a `Config.ts` comment that says "a Hills valley floor at 2,963 m": drift +1,704.789 m and a biome change nobody wrote down.** `TREELINE_BARE_M` is 1850 m, so spawn is **2,817.8 m above the bare treeline**, and swept at 20,000 candidates a band it is **0 below the treeline and 100% Mountains at 700 to 1400 m, at 1500 to 6000 m AND at 6000 to 20000 m**; the first sub-treeline sample appears at 24 to 28 km. **The storyline's first goal is "gather wood" and the only wood within 20 km is the 14 hand-placed starter trees.** Raised, not fixed: moving `homeDir` re-baselines the pad, the pond, the probes, the screenshots and the saves. **The ruin is immune to it either way, because the anchor is read from `body.homeDir` and a moved spawn moves the ruin with it.** Also found: **`?seed=` does not move the spawn** (`homeDir` is a DW-14 literal calibrated for one seed; on seed 0x7 all 4096 candidates are refused as Ocean), and **the spawn pad's blend reaches a 42.1% grade** against its own comment's claim of one percentage point. ****THE BRIDGE IS NOW BUILT (WG-151, ABI 24, 2026-08-12): `of_poi_*`, `poiabi.ts`, `Sites.ts`, a second `known_` bit, `SaveSlot.poi`, proven by a real `page.reload()`.** OWED: the ruin asset, and the keep-out wiring in TreeField/RockField/scatter.** Previous status, **WG-141 to WG-148: THE MOON IS REACHABLE AND IT IS MADE OF CRATERS, AND THREE THINGS ABOUT CINDER HAD SIMPLY NEVER BEEN REVISITED (2026-08-02).** Reid: *"go ahead and start working on the moon. This will be a fully traversable thing, we will be landing on it later."* **SIZING FIRST, because it sizes everything: Cinder was 100% real in `/core` and 0% reachable in the CLIENT.** `PlanetBody.cinder()` existed with ZERO callers, `Boot.ts:107` built Forge unconditionally, and `terrain.worker.ts:42` plus `oracle.worker.ts:50` hard-coded `_of_body_create_forge` behind a `TerrainInitMsg` that had no body field at all. Every WASM call site already threaded a handle, so the job was **plurality, not un-hardcoding**: one `BodyId`, one `createBodyHandle` rule in `PlanetBody.ts`, the field on the two worker protocols, and a `?body=cinder` flag. No ABI change, and deliberately none: **physics held ABI 22 uncommitted while I worked, so I took no ABI number.** **THE TERRAIN WAS THE PRE-WG-25 STACK: Forge got the 2026 rework and Cinder never did.** Its finest content was an 1,111 m wavelength (`terrain_probe --body cinder`, measured against Forge's 60 m), so the smallest landform on the whole moon was a kilometre wide. It is now a **nine-rung crater ladder from 28 km down to 1.8 m**, frequency tripling and amplitude thirding, which reproduces the N(>D)~D^-2 production law and the constant 0.12 depth-to-diameter ratio in one construction; amplitudes come from two closed forms (`14800/f` full, `6080/f` confined) rather than from taste. Four rungs use the full 27-cell neighbourhood and five a **CONFINED single-cell variant that costs a twelfth**, which is the only reason nine rungs are affordable at all: measured 492,507 verts/sec against the old 1,086,656, i.e. **2.21x**. **SLOPE WAS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT AND IT NEARLY GAVE ME A CONFIDENT WRONG ANSWER: a crater wall is a 24% to 48% grade at EVERY scale, so the old moon reported a healthy 0.554 m median step over 4 m and the rewrite looks like a 1.3x tweak on slope. On CURVATURE, which is what separates "tilted" from "featured", it is 33x.** **THREE REAL DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, none of them what I set out to do.** (1) `craterField`'s 3x3x3 neighbourhood **was too small for the radius it allowed**: reach was 1.2 cells, so a crater two cells away could be clipped mid-rim, and the measured worst case is a **470 m cliff out of nothing**, 346 times in 160,000 samples. Capping the radius at 0.625 makes reach exactly 1.0 and 3x3x3 exactly sufficient; `crater_neighbourhood_is_sufficient` proves it against a 5x5x5 sweep AND fires on the old coefficients in the same breath. (2) The crater PROFILE was **discontinuous at t == 1**, stepping half the layer amplitude at every rim on every body with craters: a **1,400 m vertical wall ringing every crater** on the old moon. (3) The moon's design layer **still switched gain on the DISCRETE biome**, WG-25's planet pathology surviving one body over, putting 120 m and 40 m walls along contour lines; now a continuous `designedGainForMoonRelief`, worst step at a crossing 173.8 m against the retired form's 287.5 m. **THE PICTURE IS THE ARBITER AND IT OVERTURNED THE READING TWICE.** From 12 km with `forgeAtmosphere()` the moon is a **white fog bank with no visible craters**; the identical camera with `--atmos=0` is a black sky, stars, a hard terminator and overlapping craters at four scales. **The airless look is not polish, it is the difference between a moon and a fog bank, and it is ROUTED TO RENDERING, not built here.** Determinism is untouched (WG-6 position-hash preserved, no LOD gating, both bitwise seam proofs still pass) and **FORGE IS BIT-IDENTICAL: of the 345 leaf values in `expected.json`, exactly 24 moved and every one of them is a moon value.** 35/35 ctest, parity Tier 0 126/0 and Tier A 108/0. **OWED: the wasm binary and `expected.json` are regenerated in the working tree but DELIBERATELY NOT COMMITTED, because two physics commits landed after my build; the moon reaches the browser at the next rebuild from a settled tree. Also owed: 51 starter nodes including broadleaf trees place on Cinder regardless of body, ambient collapses to near-black with the sky off, and the airless profile itself.** Previous status, **WG-116 to WG-122: THERE ARE NO SCENERY TREES. EVERY TREE IN THE WORLD IS A HARVEST NODE, AND THE PASS IS NET CHEAPER THAN THE FAKES IT REPLACES (2026-08-01).** Reid's ruling verbatim: "there should be no scenery trees. all trees should be minable." `TreeField` streams wood harvest nodes in a 620 m ring as pure functions of (seed, 28 m lattice cell), through the SAME `/core` calls a rock uses; the scenery canopy tier is retired to a `?canopy=0` default rather than deleted, so `?canopy=620` still reproduces the WG-59 world in the SAME binary. **Determinism proven by round trip at two sites: 2,233 of 2,233 trees and 813 of 813 bit-identical after leaving and returning.** The density table is the retired tier's OWN asks row for row, and that is a CORRECTION the picture caught and no counter did: a first table derived to match the canopy's ring AVERAGE delivered 0.9891 and looked wrong, because the canopy is at full density inside 300 m and only thins beyond, so its average was never the number the eye read. Size is now yield: a tree's scale is a linear map of /core's grade, which is what `InitialAmount` is multiplied by, and the band was re-derived off the SHIPPED BYTES after the first one (built from a docstring's "6.5 m tree") put the median tree a third too short. **THE CRUX IS WG-118 AND IT IS AN ASSET ALREADY PAID FOR: `NodeBatch` matched `_LOD0` only, while `_LOD1` and `_LOD2` meshes ship in every node .glb in the project.** Naively, 2,226 tree nodes cost 7,558,472 triangles at Forest; loading those LODs takes it to 1,895,584 (-74.9%) and per-instance culling to 1,365,739 (-81.9%), and even sites with NO trees gain 111 k to 135 k because the rocks, the spires and the clearing get the tiers too (this closes R10). Against HEAD the shipping world is **-112,306 triangles at Forest for 2,226 real trees against 1,560 fakes, -15,527 at the RN-15 camera for 799 against 462**, and bit-identical at Beach, Beach2, Hills and the Mountains spawn. Programs, geometries and VRAM do not move anywhere. **NEGATIVE CONTROL: `--trees=0 --canopy=620 --nodelod=0 --nodecull=0` reproduces pure HEAD IDENTICALLY on every published column at all eight sites, 8 of 8.** Persistence is cell-keyed and additive (`SaveSlot.trees`, SAVE_VERSION deliberately unmoved), proven by a REAL `page.reload()`: chop 18 wood off a streamed tree 70.2 m from spawn, reload, and the tree returns at the same position and `remaining` 12.358138084411621 exactly, drained from the PENDING path. Refusals: the treeline emptied 4,633 cells at the Mountains spawn, the pond refused 4, the clearing keep-out refused 14 at a Forest spawn (and read 0 until a frame bug in it was found by expected-count), and **the slope gate CANNOT FIRE: a 26,120-sample hill-climb finer than its own arm puts the steepest sub-treeline ground on Forge at 29.15 degrees against a 43.95 degree gate.** Trees do not respawn, matching the rocks. **OWED: props_canopy's three tall species want Full/Half/Low variants so the parasol forms can come back as node art; the far-field impostor ask (WG-63) still stands; the ring edge still pops; and `RockField` should inherit `TreeField`'s forget path.**
> Read alongside: [MASTER_PLAN](../MASTER_PLAN.md) · [AGENT_ARCHITECTURE](../AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md) · [ADMIN](ADMIN.md) · **[Spike 1 world-gen design](../spikes/spike1-worldgen.md)** · [Spike 1 core-engine](../spikes/spike1-core-engine.md) (contract built against)

## 1. Mission
Procedurally generate believable, deterministic-from-seed planets and moons: terrain, biomes, resource deposits, and points of interest — streamable as LOD chunks, deformable where needed, and cheap to persist (seed + diffs).

## 2. Scope & owned subsystems
- **Cubed-sphere quadtree terrain** (KSP PQS-style) — six quadtree-subdivided cube faces on a sphere; noise-driven heightfield; streaming LOD.
- **Biomes & surface materials** — climate/latitude/altitude driven; feeds rendering materials + gameplay science.
- **Resource deposits** — seeded node placement; per-body resource identity (this moon = X-rich) to drive logistics/exploration.
- **Voxel/SDF deformation patches** — overlay only where players dig; keep heightfield for the untouched bulk.
- **POI / structure placement** — ruins, anomalies, loot sites (content authored by [gameplay](gameplay.md); placement here).
- **Body parameters** — radius, gravity (μ), atmosphere profile, rotation — shared with physics/rendering.
- **Non-goals:** rendering the terrain (→ rendering consumes our data); what loot/quests POIs contain (→ gameplay).

## 3. Key design decisions
| # | Decision | Rationale | Status | Date |
|---|----------|-----------|--------|------|
| WG-230 | **THE PER-CHUNK FLOAT64 PHASE SHIPS AS `frac` ALONE, ONE vec3, NOT WG-50's `(cell, frac)` PAIR — AND THE CPU HALF HAD BEEN WRITTEN AND UNCONSUMED FOR THREE WEEKS.** `ChunkPhase.ts` has existed since 2026-07-28 with the reduction, the shader contract and two precision tools; nothing in `web/src` ever imported it, which is why `TerrainArt.glsl.ts`'s header still names the phase as owed. `aPhase` is a **vec3 float32 per-chunk constant replicated across the slot's vertices** (`aFadeT0`/`aCanopy`'s precedent, forced by DW-11: a `BatchedMesh` draws every resident chunk in one multi-draw, so a per-chunk uniform does not exist inside one), and the vertex shader forms `vPhase = aPhase + position / PHASE_PERIOD_M`. `frac` alone because the consumer is the far-field SPLAT rung and **a tiling texture fetch never sees an integer lattice cell**; three floats instead of six is 6.4 MiB of pooled vertex memory instead of 12.8. `cell` stays exported for a lattice consumer. **THE WIRE FORMAT IS UNTOUCHED** — no byte of the 28 B/vertex layout, no `of_packed_*`, no ABI, no parity re-baseline — because the phase is a pure float64 function of `ChunkView.anchor`, which `ChunkBatch` has already retained beside every slot since RN-2265. **DELIBERATELY ABSENT FROM `stitched()`**, the one asymmetry against `aCanopy`: the phase is a function of the chunk and the per-vertex half is `position`, which the stitch has already rewritten. | The brief named a chunk-format change; it is not one, for the reason WG-50 gave and for one more that was not available then — `ChunkBatch` now retains the anchor, so the whole plumbing cost outside the shader is one private method. Full record in section 6.10. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-21 |
| WG-231 | **`PHASE_PERIOD_M` = 256 IS A PRECISION BUDGET, AND THE SEAM RULE IS THE ONLY RULE.** The quantum of the reconstructed coordinate is `ulp(t) * P`, i.e. the period over ~2^23, so **doubling the period doubles the quantum everywhere**. 256 m holds it at 30.5 um for every chunk whose half-extent is under the period, which is every chunk inside ~600 m; the cost is that 256 m is the coarsest world-locked field a consumer can build here, and a kilometre-scale one needs a SECOND reduction rather than a larger value. **Two chunks over the same ground hold `vPhase` values differing by an exact INTEGER**, so `fract(vPhase * n)` agrees across the boundary for integer `n` and for nothing else; `assertPhasePeriod()` throws at module load. | A non-dividing tile is the single input that makes this attribute draw the seam it exists to avoid, and it would draw it only at range, where boundaries are far apart and the cause is least obvious. So it is a throw and not a comment. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-21 |
| WG-232 | **THE PRECISION ARITHMETIC, AND THE RATIO COLUMN IS THE WRONG ONE TO READ.** Against `pM`'s 31.25 mm (WG-50's measured typical case): 30.5 um at 2 m and at 75 m (**1024x**), 61.0 um at 600 m (512x), 1.95 mm at the 38 km horizon (16x); measured worst float32 reconstruction 17.9 um / 52.2 um / 1.902 mm, tracking the prediction. **What decides whether a derivative survives is quanta per pixel, and `pM` carries 0.114 at two metres and 4.27 at 75 m** — the acute band is the near end of phase 2's own charter, and by 600 m `pM` is at 34 a pixel where precision alone would not justify the attribute. **THE BLOCKING DEFECT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT NUMERIC:** the splat rides `vChunkUv`, which normalises over the quad, so a far rung on it puts a world-scale STEP at every LOD ring inside the band phase 2 must make seamless. The invariant: the phase pins the shading coordinate's quantum to the CHUNK's extent instead of the BODY's radius, which is the precision the vertex positions already carry. | Selling this as a precision fix alone would misdescribe it and would invite "600 m is fine, skip it". Both halves are written down so the next lane can argue with either. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-21 |
| WG-233 | **THE FREE ALTERNATIVE — a per-frame `frac(origin / P)` UNIFORM, no attribute, no varying, no per-vertex bytes — IS REFUSED ON ONE NUMBER.** `pM = vWorld - uBodyCenter` and `uBodyCenter` is the planet-scale term, so reducing it in float64 on the CPU reconstructs the same coordinate from `vWorld / P` alone. But `FloatingOrigin.thresholdM` is **4,000 m**, so `abs(vWorld)` for the ground under the player's feet ranges over `[0, 4000)` between rebases and the near-field quantum would breathe by up to 2^11 across one rebase cycle: a shading resolution that is a function of how far the player has walked. The per-chunk anchor's is a constant of the chunk. | Recorded rather than discarded, because it is the cheap answer and someone will propose it again. It also remains the right shape for any term that does not need near-field resolution. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-21 |
| WG-234 | **A COORDINATE-ONLY LANE SHIPS A PROBE, AND ITS CHECKER SIZE IS A PARAMETER.** `uPhaseProbe.x` ships at 0 so the frame is bit-identical, but a declaration nothing reads is stripped by the GLSL linker, the attribute goes unbound, and the path is dead while every counter reads correct (RN-2268's scar). `?phaseamp=` / `?phaserep=` photograph it. **The repeats are a parameter because the first version fixed them at a 2 m checker and the aerial frame returned moire** — an instrument returning the same picture for "working" and "broken" at the range the term is FOR. At `?phaserep=4` the 64 m check holds one world size from 1.2 km altitude to the 38 km horizon across every LOD ring with no line at any chunk boundary, which is the property `vChunkUv` cannot have. | Both params registered in `run.mjs`'s `PAGE_PARAMS` in the same commit (RN-152's scar). Frames `docs/screenshots/WG230_*`. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-21 |
| WG-1 | Cubed-sphere quadtree heightfield for bulk terrain | Proven KSP-scale LOD; cheap; seed-deterministic | **Accepted** (designed in Spike 1: 6-face cube + per-face quadtree, `FQuadKey`) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-2 | Voxel patches **only** at dig sites; heightfield elsewhere | Keeps 99.9% cheap; v1 may ship node-mining, no deform (D-005) | Accepted (seam pinned in Spike 1 §4.4: `bFromVoxelPatch` in every result; stub no-op) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-3 | Deposits = seeded nodes; only depletion state is saved | Seed+diff persistence (D-005, persistence) | Accepted | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-4 | Per-body resource identity to force interplanetary logistics | Exploration/automation incentive (DSP-style) | **Accepted** (pinned as the Cinderite off-world hook — Spike 1 §4.5.3; Cinderite `ItemId` placed only on Cinder, P1-D4) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-5 | Cube→sphere uses **tangent (equal-angle) warp**, not raw normalize | Uniform LOD triangle density; warp cost is gen-time only; inverse closed-form (lat/lon↔quad cheap) | **Accepted** (Spike 1 §1.1) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-6 | Height is **position-hashed** `(bodySeed, dir)`, never sequence-hashed | Shared edge verts bit-identical between neighbors/parent-child → crack-free seams; order-independent regen | **Accepted** (Spike 1 §1.3–1.4) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-7 | Noise = **3 layers/body** (base + signature + detail), octave-gated by LOD; planet=ridged mountains, moon=crater field | Recognizable planet + cratered moon at minimal cost; gated octaves keep low-LOD cheap (R2) | **Accepted** (Spike 1 §2) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-8 | Seam strategy = shared-edge determinism + **skirts** + balanced quadtree (≤1 level edge-neighbor diff); edge-stitch index buffers deferred | Standard chunked-LOD trio; skirts need no per-frame neighbor bookkeeping | **Accepted** (Spike 1 §1.4) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-9 | Chunk centers stored as `FUniverseCoord` (+`FFrameId`); verts body-center-relative; rebase = re-project transform only | Honors core-engine §1.4/§8 floating-origin contract; rebase is O(resident quads), no vertex rewrite | **Accepted** (Spike 1 §3.3–3.4) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-10 | **Append-only** additive fields on pinned contracts (RC-2, RC-7): `FAtmosphereProfile` += Mie (`MieScaleHeightM`/`MieScatteringScale`/`MieAnisotropy`); `FTerrainContact` + `FTerrainHit` += `SlopeRad` (rad) + `SurfaceHardness` (normalized [0,1] `double`) | Both derivable from data the gen/query already produces (normal→slope, material/biome→hardness; Mie is a per-body atmo constant world-gen owns per D-006). End-of-struct appends → no caller breaks. Hardness normalized [0,1] (not physics's first `uint8 0..255` sketch) so it lerps directly into friction/restitution | **Accepted** (reconciliation; Spike 1 §4.3, §5, §5.1–5.2) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-11 | **C-1 — `FDepositNode` pinned** (Spike 1 §4.5): id / `Position`(`FUniverseCoord`+`FFrameId`) / lat-lon / `SurfaceNormal` / `FBodyId` + **opaque gameplay-owned `ItemId` resource** + Grade / InitialAmount / single-mutable `RemainingAmount`. Catalog **deterministically seed-generated** per body (position-hashed, WG-6 discipline). `ItemId` belongs to gameplay's C-3 registry — referenced, not defined; **collapses** gameplay's proposed `DepositTypeId` into the one shared id space. Cinderite `ItemId` placed **only on Cinder** (WG-4 hook). | One struct serves all three consumers (gameplay UX, factory-sim extraction, persistence depletion). `ItemId` as the type keeps one id space (deposit==belt==smelter==pack) — no translation table. Seed-generated catalog → regenerates bit-identically (WV1), only depletion diffs. | **Accepted** (Phase-1 C-1; Spike 1 §4.5, §5.1–5.2) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-12 | **C-6 (world-gen side) confirmed** (Spike 1 §4.6): world-gen implements persistence's `IPersistable` (chunk-scoped, `DomainId=WorldGen`) over **deposit depletion only** — `(depositId, remaining)[]` per region, `depositId = hash64(bodySeed, region, localIndex)`. Terrain + deposit *placement* regenerate from seed (PS-7/WV1) — not serialized; voxel seam empty (WG-2). | World-gen's entire Phase-1 mutable surface is depletion. Stable hashed id = exactly the `(bodySeed, FQuadKey, localIndex)` id persistence §2.1 asked for → depletion re-binds on reload with no fuzzy match. No new ask of persistence beyond implementing the interface. | **Accepted** (Phase-1 C-6; Spike 1 §4.6) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-14 | **Phase-1 deposit gen + mining query surface BUILT + GREEN** (C-1 / WG-11 realized) — `core/include/of/deposits.h` + `core/tests/test_deposits.cpp` (`deposits_tests` target). Implements the pinned `FDepositNode` (Spike 1 §4.5.1; `Resource` = bare `uint16_t` `ItemId` per WG-11, **not** including gameplay.h — Ferrite `0x0001`, Cinderite `0x0004`), `GenerateDeposits(body, bodySeed, bodyFrame)` (a **deterministic** position-hashed pass over a Fibonacci-sphere candidate lattice; each kept node snapped to the terrain via the SAME `sampleHeightField` the mesh uses; stable `Id = hashCombine(mix64(bodySeed), face, candidateIndex)`), and `DepositCatalog` with the full query surface (`GetDeposits`/`QueryDepositsNear`/`GetDeposit`/`ExtractFromDeposit`). `PickResource` enforces **WG-4**: Forge nodes are Ferrite-only and **never** Cinderite; Cinderite is returned **only** for Cinder (off-world hook). `ExtractFromDeposit` is the **only** mutator — grants ≤ `RemainingAmount`, clamps at 0 (no negative, no over-grant). | Realizes the three-way contract (gameplay mining UX, factory-sim extraction, persistence depletion) in real headless C++, mirroring the green Wave-0 pattern (header-only `of::worldgen::` + `test_framework.h`, parallel `deposits_tests` target only — `cubed_sphere.h` consumed READ-ONLY, untouched). Determinism proven **bitwise** (same seed → identical catalog field-for-field; diff seed → different) and the **seed+diff property** proven (regenerate from seed + re-apply recorded `(depositId, extracted)[]` → live state bit-identical — `RemainingAmount` is the only thing that ever changes, WG-3/C-6). On-surface proven (`|Position|` ≈ `radius + SampleTerrainHeight(lat,lon)` within 1 m, matches WV7). **Result: 7 tests / 1620 checks pass; all 11 ctest suites green.** Per body (world seed 20260615; counts are seed-dependent): **Forge = 732 deposits, all Ferrite, 0 Cinderite**; **Cinder = 210 deposits (133 Cinderite + 77 Ferrite)**. Cinderite-only-on-Cinder confirmed; determinism confirmed (same seed → bit-identical catalog). | **Accepted** (Wave-2 build; Spike 1 §4.5, WG-11/WG-4/WG-3) | 2026-06-15 |
| WG-15 | **GAP-1 (slice-e2e) CLOSED — `FDepositNode::Id` is now the ONE shared deposit-id type.** Gameplay's deposit reference (`gameplay::DepositId`) was a parallel narrower `uint32`; it is now `using DepositId = worldgen::DepositId` (the `uint64` `FDepositNode::Id` type), so a deposit key carries from `DepositCatalog` → gameplay mining → the persistence depletion diff **with no truncation**. Gameplay now consumes `FDepositNode` directly (new `gameplay::mineDeposit(worldgen::FDepositNode&, …)` overload reads/decrements `RemainingAmount` in place); the slice's truncating `bridgeNode` is removed. **World-gen header itself is UNCHANGED** — it still defines `worldgen::DepositId`/`FDepositNode` exactly as pinned (WG-11) and never includes gameplay.h (the dependency is one-way: gameplay→world-gen). | The catalog's stable id is a `hash64(bodySeed, region, localIndex)` `uint64` (WG-11/C-6); narrowing it to `uint32` at the gameplay/persistence boundary risked save-key collisions and forced a bridge in the loop. Making gameplay alias world-gen's type (rather than the reverse) keeps world-gen's WG-11 boundary intact while giving the whole slice one faithful id. **13/13 ctest suites green.** | **Accepted** (slice-e2e gap closure; WG-11/C-6) | 2026-06-15 |
| WG-16 | **Wave-1 terrain STREAMING + LOD manager BUILT + GREEN, and the procgen hot path OPTIMIZED bit-identically** — `core/include/of/terrain_stream.h` + `core/tests/test_terrain_stream.cpp` (`terrain_stream_tests` target, 8 tests / 52,465 checks) + `core/tools/procgen_bench.cpp`. **(a) The headless terrain streamer the UE renderer binds to** (Spike 1 §3, §4.1–§4.2): `TerrainChunk` (== the pinned `FTerrainChunk` — `key`/`centerUniverse`(`UniverseCoord` anchor)/`chunkRadiusM`/`gridDim`/body-center-relative `positions`+`normals`/`skirtPositions`/`materialId`/`contentHash`); `selectResidentSet(body, observer, cfg)` = the DETERMINISTIC distance-based LOD quadtree (subdivide when `quadEdgeLengthM/dist > splitRatio`, capped at `maxDepth`), then **balanced** (`<=1`-level edge step, cascade-split coarser neighbours) so skirts cover at most a 1-level seam; `TerrainStreamer::updateStreaming(observer)` → `StreamUpdate{ready[], evicted[], generated, converged}` maintaining the resident set across motion under a **per-update `genBudget`** (build ≤N quads/call; converges over several calls to the bit-identical unbudgeted set); `makeObserverLatLonAlt`, `onOriginRebased()` (O(resident) re-anchor, positions rebase-invariant per §3.4). LOD runs on the 64-bit authority position (§3.1), not the floating-origin engine position. **Crack-free = shared-edge HEIGHTS bit-identical across a LOD step (WG-6 position-hash, proven bitwise) + a radial SKIRT** (`skirtDepthM = skirtFraction·quadEdgeLengthM`, dropped inward, proven strictly-inward by exactly `skirtDepthM`). **(b) Procgen optimization:** `valueNoise` rewritten to hoist the shared hash-chain prefixes — the 8 cube corners share `base = mix64(seed^channel·C)` (was 8× → 1×) and per-axis partial combines (x-stage 8→2, xy-stage 8→4); identical operands in identical order ⇒ **bit-identical** but ~half the `mix64`/`hashCombine` calls. `procgen_bench` times the optimized sampler vs a self-contained verbatim copy of the original in ONE binary: **Forge 909,893 → 2,385,621 verts/sec (2.62×), Cinder 1,060,332 → 1,554,351 verts/sec (1.47×)** (moon lower because its cost is dominated by the untouched `craterField`), checksum match. A ctest (`procgen_optimized_is_bit_identical`) re-asserts every vertex height bit-for-bit vs the original chain. `cubed_sphere.h` `valueNoise` changed in place (bit-output preserved → `world_gen_tests`/`deposits_tests` still green); everything else additive. | Realizes the Wave-1 streaming/LOD half of the §6 roadmap (the `UTerrainStreamer` row) headlessly so the UE renderer (rendering M2.1 currently renders ONE static `generateQuadMesh`) can bind a real LOD/streaming/skirt provider next. The streamer is a pure function of (body, observer, cfg) → testable determinism without an engine. The procgen hoist matters because per-vertex noise is the streaming budget's bottleneck (R2): 2.6× more quads/sec for the SAME bits = more terrain per tick or a smaller budget for the same coverage. Mirrors the green Wave-0/Wave-2 pattern (header-only `of::worldgen::` + `test_framework.h`, parallel `terrain_stream_tests` target). **Result: 8 tests / 52,465 checks pass; all 17 ctest suites green.** | **Accepted** (Wave-1 build; spike §3, §4.1–§4.2; R2) | 2026-06-15 |
| WG-17 | **Wave-3 BIOME + terrain-design + planet-wide RESOURCE layer BUILT + GREEN** — `core/include/of/biome.h` + `core/tests/test_biome.cpp` (`biome_tests` target, 11 tests / 8361 checks). The layer that turns uniform noise into a *designed*, varied planet. **(a) Biome classifier** `Biome biomeAt(const BodyParams&, const Vec3& dir)` (+ `biomeAtLatLon`) → enum `{Ocean, Beach, Plains, Forest, Hills, Mountains, Polar, Regolith, MoonHighland, CraterFloor}`; planet biomes are climate-driven (latitude→temperature with poles cold, position-hashed temperature/moisture fBm WG-6 style, elevation from the canonical `sampleHeightField`), moon biomes are airless elevation bands (crater-floor / regolith / highland). Pure & deterministic. **(b) Terrain design** `double sampleDesignedHeight(const BodyParams&, const Vec3& dir)` (+ `SampleDesignedTerrainHeight(lat,lon)`): per-biome relief modulation layered **ADDITIVELY on top of the unchanged canonical `sampleHeightField`** — Mountains ×1.6 (ridge high), Hills ×1.25, Plains ×0.45 (flatten), Beach ×0.15, Forest ×1.05, Polar flatten + ice-cap dome, **Ocean carved into a real basin below the datum** (low-freq fBm depth). Because biome + modulation are pure functions of the (bit-identical) shared dir, designed height is **bit-identical across a LOD/neighbour seam too** (proven in `designed_height_preserves_shared_edge_bit_identity`). **(c) Material mapping** `uint16_t materialForBiome(Biome)` (pinned ids 0..9 + `kMatUnknown`) + `double hardnessForBiome(Biome)` ([0,1], feeds physics `SurfaceHardness` WG-10). **(d) Planet-WIDE resource distribution** `GenerateBiomeDeposits(body,seed,frame)` — a planet-scale (8192 planet / 4096 moon) Fibonacci-sphere pass whose resource + spawn-density are biome-biased (iron/copper/coal/stone concentrate in Hills/Mountains; Forest=wood/oil; Plains/Beach/Polar sparse; Ocean none), reusing the survival raw-ore `ItemId` block, surface-snapped to the **designed** height; stable hashed `Id`. **Cinderite stays moon-ONLY (WG-4)**. Queryable by region via `BiomeResourceField::QueryRegionDeposits(body, FQuadKey, marginRad)` (angular cube-quad footprint) so the streamer/renderer populates the planet on the fly (+`GetDeposit`/`ExtractFromDeposit` depletion mirror). **(e) Wiring:** `terrain_stream.h` `TerrainChunk.materialId` **widened `uint8_t`→`uint16_t`** and now set from `materialForBiome(biomeAt(quadCenterDir))` (+ a `Biome biome` convenience field) so each chunk textures by biome — additive (no test pins `materialId`; all prior suites unaffected). | Realizes the §2 "biomes & surface materials" + the planet-scale generalization of §4.5 resources. The additive `sampleDesignedHeight` keeps the headline crack-free determinism intact: `cubed_sphere.h` is **UNTOUCHED** (consumed read-only), so the pinned WV1–WV3 bit-identity and the deposit catalog see the identical canonical heights — **NO canonical height bits were re-baselined, NO pinned test changed**. The renderer binds `biomeAt`/`materialForBiome`/`sampleDesignedHeight`/`BiomeResourceField::QueryRegionDeposits` exactly as terrain_stream now does. **Result: 11 tests / 8361 checks pass; all 18 ctest suites green** (was 17). | **Accepted** (Wave-3 build; spike §2, §4.5; WG-4/WG-6) | 2026-06-15 |
| WG-18 | **TERRAIN-DEFORMATION layer (Valheim-style digging) BUILT + GREEN** — `core/include/of/terrain_deform.h` + `core/tests/test_terrain_deform.cpp` (`terrain_deform_tests` target, 7 tests / 251 checks). The deterministic, persistable terrain-EDIT layer the UE pickaxe + automated-miner + collision pass binds to — the first concrete fill of the long-deferred R1/WG-2 dig seam, done as a **heightfield-lowering DIFF** (not full voxels): cheap, persistable, and additive. **(a) Edit store** = a sparse `std::map<uint64_t,double>` from a **quantized cubed-sphere deform cell** (`DeformCell{faceId,ix,iy}` at a fixed deep lattice `kDeformLevel=18` ≈ 3.6 m/cell on Forge; packed id `(face<<58)|(ix<<29)|iy` via `deformCellId`; `cellForDir` snaps a dir to its canonical face + nearest lattice point reusing `faceOfDir`/`unwarp`/`latticeDir`) → accumulated **lowering in metres**. **(b) Deformed sampler** `double deformedHeight(body, dir, deform)` = `sampleDesignedHeight − edit(cell)`, **clamped to bedrock** `base − maxDigDepthM` (default `kDefaultMaxDigDepthM = 80 m`, DEEP, configurable); where a cell is unedited it returns the base **BIT-IDENTICALLY** (subtraction is exactly 0.0 — proven via uint64 bit `==`, NO regression to existing terrain). **(c) Dig brush** `digBrush(body, centerDir, radiusM, amountM)` lowers cells within the radius with a **soft `(1−t²)` falloff** (centre deeper than rim → natural sloped holes), clamped to bedrock, returns the total lowering applied (0 at bedrock). **(d) Drill column** `drillStep(body, centerDir, columnRadiusM, depthPerStepM)` sinks a flat-profile narrow **shaft** toward bedrock over many calls (`shaftDepthAt`/`shaftBottomedOut` queries). **(e) UE queries** `editAt(cell)`, `depthDugAt(dir)`, `atBedrock(dir[,maxDepth])`, and `editedCellsInQuad(FQuadKey,marginRad)` / `quadHasEdits(...)` (angular cube-quad footprint over the sparse edit set — tells the renderer which resident chunks to **re-mesh** after a dig). **(f) Persistence** `serialize(Writer)`/`deserialize(Reader)` templated over the persistence.h `SaveWriter`/`SaveReader` cursor style (NO dependency on persistence.h — stays leaf): `[f64 maxDigDepth][varint count]{[varint cellId][f64 lowering]}` — the player's terraforming as a compact diff. **Determinism:** edits are EXPLICIT ops, so the same op sequence yields the bit-identical edit map + heights (proven). | Realizes Valheim-style digging headlessly as a **lowering diff over the procedural base** — exactly the seed+diff discipline persistence asked for (the edit map IS the diff, like deposit depletion WG-3/C-6). A heightfield-lowering diff (vs full SDF voxels) keeps the untouched bulk free and the dug regions a tiny sparse map, while still reading as real holes/shafts in mesh + collision once `terrain_stream`/UE sample `deformedHeight`. **`cubed_sphere.h` + `biome.h` consumed READ-ONLY (UNTOUCHED) — NO canonical/designed height bits re-baselined, NO pinned test changed; the undeformed path is bit-identical.** Mirrors the green Wave-0..3 pattern (header-only `of::worldgen::` + `test_framework.h`, parallel `terrain_deform_tests` target only). The UE dig/mine + collision layer binds `deformedHeight`/`digBrush`/`drillStep`/`editedCellsInQuad`/`serialize`. **Result: 7 tests / 251 checks pass; all 20 ctest suites green** (was 19). NOTE: this advances the WG-2 voxel seam from a no-op stub toward a working deform layer; the `bFromVoxelPatch` collision-query branch should now route through `deformedHeight` (follow-up wiring in physics/terrain_stream). | **Accepted** (terrain-deform build; R1/WG-2 seam, WG-3/WG-6 discipline) | 2026-06-16 |
| WG-20 | **TRUE VOXEL TUNNELING layer (1 m³ destruction) BUILT + GREEN** — `core/include/of/voxel_terrain.h` + `core/tests/test_voxel_terrain.cpp` (`voxel_terrain_tests` target, 9 tests / 5761 checks). The first real fill of the WG-2/R1 **voxel** seam (vs WG-18's heightfield-lowering diff): a sparse **1 m³ voxel destruction layer** that lets the player dig DOWN *and sideways* — tunnels, overhangs, caverns the heightfield can't express. **(a) Cell lattice** = a plain Cartesian **body-frame** lattice at `kVoxelSizeM = 1.0` m: `VoxelCell{cx,cy,cz} = floor(bodyPos/1)` via `cellForPos`, centre via `cellCenter`, packed to a stable `uint64` id (`voxelCellId`/`voxelCellFromId`, 21 bits/axis bias-encoded — covers Forge 600 km / Cinder 200 km). Cartesian (NOT the angular deform lattice) is what lets a removed cell sit ANYWHERE in the solid volume, incl. horizontally below the surface. **(b) Solidity = procedural-solid XOR removed (seed+diff):** `isProcSolid(body,cell)` = cell-centre radius ≤ `surfaceRadiusAt` (= `radiusM + sampleHeightField` at the cell-centre's **unit** dir — the input is normalized so 1 m-adjacent cells don't alias height); `VoxelEdits::isSolid(body,cell)` = `isProcSolid AND NOT removed`. Cells above the surface are AIR. Only the sparse REMOVED set is stored — never the full volume. **(c) Dig brush (tunnel maker)** `int VoxelEdits::dig(body, centerWorldPos, radiusM)` removes every currently-solid cell whose centre is within radius, returns the count (harvest yield); `digCell`/`digCellId` for the atomic carve. Idempotent (re-dig removes 0). **(d) Mesh extraction** `std::vector<FaceQuad> exposedFaces(body, edits, regionAABB | center+radius)` emits a `FaceQuad{cell,axis,sign}` per SOLID cell face whose 6-neighbour is AIR (the visible voxel surface) — UE builds the unit quad + `faceNormal(axis,sign)` per quad; `forSolidCellsInRegion(...)` iterates the same solid set so UE collision is built from it (region scanned ONCE). **(e) Dirty region** `dirtyRegion()`→ inclusive touched-cell AABB after a dig (UE re-meshes only there), `clearDirty()`. **(f) Persistence** `serialize(Writer)/deserialize(Reader)` of the removed set, sorted-id varint stream, templated over persistence.h's cursor (NO dependency — stays leaf). **Determinism:** procedural-solid is a pure fn of the height field, edits are explicit → same ops = same state bit-for-bit (proven). **TUNNEL proof:** a 12 m horizontal bore dug 5 m below the surface is AIR while the rock 4 m ABOVE it stays SOLID — a genuine overhang. | Realizes the long-deferred WG-2 **voxel** seam (Phase-4 in the roadmap) as a real 1 m³ destruction layer, complementing — not replacing — WG-18's heightfield deform: heightfield stays for the cheap untouched far-field + dig-DOWN, voxels carve the sideways/overhang topology only voxels can. Same seed+diff discipline (the removed set IS the diff, like deposit depletion WG-3/C-6 + deform WG-18). **`cubed_sphere.h` consumed READ-ONLY (UNTOUCHED) — NO height bits re-baselined, NO pinned test changed.** Mirrors the green Wave-0..3 pattern (header-only `of::worldgen::` + `test_framework.h`, parallel `voxel_terrain_tests` target only). The UE cube-mesher/dig/voxel-collision layer binds `cellForPos`/`isSolid`/`dig`/`exposedFaces`/`FaceQuad`/`dirtyRegion`/`serialize`. **Result: 9 tests / 5761 checks pass; all 21 ctest suites green** (was 20). | **Accepted** (voxel-tunneling build; R1/WG-2 voxel seam, WG-3/WG-6 discipline) | 2026-06-16 |
| WG-21 | **SINGLE SURFACE AUTHORITY ("surface oracle") BUILT + GREEN, the RETHINK R1 consolidation.** `core/include/of/surface_field.h` + `core/tests/test_surface_field.cpp` (`surface_field_tests` target, 7 tests / ~1900 checks). Replaces the FIVE competing surface definitions (RAW `sampleHeightField` · DESIGNED `sampleDesignedHeight` · DEFORMED · the MESH which subtracted edits from RAW · VOXEL-SOLID from RAW) that were the deterministic tax behind every floating/air-gap experience bug (RETHINK §3 RC-A; core-docs-audit Task B). **ONE truth, three layers:** (a) **`baseHeight(body,dir)` ≡ `sampleDesignedHeight`**: the DESIGNED relief is the canonical surface everyone samples; RAW survives ONLY as an internal ingredient of the designed shaping, never a surface anyone stands on. (b) **Voxel solidity derived from the SAME base:** `voxel_terrain.h`'s `isProcSolid`/`surfaceRadiusAt` switched **RAW to DESIGNED** (now sample `sampleDesignedHeight`), so the voxel solid shell agrees with the mesh/collision/walker, retiring the root cause of the UE 18 m "surface-snap" hack (the raw-vs-designed gap). (c) **Derived far-field lowering** `derivedLoweringAt(body,dir,voxelEdits[,maxDig])` = the depth of the **top-anchored contiguous removed-voxel run** in that column (the UE `OpenColumn` logic **promoted into core**, testable + single-sourced): a dig-down pit lowers the heightfield; a sideways TUNNEL under solid ground has a solid top cell, so run 0, so **NO lowering (ceiling intact)**. **`surfaceHeight(body,dir,voxelEdits) = clamp(baseHeight − derivedLowering, baseHeight − maxDigDepth)`**: the ONE surface + the ONE bedrock clamp (`kSurfaceMaxDigDepthM = 80 m`), defined here and nowhere else. A `SurfaceField{const BodyParams&, const VoxelEdits*}` binder exposes `heightAt`/`baseHeightAt`/`loweringAt`/`radiusAt`/`solid`/`loweringFn()`. **Consumers wired IN CORE:** `terrain_stream.h buildChunk` now samples the DESIGNED surface as the mesh base (via `generateQuadMesh`'s new optional `HeightFieldFn`, default null = RAW, bit-identical, so the header stays leaf), so the streamed mesh + cooked collision finally match the walker; `surface_walk.h` `surfaceHeightM()` reads `surfaceHeight` (new borrowed `setVoxelEdits(const VoxelEdits*)`, so the player drops into their own digs; null = undug designed base, bit-identical); `deposits.h GenerateDeposits`/`LayoutTestArea`/`DepositCatalog::ForBody` gained an optional `SnapHeightFn` (default RAW; the oracle-aligned UE/biome path passes `sampleDesignedHeight`) and `biome.h GenerateBiomeDeposits` already snapped to designed. **DEFORM DEMOTED:** `terrain_deform.h` is kept COMPILING (deprecated header note) but is **no longer an edit authority**: the oracle reads voxel-derived lowering, NOT the independent deform store. The two false-consistency comments the audit flagged (`surface_walk.h:29`, `terrain_deform.h:18`) are corrected. **RE-BASELINE (the RAW to DESIGNED switch):** `test_terrain_stream`'s `deform_lowering_…` case re-based its undug reference from `generateQuadMesh` (RAW) onto the designed base and its lowered-resident check from `sampleHeightField` onto `sampleDesignedHeight`. **NO determinism/seam assertion deleted:** both seam bit-identity proofs (`stream_crackfree_across_lod_boundary`, `stream_same_depth_edge_bit_identical`) still pass **bit-for-bit against the designed base**, proven, because designed height is a pure function of the (bit-identical shared) dir, so the position-hash seam guarantee is base-agnostic. `procgen_bench` still bit-identical (it benches the untouched RAW sampler). **R2 migration note:** the UE pickaxe/miner today write BOTH the voxel set and the deform store; after R2 they write voxels ONLY, and the far-field deform view is regenerated from the voxel removed-set on load (`derivedLoweringAt`), not persisted. | The worst experience bugs (floating player M4.0/M4.3, floating nodes M4.4, dig-in-air + the 18 m surface-snap M5.2) were the deterministic tax of four/five surface definitions each patched at the symptom (trace-snap nodes, drop-the-player, probe-inward digging, per-column reconciliation) instead of consolidated at the source. One oracle in `/core` + voxels-as-sole-edit-truth removes the whole hack family, at the one-time cost of the RAW to DESIGNED re-baseline. **Visible intended consequence: the planet's relief becomes the DESIGNED terrain everywhere, mountains actually mountainous, because the mesh/collision/voxel shell now read the designed surface, not raw.** Additive + leaf-preserving: every existing public signature stays alive (delegating/deprecated) so the UE layer keeps compiling for R2. **Result: 7 new tests pass; all 22 ctest suites green** (was 21) from plain PowerShell (post-R3 static link). | **Accepted** (RETHINK R1; RC-A consolidation) | 2026-07-05 |
| WG-22 | **TERRAFORMING BUILT + GREEN — fill is representable, and `levelArea` flattens a disc.** The voxel layer was **SUBTRACTIVE BY CONSTRUCTION**: `VoxelEdits` held ONE set (`removed_`), so "the player put a cell of ground here" was not a sentence the data model could say, and levelling a slope (cut the high side, fill the low side) could not be expressed at all. **(a) `voxel_terrain.h`:** a second sparse set `added_`; solidity = `added OR (procSolid AND NOT removed)`; the two sets are **DISJOINT by construction** (filling a dug cell erases it from `removed_`, digging a placed cell erases it from `added_`), so undoing an edit SHRINKS the save; `fillCell` / `fill(body, centre, radius)` mirror the dig brush exactly, same dirty-AABB and idempotence; persistence is **self-describing** (magic varint `0x4F464532` + version, then both sets) and `deserialize` branches on the first varint so **every slot written before today still loads**, including VoxelSave's single-zero-byte reset. All `added_` tests are behind an `empty()` guard, so a world nobody has filled takes the branch it took before. **(b) `surface_field.h`:** `derivedRaisingAt`, the mirror of `derivedLoweringAt` (contiguous run of ADDED cells anchored AT the base; a filled cell floating over a gap raises nothing, exactly as a sideways tunnel lowers nothing); `surfaceHeight = clamp(base − lowering + raising, base − 80 m, base + 24 m)` with `kSurfaceMaxFillM = 24`, **bit-identical where nothing is filled**; `surfaceOffsetAt`, the SIGNED offset the chunk mesher subtracts, so ONE callback carries both halves; **`levelArea(body, edits, centre, radius, targetHeight)`** — inside a cylinder about the local up, every cell above the target becomes air and every cell below it becomes solid, so cut and fill fall out of ONE predicate rather than being two tools. Deterministic and **idempotent**, so a held key cannot make the pad creep. **(c) `cubed_sphere.h`:** the mesh's offset guard `dug > 0.0` became `dug != 0.0`; it would have silently discarded every fill and left terraformed ground RENDERING at the height it used to be while the walker stood on the new one, the five-surfaces failure with the sign flipped. Undug path still bit-identical. **(d) Bridge, ABI 3 to 4:** `of_edits_fill`/`_fill_cell`/`_added_count`/`_is_added_cell`, `of_level_area`, `of_streamer_level`, `of_streamer_load_edits`, `of_derived_raising`, `of_surface_offset`, `of_max_fill`. Also fixed a LIVE mismatch: the shim already returned 3 while `OfCore.ts` expected 2, so `loadOfCore` threw on any freshly synced wasm. **(e) Client:** hold **Q**; the floor is **LATCHED on the press** from the player's own feet (reading it live would chase the player — levelling raises the ground they stand on, which raises the target, and the pad would climb for as long as the key was held); `world/LevelRing.ts` draws the footprint through the SAME oracle, so it lies on ground already terraformed; `TerrainEditChannel` splits ops (live play) from state (restore). | Realizes the user-facing half of the WG-2 voxel seam: a player can flatten a spot and build on it. Done as a core data-model change rather than a client trick because the model is what was wrong, and a reserved-for-later note in a comment is not a seam. **Measured:** `/core` 7.703 m spread to 1.511 m on a 26 deg hillside; browser 2.893 m to 1.279 m (2.26x), 60 cut + 99 placed over 2,815 scanned in 0.4 ms, outside the radius 0.000000 m over 12 points, 17/17 surface-vs-solid agreement, 3.68 m walked with eye-altitude spread 0.000 m, 1,439 save bytes restoring to 0.000000 m. **A pad is flat to about ONE VOXEL and never to zero** — a Cartesian 1 m lattice cut by a non-axis-aligned plane terminates on a staircase. That is the medium, not the algorithm (ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 99). **NO re-baselining: 22/22 ctest, parity self-determinism 119/119, cross-toolchain exact 94 to 106/106, and the ONLY change to `expected.json` is the 7-byte save header.** | **Accepted** (terraforming; WG-2/WG-21 line) | 2026-07-26 |
| WG-23 | **THE LEVELLING TOOL COULD NOT BE REACHED, AND IT LEFT HOLES IN ITS OWN PAD.** Reid: "i tried the click q to level the ground thing and it didnt really work at all", against WG-22, which measured a 2.3x spread collapse and passed nine assertions. **Three causes; the two that mattered were not the voxel resolution.** **(a) UNREACHABLE.** `LevelAction.groundHit` centred the disc on the first ground the aim ray hit within 9 m of the eye. On the 29 degree slope the tool exists for, looking downhill, the ground falls away faster than the ray descends: MEASURED, at pitch 0, -10, -20 and -30 the ray found NOTHING, the footprint ring hid itself, and the key did nothing at all; ground was first found at **-45 degrees**. The WG-22 probe had aimed at -72 throughout and its own comment recorded that a shallower aim "cost a run to learn": the harness met the bug, worked around it, and wrote the workaround down as a lesson about framing. **Fixed:** `discCentre` falls back to the player's own feet, which is the rule the tool already stated (the target HEIGHT always came from the feet; only the centre came from the ray), and the ring follows the same centre so it cannot vanish at the moment it is needed. **(b) FULL-DEPTH HOLES.** `levelArea` stored only cells whose solidity CHANGED, while `derivedRaisingAt` / `derivedLoweringAt` rebuild the surface from a CONTIGUOUS run of explicitly edited cells along each column. The procedural surface is a 1 m staircase around the smooth designed height, so a column's probe regularly lands in a cell already air on the cut side or already rock on the fill side; nothing was stored, the run ended at step one, and that column kept its ORIGINAL height while its neighbours moved metres. **Measured: 8.7% of 253 columns did not move at all, worst 2.5 m below target**: not a rough floor, holes, each drawn as a wide gash at the terrain's 1.8 m vertex spacing. **Fixed:** `levelArea` RECORDS its decision over the band it is responsible for (`kLevelRecordBandM` = 1.5 cells about each cell's own procedural surface, read through the memo `isSolid` already paid for) via new `VoxelEdits::markAdded` / `markRemoved`. **Recording cannot change `isSolid`**, so the voxel shell, mesher, collision and every voxel pin are untouched: `expected.json` moves exactly ONE line and `changed` / `dug` / `filled` / `scanned` / `centreH` on it are unchanged. `procSolidMemo` now caches the surface RADIUS rather than the solid bit so the band test costs nothing (10.2 ms a press back to 0.9 ms). **(c) NO FEEDBACK.** Every press now quotes the flatness achieved, measured back through the oracle over 0.7R, and a no-op quotes it too, so a held key shows the number stop improving. **MEASURED, same site, same camera:** oracle spread over the pad 5.314 to 2.029 m; **DRAWN worst step within one 4 m foundation module 1.883 to 0.973 m, where the OLD code made it WORSE at 2.727 m**; per-column residual over 253 columns min -2.518 to -0.954, max +1.562 to +0.979, spread 4.08 to 1.93 m. In `/core` over 441 columns: spread 6.74 to 2.16 m, within 1 m 95% to 99%, worst stuck column 3.45 to 1.08 m. Save per pad 1,439 to 6,445 B, the price of recording the band. **PERCEPTUAL THRESHOLD, chosen and NOT met: 0.25 m over a 4 m span** (4 m is the DW-32 module, so it is the span a structure bridges; 0.22 m is the unevenness the build system already measured as placeable; 0.25 m at 1.6 m of eye height is a step you do not notice, 0.5 m is knee height and reads as a terrace). A 1 m Cartesian lattice cut by a non-axis-aligned plane cannot reach it, so `probes/level.js` REPORTS it instead of asserting it, and DW-32 foundations remain the answer to a slope. **Sub-cell fill assessed and REJECTED**: partial occupancy turns two sparse id SETS into maps, replaces the cube mesher with surface nets, and redefines `solidCell`, which is the shape DW-26 pins and asserts a bound on; it would re-baseline every voxel pin, the near mesher and collision, for a job DW-32 has already reassigned to foundations. New `of.meshVerts(x,y,z,r)` returns the vertices the GPU has this frame: the first instrument here able to tell the oracle and the picture apart. 23/23 ctest, parity 119/119 + 106/106, npm run check green (133 files). | A tool can pass every test of its EFFECT and still be unreachable, and the measurement that catches that is about the INPUT: the pitch at which the tool first finds ground, swept, against the pitch a player looks from. And spread is a claim about the middle of a distribution while a surface is judged by its worst neighbouring pair, which is why the fix is stated as a local bound over a named span, measured on the drawn geometry (ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 123). | **Accepted** (levelling repair; WG-22 line, DW-24 / DW-32 context) | 2026-07-26 |
| WG-24 | **THE VOXEL REPRESENTATION CHANGED: signed density at lattice CORNERS, meshed by SURFACE NETS.** `core/include/of/voxel_field.h` + `core/include/of/surface_nets.h` + `core/tests/test_voxel_field.cpp` (143 checks). Three separate user complaints had ONE cause and it was the representation, binary occupancy on a 1 m lattice drawn as cubes: "the way the ground breaks when you dig is kinda fucky ... all these sharp edges" IS the mesher; "Q still makes the ground more rough not flat" is the same lattice seen from above, because a whole-cell edit can only put a surface on a lattice plane so a pad cut by a non-axis-aligned target plane terminates on a staircase; and DW-33's 1.01 m spread under a 4 m module is that staircase measured by the build system. WG-23 had ASSESSED sub-cell fill and REJECTED it, correctly for what was being asked then and not for three complaints plus an explicit request for smoothing. **(a) The field.** The untouched planet ALREADY HAD a smooth signed field, `procDensity(p) = surfaceRadiusAt(p) - |p|`, and the old code was discarding it by keeping only the sign; along a radial that expression is exactly a signed distance (`d/dr == -1`), so nothing had to be invented for 99.99% of the world. An edit is a sparse per-corner override and the ops are SDF CSG: dig is `min` with a sphere, fill is `max` with a sphere, levelling ASSIGNS the plane. Any point's density is the TRILINEAR interpolation of its cell's eight corners and that interpolation is THE authority, so the mesher, collision, the aim ray and the column height query cannot disagree about where the surface is. **(b) The mesher.** Naive surface nets: one vertex per crossed cell at the mean of that cell's edge crossings, one quad per lattice edge that changes sign, normals from the trilinear gradient AT the vertex so neighbouring cells shade smoothly. Chosen over marching cubes for one vertex per cell instead of five triangles, no 256-entry case table, no ambiguous-face resolution, and trivially watertight connectivity. **(c) The oracle.** `surface_field.h` root-finds the field instead of walking a contiguous run of edited CELLS, which DELETES WG-23's `kLevelRecordBandM` prosthetic entirely: that band existed only because a run of cells breaks whenever one cell is already in the state the op wanted, which on a staircase happens constantly. A sideways tunnel still lowers nothing, now automatically. `surface_walk.h` follows. `voxel_terrain.h`'s `VoxelEdits` is DEMOTED exactly as `terrain_deform.h` was in WG-21: still compiles, still has its suite, used by nothing live. **(d) Bridge, ABI 6 to 7.** `of_exposed_faces` GONE, replaced by `of_surface_nets` + `of_surface_nets_brick`; four signatures gained a `body` because "is this cell removed" is now DERIVED against the procedural surface; new self-describing save format (`0x4F464633`, v3) that still honours the client's single-zero-byte reset. **(e) Client.** `VoxelMesh.ts` consumes triangles; `VoxelGreedy.ts` DELETED with the cubes it merged; `VoxelSkin.filterDrawnFaces` deleted because the ownership question moved into `/core` where it can be asked of the edit store directly; `flatShading` removed, since it would have thrown away the gradient normals; **`SurfaceOracle.solidForAim` collapsed to one line returning `solidAt`**, retiring ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 110's client-side reconciliation at its root rather than keeping the sixth surface definition alive. **MEASURED. Q flattening, worst height step over a 4 m span (the DW-32 module, so the span a foundation bridges), on a site with 7.03 m of spread across 12 m: 2.1408 m before, 0.0000 m after**, against the 0.25 m threshold WG-23 defined and reached only 0.973 m on; 0 of 49 columns off target; the DRAWN pad's radial spread 0.000001 m over 223 vertices. **DW-26 re-derived and still asserted:** cell-versus-point 0.4012 m worst against the unchanged 0.8660 m half-cell-diagonal bound, and the bound that actually hurt, drawn-versus-collided, **0.8660 m (a whole cell face) to 0.087116 m**. Digging: the carved sphere is the surface to 0.0957 m, 77 distinct normals where a cube mesher emits exactly 6. Tunnel residual 0.0000 m. Save 11,082 B per 10 m pad against WG-23's 6,445. **Two bugs worth keeping, both found by measurement and neither visible in the design:** the CSG form of levelling (min above the target, max below) gets every SIGN right and is still wrong, because surface nets interpolates VALUES and a corner half a metre under the target keeps its procedural +2.78 against the plane's -0.4 above it, so the crossing lands at 12% of the edge and the pad comes out 1.24 m rough; and `unitOf(unitOf(v))` is not bit-identical to `unitOf(v)`, so re-normalising an already-unit direction inside the column query made it disagree with the oracle in the last bits at every untouched column, which is a second surface definition arriving by rounding. | A representation that cannot express the answer cannot be tuned into expressing it. WG-22 measured a 2.3x spread collapse and WG-23 a further 2x, and the user still said the tool did not work, because both were improving a staircase. The general form, which is DW-26's lesson pointed at the storage rather than at the API: **when three separate complaints share a root, the root is usually the data model, and the cheapest honest fix is to stop quantising a quantity you already have.** | **Accepted** (WG-22/WG-23 line; DW-26 re-derived) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-25 | **THE NOISE STACK HAD NO DETAIL, AND IT HAD KILOMETRE WALLS** (`cubed_sphere.h`, `biome.h`, `core/tools/terrain_probe.cpp`). Two defects behind "mountains look like shit lol", and only one was the one I sent a subagent to find. **(a) Expected:** no high-frequency content at all. The finest octave, f=320, is a 1.9 km lattice cell at about 30 m of amplitude, so at the shipped 1.8 m LOD the ground was a PLANE. The wavelength sweep is unambiguous: RMS neighbour difference over spacing 9.02% at 1 m, 9.01% at 2 m, 9.01% at 5 m. The slope spectrum on a peak ran p50 0.616 to max 0.630, a 1.3% spread between the median and the worst case. A mountain was a 30 km dome because `ridged()` starts at amplitude 0.5 and halves, so its FIRST octave carried half the relief. **(b) NOT expected, and larger:** `designedReliefFactor` switched on the DISCRETE biome, so the Hills/Mountains boundary was a **985 m vertical wall between two samples 100 m apart** (2,700 m of base relief read 3,375 m as Hills at gain 1.25 and 4,320 m as Mountains at 1.60). Coastlines carried a ~1.2 km wall where Ocean jumped to a separately generated basin, the moisture threshold about 860 m, the ice cap 240 m. So the complaint was flat domes separated by sheer unclimbable steps. **FIXED:** a smoothstep orogeny mask replacing `max(0,L0)`; a ridged multifractal on a domain-warped dir replacing `ridged`; detail from f=80 to f=2500; every hard biome gain replaced by `designedGainForRelief`, a monotone C1 smoothstep blend, so `rel*gain` has no step anywhere; the sea clamp removed, which let the ocean-basin branch and its coastline wall go. **MEASURED:** worst grade over 100 m **985.37% to 87.54%** (home 985.37% to 108.07%); slope p90/p50 at 2 m **1.013 to 2.28**; wavelength coverage **240 km-1.9 km to 240 km-60 m**; Mountains biome 3.4% to 7.0%. **COST WENT THE RIGHT WAY:** raw `sampleHeightField` 11 to 19 valueNoise calls, 1.93x slower and inside the 2x gate, but **`sampleDesignedHeight`, the one authority every consumer reads, 40 to 19 calls and 2.00x FASTER**, because it had been evaluating the height field THREE times per vertex (directly, inside `biomeAt`, and inside `temperatureAt`); the browser HUD shows the oracle at **2.23 to 0.97 us/call**. A DW-14 win too: `temperatureAt` computed `cos(asin(dir.y))` where `dir.y` IS `sin(lat)`, so it is `sqrt(1-y*y)`, removing two libm calls and one real mingw-versus-musl divergence from the classifier. **`test_world_gen.cpp` needed NO change** (WV1-WV3 and the relief bound pass unmodified), the cleanest evidence the crack-free property survived, because height stayed a pure function of the shared dir. Exactly one re-baseline, `orig::sampleHeightField` in `procgen_optimized_is_bit_identical`, with `orig::valueNoise` deliberately UNTOUCHED so the test still proves the WG-16 hoist; `procgen_bench` re-baselined the same way, still bit-identical, 2.60x. | The visible complaint was one defect and the measurement found two. And a benchmark's reference copy is a second thing to re-baseline: re-baselining the wrong half would have silently retired the proof. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-26 | **THE FLAT START AREA, and it goes through the oracle.** Asked for explicitly, "for simplicity". `BodyParams` gains `homeDir` / `homeFlatRadiusM` / `homeBlendRadiusM` (zero on Cinder) and `sampleDesignedHeight` becomes a strict wrapper over `designedHeightNoPad` adding a smoothstep blend. It lives in the SURFACE FUNCTION, so mesh, collision, walker, voxel solidity, deposit snapping and build placement all get it with **no special case in any consumer** (standing rule 1). Flat to 150 m, blended to nothing by 600 m. **Worst height step over a 4 m span inside the pad: 0.000000 m, exactly**, asserted as a uint64 bit compare over 5,000+ samples, because smoothstep returns exactly 0.0 inside the flat radius and the blend is written pad-first. Outside the blend radius the height is BIT-IDENTICAL to the unpadded field over 20,000 Fibonacci dirs, and the effect provably reaches zero at 601 m. `homeDir` is a hard-coded literal with a test asserting it matches `latLonToDir(2,144)` to 1e-12, because computing it with trig would reintroduce the DW-14 divergence. `worldSig` in `voxel_terrain.h` AND `fieldWorldSig` in `voxel_field.h` both hash the new fields: a BodyParams field that world generation reads and a memo key does not is a cache that answers for the wrong planet, silently. **OPEN for Reid:** at this seed the fixed spawn is now Mountains at 4,668 m on a 42% mountainside, so the pad is a plateau perched on a mountain rather than a clearing. Widening does not help; moving the spawn means `Config.ts`, which this lane does not own. | | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-27 | **ABI 7 then 8: the client reads the one signed surface, and the level op reports the diff it made.** ABI 7 wired the field through the bridge and the client (WG-24). ABI 8 followed within the hour because a WORKING levelling tool drew nothing: `of_level_area` returned CELLS whose centre crossed the zero level, and `VoxelWorld.level` returned a null dirty box when that was 0, so the near mesh never rebuilt. On a binary lattice that was sound; on a signed field it is not, because shaving 40 cm off a slope moves the surface under the whole disc without carrying one cell CENTRE across zero. **Measured in the browser: one press reported dug 0, filled 0, scanned 8,797, and had flattened the ground.** The op now counts CORNERS whose stored distance moved, and that is what the bridge returns and what the client gates on. ABI 8 rather than a silent change because the RETURN VALUE changed meaning, and nothing in the version guard can catch a client reading an old contract from a new build at the same number. Also `LEVEL.radiusM` 6 to 10 m, for a reason about the INSTRUMENT rather than the tool: the field is flat to 0.0000 m through the oracle and 0.000001 m on the extracted 1 m mesh, but the streamed chunk samples it at the shipped 1.8 m LOD, so a 6 m disc is about seven chunk vertices across and its rim wall falls inside any 4 m span; 10 m is also 2.5 DW-32 modules, so a foundation fits INSIDE the pad. **MEASURED, driven through a real DOM key event, same site and same probe WG-23 used, on the DRAWN chunk geometry: worst step within one 4 m foundation module 1.788 m to 0.000 m**, drawn spread 3.327 to 0.000 m, oracle spread 4.029 to 0.000 m. WG-23's numbers on that instrument were 1.883 to 0.973 m against a 0.25 m threshold it defined and could not meet; **it is met**. 29/29 ctest, self-determinism 119/119, cross-toolchain exact **108/108** (105/106 when the night started, the vessel lane's ABI 6 bump having run with `-SkipNative`; that pre-existing gating blocker is cleared). **OPEN:** the probe's negative control `outsideUntouched` now fails at 2.779 m on a ring at 2.5x the pad radius where it read 0.000000 m at 6 m, while `/core` proves the same property bit-identically at 30 m. Two instruments disagree and one is wrong; recorded in ROADMAP-W11 rather than smoothed over. | WG-23's lesson one layer down: **a tool can do its work correctly and still read as dead, if the number the client watches is not the number that changed.** WG-23 was an unreachable tool; this was an invisible one. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-29 | **DW-36: ONE MAP, CENTRED ON THE PLAYER, DISCOVERABLE, WITH ORE COUNTS, and discovery is a NEW `/core` field.** `core/include/of/discovery.h` + `discovery_tests` (25 tests / 484 checks), ABI **12** (`of_disc_*` in `web/wasm/of_discovery_api.inc`, typed in `web/src/sim/wasm/discabi.ts`), and the map WIDENED rather than rebuilt: the mode, the modal registration, the `MAP_ALLOWED` list and the node UI all survive. **(a) THE CENTRE IS A PARAMETER.** `MapScene` gained `centreM` and the change is absorbed by `toPx`, which is the property MapDraw already claimed ("the projection lives in one place"); `paintBody` stopped stamping its arc at the canvas middle and projects `toPx(0,0,0)` like everything else. Focus switching is then not a feature, it is which number goes in that field. **R17's bug had a second head:** `apsis()` nudged its label along `p − (cx,cy)`, which means "away from the body" only while the origin IS the body, so centred on a ship a PE label walks into the planet; it now nudges away from the projected body centre. `fitSpanM` likewise measured from the body and now measures from the centre to the NEAREST limb, proven identical to the old function on five scenes at `centreM=[0,0,0]` by compiling both and running them side by side. **(b) THE ZOOM CONTINUUM IS A PROPERTY, NOT A CLAIM.** No span threshold exists anywhere in the map: one exported ramp, `MapPaint.sizeAlpha(sizeM, m2p, minPx, fullPx)`, fades a layer in on ITS OWN feature size (a 9 m ore patch fades in when 9 m is a few pixels; a 9,375 m survey cell when that is), and `MapDrawReport.alphas` publishes what was painted so a probe sweeps the zoom and asserts it. Measured over 20 decades of pixels-per-metre: monotone non-decreasing, exactly 0 and 1 at the knees, largest step per 0.01 decade **0.0310**: a `if (span < X) return` would read as exactly 1.0. The giant-circle problem (centred on the player the body's projected radius is ~1e9 px and Canvas 2D is wrong past 2^24) is solved with **zero tuning** by `discCoversCanvas`, true exactly when the disc covers every canvas corner, so filling and stroking provably paint the same pixels. **The AXIS rides on the FOCUS, not on the zoom**: a first draft slewed it with scale, which would have made a maneuver planned at one zoom foreshorten at another. **(c) THE RULE, and the third option again.** A cell is discovered when it was above the observer's horizon. That alone fails the way Reid predicted, at 80 km it takes 3.7% to 7.5% of Forge per instant and ~40% per lap, and the answer is NOT a smaller radius: **height buys EXTENT and costs RESOLUTION**, which one radius cannot express. So ONE rule at TWO resolutions: **SURVEY** (9,375 m cells, uncapped, the shape of the world, what the map shades) and **EXPLORE** (293 m cells, capped at a 10 km ground chord, the detail, what gates an ore patch). A lap gives you the continents and a 20 km thread under the ground track. Every number is in `DiscoveryTuning` with its derivation; `horizonFraction` defaults to **1.0**, the geometric horizon, because the derived value is the honest default and the dial exists for balance rather than for making a test pass. **NO TRANSCENDENTALS**: the horizon is `dot >= R/(R+h)`, the cap is the chord form `dot >= 1 − (c/R)²/2`, both exact arithmetic, because CE-11/DW-15 make `tan/asin/atan2/cos` a cross-toolchain hazard and 1 ULP would flip a boundary cell in a SAVED set. **(d) THE LATTICE IS enemies.h's, CHECKED NOT CLAIMED.** `SurfaceCellGrid` is a second copy of `PollutionField`'s raw-gnomonic grid (enemies.h is another lane's and a spatial index should not drag the enemy model in), so the suite includes BOTH headers: **560,000 directions, 0 `cellOf` mismatches, 0 `cellCentreDir` BIT mismatches** (uint64 via memcpy, never `CHECK_NEAR`), **62,940 neighbour cells including all 72 cube-corner cells, 0 mismatches**.
