Flagged not taken: enemies.h should adopt this grid so there is one copy rather than two agreeing ones. **(e) THE FLOOD IS PROVEN BY BRUTE FORCE.** 72 cases, **1,105,920 cells enumerated exhaustively, 50,023 inside the cap, 0 MISSING and 0 unsound**; the unconditionally-seeded observer cell is asserted as a NAMED exception rather than absorbed as slack. `accepted` is pinned against the exact continuum cap-cell count to −2.9/+4.5/+26.0 against perimeters of 161/322/643, grows as r² (4.008x, 4.001x), and is **2.7879x** at a face-edge midpoint and **5.0618x** at a cube corner against the exact gnomonic Jacobians 2^1.5 and 3^1.5, which pins the 2.12x non-uniformity the lattice pays for being transcendental-free **and would fail loudly if anyone swapped in `cubed_sphere.h`'s tan() warp**, since both ratios would collapse toward 1. **(f) THE SUITE FOUND A REAL DEFECT AND CONTRADICTED TWO OF MY OWN COMMENTS.** The deserialize guard read `if (i > 0 && d == 0)`, but the accumulator is `uint64` and the delta comes off a save file, so a delta near 2^64 walks `prev` BACKWARDS with `d != 0`: the guard waves it through, the sort never runs, and every `has()` becomes a binary search over an unsorted vector. Measured: a 3-cell stream deserialized to `[1000, 499, 509]` and returned `true`, exactly the failure the comment claimed to prevent. Fixed to `next <= prev`, free on the legal path, **verified by reverting: exactly 1 test and 3 checks fail by name.** Also fixed on review: `cells_.reserve(n)` took a hostile varint straight off the stream (a corrupt save became `bad_alloc` rather than a refusal) and now reserves against the lattice's own cell count; and `WorldDiscovery::deserialize` was `a && b` over two layers, which put a HALF-LOADED world behind a `false`, and is now all-or-nothing. **Two header comments were wrong and are now the measurements**: the 80 km horizon is **291,042.8 m**, not the "285 km" I wrote from memory, and "re-observing known ground merges nothing, so standing still is one dot product" is **false**: a warm pass measured 1.04x to 1.40x MORE than a cold one, because a cold store is empty and there is nothing to search. The true property, now asserted, is `added == 0` AND the store's buffer address does not move: a `set_union` into a fresh vector must move it, so a no-op provably cannot have run. **That is a structural check where a stopwatch would have been a threshold**: the same lesson as the inverted winding and the stale control. **(g) COST, and the claim narrowed to the true one.** The FLOOD is O(AREA) and specifically not O(machines/patches/vessels), because `observe` takes a direction and an altitude and cannot see an entity: **0.69 µs on foot, 59 µs at 10 km, 388 µs at 80 km** (survey); ~420 µs from any altitude for the capped explore layer. The MERGE is not: 557 µs at 6.8k cells held rising to 1,890 µs at 387k, so 56x the store for 3.39x the cost, sublinear, asserted, bounded at 3.5 ms once a second on a fully lapped Forge, and improved on review by walking one advancing cursor instead of a fresh binary search per key. **Save cost 1.0398 bytes/cell** (delta-varint over a sorted key set, derived ceiling 1.1172): a whole 80 km lap, 40.8% of Forge surveyed, is **1.33 MB for both layers**; a 100 km walk on foot is 3,679 bytes. Round trip exact; the byte stream is a pure function of the SET (proven by filling in two orders); a stream cut at a different cell size is REFUSED rather than reinterpreted onto the wrong lattice. **(h) ORE COUNTS, Factorio's model, ONE POOL.** The map draws `OrePatch::RemainingAmount` straight through the bridge, unrounded and unformatted, and `MapDrawReport.oreDrawnRows` publishes the raw integers so a probe compares field for field rather than parsing "2.4k" back into a number, the method the power panel was proven with. **(i) THE GATE IS ONE PLACE.** `MapWorld.ore()` drops a patch whose ground is unexplored, so an undiscovered patch is ABSENT from the array the painter receives rather than present-and-skipped: a drawing bug cannot leak one because the painter never held one. The mode is asked BY NAME through a new `ModeRules.fullMapRevealed` (DW-31/DW-36), never a raw `sandbox` boolean. Persistence is additive and deliberately does NOT bump `SAVE_VERSION` (a bump refuses every existing world, and nothing here would MISREAD an old slot); `RestoreLedger.discovery` has THREE states because a silent 0 would make a world `/core` refused look like a new game. **31/31 ctest** (was 30), TIER 0 **126/126** and TIER A **108/108** unmoved, `expected.json` moved by exactly one line (`"abi": 11 -> 12`, which `dump_expected.cpp` prints and `parity.mjs` asserts, so it is an artifact an ABI bump must move). **(j) THE DRIVEN VERIFICATION, and THREE MORE DEFECTS it found, two of them mine.** `probes/discovery.js` (new, 847 lines). **The negative control, a 26-rung fixed ladder at bearing 45 degrees with a `forget` and one observation per rung:** survival draws 0 of 4 patches from 2,600 m out to 2,100 m, **1 of 4 at 2,050/2,000/1,950 m (the partial frame, which is the rung that proves the gate rather than the absence)**, and 4 of 4 from 1,900 m in; sandbox draws 4 of 4 at every rung including 2,600 m. Survival's drawn set is a STRICT SUBSET of sandbox's at every rung, compared by raw `resource` + raw `centre` + raw `initial` tuples rather than by count; `drawn + hidden` equals `/core`'s patch count at all 26 rungs in both modes; and `spanM`, `focusActive`, `pixelsPerMetre`, `discoveredQuads`, `currentPoints`, `plannedPoints`, `surveyCells` and `exploreCells` are IDENTICAL between the two modes at all 26 rungs, **the same world in the same frame differs in nothing but which patches reached the painter.** (One last-ULP difference in `focusCentreM` at one rung, 3e-10 m, is float noise in the physics settle and is reported rather than absorbed.) **The shading property turned out NOT to be what the brief assumed**: `discoveredQuads` is EQUAL between the modes, not smaller in survival, because `MapWorld.shading` has no mode gate at all, it draws only what IS discovered, so there is nothing to hide. Asserting it smaller would have been inventing an assertion to make a number match, and the probe says so instead. **The zoom sweep, 110 notches out at x1.15 and 110 back, 600 m to 2.85e9 m:** every alpha monotone in pixels-per-metre out and back; worst single-notch move ore 0.18374 / discovered 0.18685 / body 0.16121 against bounds **0.18687 / 0.18687 / 0.16124 DERIVED from `sizeAlpha`'s own ramp under a x1.15 pixel step** rather than chosen; every alpha reaches exactly 0 and exactly 1; **660 of 660 sampled alphas `===` `sizeAlpha(feature size, reported ppm)`, so one ramp and provably nothing else**; reversible to 2.35e-15 relative over 220 notches against a 9.77e-14 float budget; `bodyFilled` flips once each way in the SAME bracket both ways with no hysteresis, and the marker delta at the flip is exactly `["air"]`, correct, because once the disc provably covers the canvas the annulus is entirely off it. **Ore, field by field:** all 4 patches match `/core` before and after mining, `===` on `resource` and `centre` and the exact inverse of `OreField.report`'s known rounding on the amounts (never a tolerance, never a parsed string), and the painter's own `oreDrawnRows` matches the handed rows field for field; 12 strikes paid 12 times, pack +24 Raw iron, pool 1812.5088938553483 -> 1788.5088938553483, fell exactly 24. **Frame cost:** map closed 8.12 ms; open at a 600 m span **+0.85 ms**; open at a 1,729,530 m whole-body span **+0.69 ms**: the SURFACE framing is the expensive one, and the cost is the ore labels and the label avoider rather than the quads. **THE THREE DEFECTS. (D1, fixed by the probe lane)** `alphas.discovered` stepped 0 to 1 in ONE zoom notch, because `of_disc_window` selects cells on their CENTRE and the margin was a multiple of the SPAN only, so a cell WIDER than the view was dropped entirely: **the surface map drew with no discovered shading at all** at the 600 m span it opens on. Fixed by adding the cell's own half-diagonal to the window radius. Note that my own earlier fix to that same constant, 0.75 to 1.6 spans, moved the step rather than removing it, a margin expressed only in spans cannot be right for a feature measured in cells. **(D2, mine)** the 1 Hz sample interval WAS NOT IN FORCE: `Systems` passes `loop.simSecs`, which is a CUMULATIVE clock because `flight.frame` on the line above wants one, and `MapMode.frame` treated it as a delta, so from one sim second on, every frame added at least 1.0 and the gate opened every frame. **Measured 144.0 passes per sim second**, an orbital pass costing ~2.2 ms in every FRAME instead of once a second, which is exactly what `Discovery.ts`'s own header rules out. And the check that existed to catch it could not: `gapRatio` was then measuring frame-to-frame motion, ~8 cm against a 2 km sweep, which `toFixed(4)` rounds to exactly 0. **A guard rounded into silence by the very defect it was watching for**: the sharpest instance of standing rule 11 this lane has produced. `MapMode.frame(nowS)` now differences the clock itself and the parameter is named for what it is. **(D3, mine, and the one that mattered)** discovery DID NOT SURVIVE A PAGE RELOAD: `restored.discovery = -1`, because `Gameplay.create` runs `g.load()` at Boot line ~267 while `bootMap` constructs the field at line ~370, so `of_disc_deserialize` was called before the field existed and `bootMap` then built an empty one over the top, after which the 20 s autosave wrote the empty set back and it was gone for good. **The in-page round trip PASSED (279 cells, byte-identical), which is precisely why an in-page probe alone would have shipped it**, and `reload.mjs` did not assert discovery at all. Both halves are being fixed: the save is made SELF-DESCRIBING (it carries its own body radius, so a load can happen before anything has told the field which body it is on and the boot order stops mattering), and `reload.mjs` gains the assertion, required to FAIL against the old code before it is believed. **Acceptance:** `ascent.js` green, `flightabuse.js` green, `maneuver.js` green at 65/65. `maneuver.js` did NOT survive the centring change and should not have: rows B1/B2 asserted "the map key off the vessel does not open the map" and "the HUD says so", which DW-36 deliberately reverses. The probe's expectation was stale, not a map regression, and it was replaced with SIX STRONGER rows, including **R17's own negative control, `|centreM|` = 604,668 m, within 1% of the body radius and NOT ~0**, which is the one number that distinguishes a player-centred projection from a body-centred one. `controls.js`'s map opener had been permanently SKIPPED because the map refused on foot, so that modal's Escape derivation had never once run; it now runs and passes. | The map was the last place this project contradicted its own premise: the engine spends a year making surface-to-orbit continuous and then the map hard-cut into a separate mode, which is why DW-36 calls the continuum "not a UI flourish". Making the CENTRE a parameter collapses four features into one, focus switching, re-centring, the plan view and the orbital view are one projection with a different origin. Discovery belongs in `/core` and not in the UI because it is world state: it must persist, be deterministic and be cheap, and the enemies lane had already solved that exact shape. Two resolutions rather than two rules is the DW-7/DW-26 move again, when both options are unattractive, find the third, and it is what makes orbit worth flying without making it a cheat. | **Accepted** (DW-36; physics R17; DW-17/DW-31; standing rules 4, 9, 10, 11) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-28 | **THE ADVERSARIAL PASS OVER THE FIELD, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT WAS WRONG.** Standing rule 11 applied to WG-24's headers, which five systems now stand on. **(a) THE TWO INSTRUMENTS, resolved: THE PROBE WAS WRONG AND THE LEVEL OP IS CORRECT.** `probes/level.js`'s negative control read 2.779 m of movement "outside the radius". It sized its ring at 2.5x **its own local copy of the pad radius, `R = 6.0`**, so 15 m. WG-27 widened `LEVEL.radiusM` to 10 and the disc is centred on the AIM POINT, up to `reachM` = 9 m downhill of the player, so the pad reached **16.279 m from the feet** and the control ring was policing ground **1.17 m inside it**. Measured, with the discs the tool reported: the two ring points that moved were **8.844 m and 9.968 m from the disc axis, and the next point out at 10.386 m moved 0.000000 m**, against a radius of 10.000. `levelDisc`'s corner loop rejects on the unpadded radius, so the op's footprint is right; the control's geometry was a remembered constant. **Fixed at the root rather than by re-tuning a number**: `LevelStats` now publishes `lastCentreM` / `lastRadiusM` / `maxRimFromFeetM`, `terraform()` publishes `limits`, and the probe derives its ring from `reachM + radiusM` and **asserts it cleared every disc the tool cut** (`controlRingClearsThePad`, ring 20.000 m against a rim of 16.279 m, clearance 3.721 m). `outsideUntouched` is back to **0 of 12 points moved, 0.000000 m**. **The general form: a control whose geometry is a constant copied from the thing it watches stops being a control the moment that thing is retuned, and it fails in the direction that looks like a real defect.** **(b) EVERY TRIANGLE THE MESHER EMITTED WAS WOUND INSIDE OUT.** Found by the review, confirmed independently two ways before touching it: stepping 0.35 m along each triangle's geometric normal landed in ROCK for **258 of 258** crater triangles and **259 of 260** on a mound, and the geometric normal disagreed with the mesher's own per-vertex gradient normal on **100%**. The client draws this mesh `MeshLambertMaterial`, no `side` override, so three.js back-face-culls it, and an inverted CLOSED surface does not vanish, it draws its **far side through its near side**. **That is what the "isolated pale fragments inside the crater" were**, and `docs/screenshots/WG28_crater_before.png` against `WG28_crater_after.png` is a black void full of pale shards against a lit, readable bowl. It survived 143 green checks because winding is invisible to every question the suite asked, and the one test that reads `indices` past a count **sorts each triple**, erasing winding by construction. New `mesh_triangles_face_out_of_the_rock`; **verified by reverting the fix in a scratch copy, where it fails 9 checks in exactly one test and nothing else in the suite notices**. **(c) THE COST, 4.6x cold and 12.8x warm, with a byte-identical fixture.** `levelDisc` asked EVERY cell in the padded box for its solidity, twice, to compute `dug` / `filled`: about 32,000 `solidCell` calls where the op writes ~2,000 corners. A cell reads exactly its own eight corners, so a cell none of whose corners the op writes cannot change; the cell passes are now restricted to that candidate set, marked while the corner pass is already visiting each corner. Writes are deferred to their own pass so "before" is still read against an untouched field. **Native A/B, same binary, same sites: cold 42.7 to 9.3 ms, warm 12.9 to 1.0 ms, and `expected.json` is byte-identical**, which is the proof the output did not move. In the browser a press is now **39.7 ms cold (op 15.2, near-mesh rebuild 25.6, quote 1.2) and 7.0 ms held (op 2.4, rebuild 2.8)**; the remaining cold cost is the re-mesh, not the terraforming. **(d) `of_streamer_level` still gated on `res.cells()`**, so ABI 8's fix was applied to the main thread and not to the worker that owns the drawn chunk. Corrected to `res.corners`. Honest about magnitude: a sweep from 0.5 m down to 0.001 m of edit could not produce a `cells == 0, corners > 0` press at the test site, so this removes a class rather than a measured symptom. **(e) `setCorner` never updated `airCount_` on an OVERWRITE**, so the air/rock split drifted the moment a second op touched a corner the first had written: dig 2.5 m then fill 3.2 m reported **257 air / 187 rock against a true 212 / 232**. The client uses those two numbers for drift detection between its copy of the edit set and the worker's, so a counter that lies disarms a detector. The shipped round-trip assertion applies exactly ONE op, the only case where the bug is invisible. **(f) `fieldWorldSig` omitted two BodyParams fields world generation reads**: `kind`, which dispatches the entire height stack, and `homeBlendRadiusM`. Warmed on one body the memo answered for another, out by **2,778 m across `kind`** and **28.7 m inside the blend annulus**, and the bridge makes it reachable because `of_edits_create` binds a field to no body. The identical omission sat under the identical warning comment in `voxel_terrain.h`; both fixed, `fieldWorldSig` made public because the only way to observe a memo key is to compare two of them and it had no test at all. **Two fixture lines move and both ARE the fixes** (`voxel.faceHash`, the winding; `digColumn.removed` 9 to 29, the air counter). 29/29 ctest, self-determinism **119/119**, cross-toolchain exact **108/108**, `npm --prefix web run check` green at 166 files. **NOT FIXED, logged with numbers in ROADMAP-W11**: the floating-slab bypass in `columnSurfaceHeight`'s first sample (a slab 21 to 27 m up pins the heightfield to the +24 m cap), `runIsAnchored`'s floor being the designed base (so nothing below the base can read as floating; a slab in a pit refills it by 10.9 m), `columnTouched` documented as exact and missing 19.29% of the true stencil (sub-millimetre today), and `surfaceNets` returning an empty mesh when `maxCells` is exceeded, which is byte-identical to "there is no surface here". | The review found more in one pass than the implementation lane found in a night, on a header the implementation lane had just measured to four decimal places, which is standing rule 11 stated as an outcome rather than a principle. Two lessons are worth more than the defects. **A negative control that starts failing after a rewrite is a claim about the CONTROL as often as about the code, and telling which apart requires the control to name its own subject.** And **a proxy metric can rank a broken frame better than a fixed one**: counting bright outliers inside the crater read 4 before and 9 after, because a black void has nothing to be an outlier against, and only looking at the two captures explained it. DW-7 for the third time: structural validation cannot replace looking at the thing. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-30 | **D3: WHAT THE PLAYER EXPLORED DID NOT SURVIVE A RELOAD, AND THE 20 s AUTOSAVE THEN MADE IT PERMANENT. Fixed by making the save SELF-DESCRIBING, which removes the ordering dependency instead of managing it.** `reload.mjs --phase=ground` reported `persist.restored.discovery = -1` while the in-page `of.save()`/`of.wipe()`/`of.load()` round trip PASSED byte-identically at 279 cells, which is exactly why an in-page probe alone would have shipped it. **The cause was BOOT ORDER, not the format.** `Boot.ts` applies the save early (`Gameplay.create` → `Persist.apply` → `_of_disc_deserialize`) and builds the map late (`bootMap` → `new Discovery(...)` → `_of_disc_reset`). A layer stream carried `bits`, which says what the keys MEAN but not what BODY they are on, so a reader had to be told the radius by somebody else before it could read: at load time the `/core` field did not exist, `of_disc_deserialize` returned -1 immediately, and `bootMap` then built a fresh empty field over the top, which the next autosave wrote back to disk. **THE FIX IS TWO HALVES AND EITHER ALONE STILL LOSES THE WORLD. (a) `WorldDiscovery` gained a stream preamble**: magic `'OFDW'`, a version, and the BODY RADIUS as `w.f64`, and `deserialize` REBUILDS both `DiscoveryGrid`s at the stream's radius when it differs, so a load may happen before anything has said which body this is. The all-or-nothing rollback got wider with it: a rebuild before the layers are read means a refusal must put the LATTICE back as well as the keys, or the cells come back into the wrong grid, which is precisely the "world that never existed" the method exists to prevent. A wrong magic is refused without consuming a payload it cannot measure. New public `bodyRadiusM()`. The format was changed outright rather than versioned around, because ABI 12 is unlanded and no shipped build has ever written a save with it. **(b) new export `of_disc_ensure(bodyId)`**, which resets ONLY when there is no field or the existing one is cut for a different body, so the LATE construction cannot wipe what the EARLY load restored; `Discovery.ts`'s constructor calls it instead of `_of_disc_reset`, and `of_disc_reset` stays unconditional because probes and new worlds want exactly that. `of_disc_deserialize` now CONSTRUCTS a placeholder field when there is none and lets the stream correct it (not a guess: the radius comes out of the bytes), drops the placeholder on a refusal, and refuses an empty arena before building anything, the reader throws past the end and this module is built with exceptions off, a case the old `if (!g_disc)` guard had been covering by accident. **NO ABI BUMP: 12 is uncommitted and unlanded, so extending it is not a contract change**; the changelogs in `of_core_api.cpp` and `OfCore.ts` now describe what 12 actually is. **THE ASSERTION THAT WAS MISSING IS WHY IT SHIPPED**: `reload.mjs` did not check discovery at all. It now does (`the discovered world came back`), and it was **verified in both directions**: it FAILS `-1` against the old build and PASSES at **`restored.discovery = 378725`** against the new one, on this working tree. Two new core tests, `a_stream_carries_its_own_body_into_a_field_built_for_another` (a Cinder-cut field, 1,532 explore cells at 11 bits, loaded into a `WorldDiscovery` built at Forge: cells, lattice, cell size and every walked direction all restore correctly) and `the_self_describing_preamble_refuses_without_eating_the_payload`; the first was **negative-controlled by disabling the rebuild, where it fails by name on 28 checks and nothing else in the suite notices**. **27 discovery tests / 752 checks, 31/31 ctest, TIER 0 126/126, TIER A 108/108 (both gating, both unmoved), TIER B 148/3 informational, `expected.json` diff is the single `"abi": 11 → 12` line.** **Also found and corrected, unasked**: the `-1 IS NOT A ROLLBACK` warning in `of_discovery_api.inc` and `discabi.ts` was STALE, `WorldDiscovery::deserialize` had already been made all-or-nothing, so the comment had been wrong since it was written. | A save that cannot be read without being told what it is has an ORDER dependency, and an order dependency in a boot sequence is a defect waiting for the sequence to change. Putting the body in the bytes is not a format preference; it is the difference between a load that works wherever it is called and a load that works where it happens to be called today. **The general lesson is about the INSTRUMENT: the in-page round trip and the reload ask different questions, and only the second one is the question the player asks.** A round trip inside one page is a test of the FORMAT; only a real reload tests the LIFECYCLE, and this defect lived entirely in the lifecycle. Standing rule 11: an assertion that has never been seen to fail is not yet an assertion, so the new check was run against the broken build first. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-31 | **THE SINKING WAS BACK, IN TUNNELS, AND IT WAS GP-53's DEFECT IN A SECOND CODE PATH.** Reid: "standing still in the tunnel im getting the same sinking thing where it snaps up every few seconds." "The same" was exact. GP-53 fixed `StructureBodies.deckUnder`, a march down from the feet clamped with `Math.min(rFrom, r + 0.05)`; `VoxelCollision.floorBelow` ended `return Math.min(r, rr + 0.1)`, so with the first solid sample within one march step of the feet, or with the feet inside rock at all, the query handed back `r` itself. **The clamp is not the same line of code and it is the same sentence: a floor query that clamps its answer to the querier's own position ratifies the querier's error, and gravity supplies a fresh error every tick.** **MEASURED, per TICK, player stationary and grounded on a dug tunnel floor at 0.00 m/s, 600 ticks (`probes/tunnelsink.js`, new).** The instrument is `StandTrace` widened with `preSnapR` (the radius the walker ARRIVED at), `underRock`, `pushM` and `pushUpM`, because `groundR` alone cannot tell a ratified floor from a correct one: both are "the ground the snap used", and only `terrainR - preSnapR` separates them, with no threshold, since a floor that is a property of the world cannot equal a moving querier tick after tick by coincidence. **Site A, the sawtooth: standing spread 0.249535 m, 6 snap-ups at a mean period of 93.2 ticks, biggest snap 0.247317 m, sinking 0.002683 m EVERY tick, which is exactly one tick of free fall and exactly the number GP-53 measured on the deck. Ratification 594 of 600 ticks. Site B, the pure sink: spread 0.053811 m, ratification 600 of 600, the feet parked 0.261495 m BELOW the world's own floor with nothing ever putting them back. After, at both sites: spread 0.000000, sink 0.000000, snap-ups 0, ratification 0 of 600, worst floor error 0.000000, 600/600 grounded and under rock.** **THE CORRECTOR WAS NOT THE ONE I BRIEFED MYSELF FOR, and the trace named it in six rows**: `resolveEmbedded` pushed on **0** ticks throughout, so the snap is NOT the mouth push. It is `resolveStep`: the ratchet sinks the feet until the capsule's lowest sample at 0.15 m reads solid, `free(q)` goes false, the ledge climb lifts a full 0.55 m rung, and `floorBelow`'s clamp immediately pulls that back down to 0.3 m under the lift, netting +0.247 m. The two authorities are visible side by side at t=659/660 and invisible in any per-FRAME reading, which is how this survived GP-53's pass. **THE FIX.** `floorBelow` is now the highest air-to-rock crossing of the signed field on that radial, **bisected**, with no clamp: if the feet are in AIR the floor is strictly below them, full stop; if the feet are IN rock it looks up by at most `VOXEL_STEP_UP_M[0]` (0.55 m, the walker's own first rung, read not recited) so a floor you have sunk into seats you on top of itself; and buried past a rung it returns **null**, because that is an embedded capsule and `resolveEmbedded` owns it, and answering anything at all there is what ratified the sink. 30 halvings take the 0.25 m march bracket to 2.3e-10 m, **below the f64 spacing at Forge's 604 km radius**, so the answer is bit-independent of the bracket and the spread is 0 rather than 1e-7. Bisection also retires a cell-quantisation assumption the old march carried: since WG-24 the floor under a tunnel is an interpolated iso-surface, not a cell face, and the old code could only ever land on a multiple of its own step. **MY FIRST CUT OF THE FIX WAS WRONG AND THE DRIVE CAUGHT IT**: looking up for air unconditionally found rock ABOVE feet that were standing in air, read a ceiling fragment as a floor, and walked the player **8 m up and out of their own bore over 14 strikes**, the 0.1 m ratchet in this function's own comment history, rebuilt from the other side. **THE PROBE WAS ALSO WRONG TWICE, both times in the direction that looks like a real defect**: its step-up control walked one way, measured 0 climbs, and the reason was that it had walked 5.24 m DOWNhill; and its under-rock search only walked forward, so it reported "never got under rock" for the FIXED build while the broken one measured cleanly, which reads exactly like the fix breaking tunnels. Both now try both directions and the probe reports how the spot was reached. Gates: **31/31 ctest**, `npm --prefix web run check` green at 206 files including the boot gate, and `tunnelwalk` (13.98 m walked, 8/8 grounded under rock, push 0.000), `walkfeel`, `jump` and `walk` (100% grounded over 83.9 m) all green. | Two lessons. **The first is that GP-53's general form was the finding, not GP-53's fix**: the same sentence was true of a different function in a different lane, and the only reason it was found in one pass is that the sentence had been written down as a sentence rather than as a patch. The second is about the instrument: `resolveEmbedded` was the obvious suspect, the brief named it, and it fired zero times. Recording the CANDIDATES separately instead of the answer is what let six rows of trace overrule a hypothesis. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-32 | **A TUNNEL MOUTH READS AS SEALED FROM OUTSIDE, AND IT IS THE HEIGHTFIELD CHUNK SHEET. DIAGNOSED AND MEASURED, DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED.** Reid: "I dug a hole through this mountain, when out the other side looking back into the tunnel it looks like its filled in but when you actually go into it you can see that its a tunnel." **The layer is the streamed CHUNK mesh, not the near voxel mesh, and the number that carries it has its control in the same frame:** over a 13x13 m patch of 169 columns straddling the mouth, at chunk depth 14 (1.798 m vertex spacing), the drawn chunk surface stands above the outermost solid cell by **0.19 m on the 146 untouched columns and 4.04 m on the 23 dug ones**. At the mouth column the pooled buffer draws relief 4649.02 m while the last solid cell is at 4644.98 m: four metres of hillside painted across open air, and it is what the crosshair lands on. The field itself is fine, and says so three ways: the aim ray from outside runs **10.25 m of continuous air** to a point under 1.5 m of real roof; the oracle DID lower, by **3.912 m** at that column, on 23 of 169; and walking in takes `underRock` false to true with `skyVis` 0.999 to 0.057. **The distance dependence Reid noticed is real and measured: mouth-core tile luminance 48.07 at 4 m, 49.86 at 6 m, 85.29 at 8 m against a hillside ring of 94.82 and a tunnel interior of 43.07 measured from inside on the same run. The hole is visible to 7 m and gone at 8.** **THREE HYPOTHESES REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT, INCLUDING THE ONE I BRIEFED AS PRIME SUSPECT.** LOD/staleness: **refuted**, every drawn vertex equals `of.surface` at its own direction to **0.000 m**, 15 of 15 digs applied, depth is 14 from 4 m to 60 m of standoff with the 4.04 m identical at every one, and COARSER LOD makes it SMALLER (3.75 m at depth 13, 1.87 m at depth 12). The mechanism is SAMPLING RATE: a roughly 3 m lowered footprint on a 1.798 m vertex grid, where the five vertices contributing to the mouth column read 4649.15 / 4648.51 / 4648.51 / 4649.76 / 4646.22 against an oracle base of 4648.98 and an oracle surface of 4645.07, so exactly ONE vertex in reach caught any lowering and the triangles interpolate a shallow dimple over a 3.9 m hole. The `editedOnly` / `growCells=1` filter: **refuted**, `--voxelskin=0` moves the mouth core by 0.000 / 0.000 / 0.182. Depth precision: **refuted**, the disagreement is 4.04 m of geometry with no z-fight speckle. **THE A/B, per tile, same camera to the byte:** `--voxelnear=0` changes a third to a half of the whole frame (mean abs 11.667 / 8.898 / 5.209 per tile) and changes the MOUTH CORE by **0.047 / 0.065 / 3.736**, and removing the near mesh makes the sealed core BRIGHTER (85.29 to 89.03), never open. **The near voxel mesh is radially INSIDE the sheet by the same 4.04 m, and structurally must be: surface nets extracts the zero level of the field `solidAt` reads, and a dig only REMOVES, so the edited iso-surface can never rise above the pristine base the sheet draws when a column fails to reconcile. The voxel lip can never stand proud.** That is why RN-9's rule ("the near mesh draws only faces the heightfield cannot show") has no answer here: the heightfield cannot show this, and it draws it anyway. **NOT FIXED, and the reason is scope rather than difficulty:** the two candidate fixes are (a) make the chunk mesh resolve the notch it already knows about, by forcing a deeper split for a near chunk containing an edited column or by snapping the nearest vertices onto lowered columns, which touches `TerrainStream.ts`, `terrain.worker.ts`, `ChunkGeometryPool.ts` and `EdgeStitch.stitchEdges` and therefore crosses into rendering's chunk format and the LOD seam proofs; or (b) PUNCH the sheet, degenerating chunk triangles whose columns are fully open and letting the voxel mesh own those pixels, which also needs `editFacesOnly` to stop restricting the near mesh to `growCells = 1` around a broken column or the aperture comes out empty. (b) is architecturally the right answer and is the inverse of RN-9's rule stated properly: **a surface that cannot represent a feature must not draw over it.** Neither is a change to make without a seam and determinism pass, and NEITHER is a depth bias: `polygonOffset` and `renderOrder` are wrong here because the two surfaces are 4 m apart, not coplanar. **`probes/tunnelmouth.js` ships with the diagnosis and TWO assertions that both FAIL on this build**, verified by me independently of the subagent that wrote them: `sheetTracksFieldOverDugGround` (no threshold at all, the control is the untouched half of the same patch in the same frame at the same LOD: 4.04 against 0.19) and `mouthReadsAsOpenFromOutside` (the mouth's tiles must read nearer the tunnel interior, measured from inside on the same run, than the hillside beside them: 85.29 against 43.07 and 94.82). The probe reports `valid` for THE RUN SET UP and a separate `pass` for THE ASSERTIONS HELD, because a probe whose assertions are false under a true top-level boolean is precisely how `tunnelwalk.js` stayed quietly red for days. | The prime suspect in the brief was a coarse or stale chunk, and the distance dependence looked exactly like an LOD boundary. It is neither: the LOD is constant across the whole range where the symptom appears and goes the wrong way when it does change. What looks like an LOD artefact is a Nyquist argument, and the two are only distinguishable by reading the CONTRIBUTING VERTICES rather than the chunk depth. | **Accepted** (diagnosis; fix deferred, cross-domain with rendering) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-13 | **Wave-0 headless core BUILT + GREEN** — `core/include/of/cubed_sphere.h` + `core/tests/test_world_gen.cpp` (`world_gen_tests` target). Crack-free determinism proven **BITWISE** (uint64 bit-reinterpret `==`, not `CHECK_NEAR`): neighbour same-depth shared edges (both axes) + parent edge-midpoint vs child corner + same-seed regen are all bit-identical. Mechanism = an **integer vertex lattice**: a vertex's `unitDir` is a pure function of `(faceId, ix, iy, level)` via `latticeCoord(i,L) = -1 + 2·(i/2^L)` (identical operands/order from any quad), then height is **position-hashed** `(bodySeed, dir)` (WG-6). Implements the tangent cube→sphere warp (WG-5), the 3-layer noise stack (WG-7; planet ridged mountains / moon crater field), `SampleTerrainHeight`, and Forge/Cinder body params (Spike 1 §5). | The bit-identity guard is the only robust crack test — a hairline crack is a last-bit disagreement. Lattice-point naming (not quad-local index/sequence) is what makes shared `dir`s bit-identical regardless of which quad/depth/order samples them; position-hashing then makes the height follow. Validates WG-1/WG-5/WG-6/WG-7 in real compiled+tested C++ on g++/Ninja. Mirrors the green core-engine/factory-sim Wave-0 pattern (header-only `of::` + `test_framework.h`); added a parallel `world_gen_tests` target only — no existing target/header touched. **Result: 10 tests / 3582 checks pass; all 3 ctest suites green.** | **Accepted** (Wave-0 build; spike WV1–WV3, WV7) | 2026-06-14 |
| WG-30 | **DW-37: THE MAP DRAWS THE WORLD. A new `/core` export samples the ground over the VIEW, and the discovery quads are gone.** Reid, on the map WG-29 shipped: *"cant see the terrain from the map, even in sandbox."* Every instrument was correct - POSITION, FOCUS, the discovery counts, the scale bar, the zoom continuum - over an empty grey plane. **Discovery REVEALS terrain, so a map with no terrain has nothing to reveal and the feature could not be evaluated at all.** **(a) THE EXPORT, ABI 12 to 13.** `web/wasm/of_map_api.inc` §19: `of_map_sample(body, centre, planeU, planeV, spanM, aspect, n)` writes an `n` by `round(n*aspect)` grid of `[biomeId, heightM, surveyed]` into the f64 arena, row-major, top row first. It is specified in **view-region terms rather than pixel terms** because DW-37 turns the map into a rotatable 3D camera next: the PAINTER downstream is expected to be replaced and the SAMPLING is not, and a centre plus two axes plus a span plus a density is a question a camera asks too. The ray solve is **exact and transcendental-free** (CE-11/DW-15): the projection is orthographic, so the ground under a sample is a line meeting a sphere - `pPerp = p - a(p.a)`, `t = sqrt(R^2 - |pPerp|^2)`, near root, dot products and one sqrt, no trig and no marching. **Standing rule 1 held and is MEASURED**: the centre sample's height is **bit-identical** to `of_base_height` at the centre's own direction (`4667.788837614854`, `===`), its biome equals `biomeAt`, and an independent JS re-derivation of the same ray solve over 2,500 samples found **0 biome mismatches**; the on-body fraction matches the analytic disc area to **0.09 pp** at three framings (78.630% vs 78.540%, 39.202% vs 39.270%, 34.987% vs 34.907%); the worst `dot(dir, axis)` over an on-body grid is **+0.028**, so every solved direction is on the viewer's side and the far root is never taken. **(b) THE QUAD LAYER IS DELETED, NOT LAYERED UNDER.** A per-sample survey bit is a finer and simpler mask than a 9,375 m discovery quad, so `MapLayers.strokeDiscovered` and `Discovery.window()` are gone with their cache, their row cap and their two counters. `of_disc_window` STAYS in the ABI - it is not wrong, it is simply no longer the map's shading source, and it is still the right call for a cell overlay (the pollution layer the enemies branch will want). This deletes a whole bug class with it: the old window selected cells by their CENTRE, so it had to reason about how far a cell WIDER than the view could reach, and getting that wrong put a 0 to 1 step in `alphas.discovered` inside one zoom notch (WG-29 D1). A sample has no extent and the grid is cut to the canvas, so every sample is inside the view by construction. **(c) THE PAINTER IS ONE BLIT.** An `ImageData` at the grid's resolution into a scratch canvas, then `drawImage` scaled with smoothing - one composite instead of thousands of paths. Biome colour is a **copy by value** of `render/materials/BiomePalette.ts`'s ten hex values (src/ui may not import three.js and the render palette builds `THREE.Color` at load; copying keeps the painters pure functions of their arguments). An unpainted sample carries SURFACE's colour at **zero alpha**, so the scaled blit fades into the body fill beneath it instead of a black fringe, and the blit is CLIPPED to the body disc unless `discCoversCanvas` proves the disc covers every corner - the same fact `paintBody` already uses. **(d) THE PLAIN HEIGHT RAMP WAS NOT ENOUGH, AND ONLY LOOKING SHOWED IT (DW-7).** The first version shaded by elevation alone, exactly as briefed, and the picture was a single pale rectangle: at a 400 m span the whole view is ONE biome with 44 m of relief and the gradient is too gentle to read as ground. Every count said terrain was drawn and the picture still said nothing, which is the failure DW-37 was raised about. Added a **hillshade**, computed from the same heights, which is what the eye actually reads as terrain. **The first hillshade was ALSO blank, and its mistake looked principled**: it normalised the per-sample rise by the view's total RELIEF, but a smooth 44 m spread over 36 samples is 1.2 m a sample, so every gradient came out near 0.03 and every lambert near the same number. Real terrain slopes are SMALL at map scales - which is why cartographic relief has always used a vertical exaggeration - so the scale has to come from the view's own **typical slope**, one extra pass over the grid. Then a 400 m hillside and a whole-body mountain range light with the same contrast and there is no threshold anywhere. **(e) `n` CHOSEN BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY TASTE.** A sample costs **2.37 us** (biomeAt + sampleDesignedHeight; an off-limb sample costs a dot product and is skipped, which is why the same grid is 5x cheaper zoomed out). Swept on the shipped wasm at a 2.9:1 aspect: n=16/736 samples/1.7 ms, 24/1,680/3.5, 32/2,976/6.4, 36/3,744/8.9, 40/4,640/11.7, 48/6,672/14.3, 64/11,904/26.7, 96/26,688/58.7. The shipped panel is **1.19:1**, not 2.9:1, so re-measured in the browser on the real panel **n = 48 is a 58x48 grid, 2,784 samples, 5.2-10.0 ms a rebuild** - the same ~8 ms budget at the shape the map actually has. Steady-state **cache hit rate 0.96**. **(f) A CACHE KEYED ON A RAW POSITION IS A CACHE THAT MISSES EVERY FRAME.** Measured: a player merely SETTLING on uneven ground moved `centreM` by centimetres per frame and rebuilt the whole grid at 60 Hz, **+7.32 ms of map cost with the map open and standing still**, against +0.84 ms on a run that happened not to jitter - a factor of nine decided by noise. The request is now SNAPPED to a thirty-second of one sample, and the snapped values are what is SAMPLED and not merely what is keyed, so the grid is a deterministic function of its key rather than of whichever frame missed. **Cost after: +0.575 ms zoomed in and +0.575 ms at whole-body in sandbox, +0.582 / +0.248 in survival** - against WG-29's quad layer at **+0.85 / +0.69**. The terrain map is CHEAPER than the empty one it replaces. Rebuilds over the same 12,866-call run fell 3,459 to 507. **(g) THE NEGATIVE CONTROL, EXTENDED TO THE GROUND.** `probes/discovery.js` gained section 3b, and the ladder gained three rows. The old file asserted `discoveredQuads` IDENTICAL between the modes and was right to: quads are by definition ground you have seen, so there was nothing to hide. Terrain is not, so the number moved to the mode-DEPENDENT half and the claim sharpened. **At a 139,755 m span on the same world and the same frame: sandbox paints 2,784 of 2,784 on-body samples; survival paints 274, which is EXACTLY `/core`'s own `seenOnBody` count and strictly greater than zero and strictly fewer than the ground that is there.** Both halves are asserted in EACH run, so neither run can pass by being the other one, and the painter's count is checked against `/core`'s count of what the mask allows - two independent reads of one answer. `terrainSamples` and `sampleSizeM` are mode-blind and join the identical-across-the-pair list. At the on-foot span the mask CANNOT bite in sandbox (the whole 600 m view sits inside one 9,375 m survey cell) and the probe says so rather than leaving it unexplained: 26/26 rungs fully painted in sandbox, **16/26 in survival**, where the view straddles a cell boundary. **(h) THE ZOOM CONTINUUM SURVIVED, IN A STRONGER FORM.** The terrain layer's feature is a SAMPLE of ground and the grid is cut to the canvas, so a sample's ground size scales with the span exactly as pixels-per-metre falls: **its size in PIXELS is invariant**. Its alpha is therefore constant rather than ramping, which is a stronger statement of "no threshold" than a ramp - there is nothing left to step. Asserted as exactly constant over all 220 notches, as `=== sizeAlpha(sampleSizeM, ppm)` at every one of them, and the invariance is checked on the pixel size itself. The other two alphas still reach 0 and 1 (ore worst step 0.18374 against a derived bound 0.18687; body 0.16121 against 0.16124). **(i) AND ONE STALE ASSERTION REPLACED BY A STRONGER ONE.** `the fill flip draws exactly the same content` compared `discoveredQuads` across the `discCoversCanvas` notch and failed at 2,784 -> 2,716. It was not a defect: `discCoversCanvas` is true exactly while the body covers every canvas CORNER, so the notch that flips it IS the notch at which the corners leave the body, and a sample off the limb has no ground under it. The row now asserts that the on-body count MUST move there, and in which direction - the same geometric fact computed in two entirely separate places, the painter's projected radius and `/core`'s per-sample ray solve. **Green:** 31/31 ctest, TIER 0 **126/126** and TIER A **108/108** unmoved, `expected.json` moved by exactly the `"abi": 12 -> 13` line, `probes/discovery.js` **valid in BOTH modes with 0 fails**, `ascent.js` green, `reload.mjs` `fails: []` with `restored.discovery = 378174`, `npm run check` clean for every file this lane touched. New `probes/mapshot.js` frames the three screenshots by driving the real M key and the real zoom hook, because a picture is a thing you look at and take again and the acceptance run is twenty minutes. | The complaint was one sentence and the fix had three layers, and only the first was the one that was briefed. The EXPORT was the easy half. What was not: an elevation ramp that is the honest shading of a height field draws nothing a person recognises as ground, and its replacement's first normalisation was dimensionally correct and equally blank - both were found by looking at the picture, twice, after the counts already said the layer was alive (DW-7 for the fourth time in this lane). And a cache keyed on a raw player position is not a cache; the difference between +7.3 ms and +0.58 ms was a decision about what counts as the same request. | **Accepted** (DW-37; WG-29 line; standing rules 1, 5, 9, 11) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-33 | **THE CLOSE-IN MAP WAS READING THE WORLD BEFORE THE PLAYER TOUCHED IT, AND SHADING ONE BAND OF A FIELD THAT HAS NO CONTENT AT ITS OWN SAMPLE SPACING. ABI 13 to 14.** Reid pressed M at the default surface view and saw a flat slate field with ore circles floating on it. Every count was green: 2,784 painted of 2,784 on-body, alphas 1, nothing refused. `painted == onBody` was true and worthless, which is the first half of `mapshot.js`'s own header proving itself (DW-7). **(a) ADMIN'S HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT.** The proposed cause was a planet-scale ramp keyed to ABSOLUTE elevation. It was not: DW-37's ramp was already normalised to the frame's own relief and its hillshade to the frame's own mean slope, and the numbers say so. Over the painted samples the blank 454 m frame scored lumaSd 22.96, lumaSpread 66 and 10 tone buckets against the LEGIBLE 52 km frame's 21.87, 70 and 11. **The close-in map was already using the full tone range.** What it had no trace of was LOCAL contrast: the mean tone difference between ADJACENT samples read 1.94 blank against 8.61 regional, a factor of 4.4 where the global numbers differed by 5%. **(b) THE MAP READ THE DESIGNED BASE, WHICH IS BY DEFINITION THE WORLD BEFORE THE PLAYER TOUCHED IT.** `of_map_sample` called `sampleDesignedHeight`, so a dug hole, a levelled pad and a tunnel mouth could not reach the map AT ALL, however the painter shaded them, and a close-in map's whole job is to show you your own work. ABI **14** puts an `edits` handle in second position and reads `surface_field.h`'s `surfaceHeight` instead, WG-21's bedrock clamp included. Breaking on that ONE signature, one day old, one caller. With `edits <= 0` the value is bit-identical to ABI 13's by the oracle's own undug overload. The handle is taken from the `SurfaceOracle` OBJECT and read every sample, never cached, because the handle is bound after boot and a copy taken in the constructor would be a permanent 0. The two sparse-set sizes ride in the grid cache key, so a dig invalidates the picture. **(c) TWO RELIEF BANDS, not one.** `MapRelief.ts`: `litFull` is the field's own hillshade, which draws a mountain range; `litDetail` is the same lambert on the field with its LOCAL TREND removed (three masked box passes, an eighth of the frame across), which draws a levelled pad, a dug trench and the microrelief of a desert. A hillshade of a SMOOTH field is a smooth wash however well its magnitude is normalised, because every sample then has nearly the same slope DIRECTION as its neighbour. **One box pass stamped soft axis-aligned RECTANGLES across the map and three do not; found by LOOKING, because a rectangle of trend has exactly the contrast statistics of a hill.** Both windows are fixed counts of SAMPLES and never distances, so there is no zoom threshold (DW-36); a weight keyed on `sampleSizeM` was considered and rejected as a span threshold with a smooth face on it. **(d) `MapContrast.ts`, the number that can fail a blank picture**, measured off the FINAL RGB bytes rather than the heights, because a shading bug, a palette bug and a flat world all produce the same blank rectangle and the player cannot tell them apart either. `lumaSd` / `lumaP5` / `lumaP95` / `lumaSpread` / `lumaStep` / `buckets`. **MEASURED, one static build against another differing ONLY in the shading, identical world data in every frame** (relief and stepM bit-identical, painted == onBody in all six): surface at +1.4 km **1.914 to 4.193 lumaStep** (+119%), regional **9.698 to 11.555** (+19%), orbital **26.139 to 26.733** (+2.3%). **Nothing regressed anywhere.** ABI bump coherent across the bridge (standing rule 9): `of_core_api.cpp` 14, `expected.json` 14 as a deliberate ONE-LINE edit, `OF_ABI_VERSION` 14, `discabi.ts` signature, the rebuilt `dist` reporting `abi=14` at handshake, and the `edits` parameter proven LIVE because passing 0 against the real handle changes the output. Parity 126/0 self, **108/108 cross-toolchain exact**. | Inherited uncommitted from a lane that was killed mid-flight and **finished rather than reverted**, on the evidence above: the ABI change is a real defect fix and not a refinement, the two bands improve every zoom and regress none, and the reasoning was already anchored in real frames. What was missing was the last mile, which is WG-34. | **Accepted** (recovered lane; DW-37, DW-7, standing rules 1 and 9) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-34 | **THE CONTRAST ASSERTION, ITS TWO NEGATIVE CONTROLS, AND THE FINDING THAT THE PROBE WAS AIMED AT THE ONE PIECE OF GROUND GUARANTEED TO BE FLAT.** **(a) `mapshot.js`'s surface shot framed the START PAD.** Its anchor is the ore cluster beside the spawn, and `BodyParams.homeFlatRadiusM` is 150 m of DEAD-LEVEL ground blended back to natural by 600 m, so a 454 m frame taken there is mostly an artificial apron. **Measured on the middle row of that frame: 34 of 58 samples sit at one height to within 0.1 m.** The map was correctly drawing a flat apron as a flat wash. That is half of what Reid saw, it is not a shading defect, and it is the instrument pointed at the wrong ground: the WG-28 pattern again. `mapshot.js` gains `offsetM`, defaulting to 0 so every existing invocation frames exactly what it framed before. **(b) RESOLUTION IS NOT THE LEVER, and that was measured rather than assumed.** The natural field has no content below roughly 60 m (WG-24) while a 454 m frame samples every 9.45 m, so raising `TERRAIN_N` puts adjacent samples closer together on a band-limited field and **lowers** every contrast number while costing 5x to 10x: at one site n=48 scores 4.224 lumaStep at 9.27 ms a rebuild, n=96 scores 2.373 at 49.1 ms, n=144 scores 1.662 at 94.0 ms. `TERRAIN_N` stays 48. The corollary is that **`lumaStep` is resolution-dependent and every floor below is pinned to n=48**, which is written into the probe that uses them. **(c) `probes/mapcontrast.js`, a floor that fails a flat wash BY NUMBER.** It asserts over a LADDER of eight sites along one meridian out to 40 km, not one frame, because one frame is not a sample of anything and this world contains a frame that is SUPPOSED to be blank. Site 0 is the start pad and is kept deliberately: it is the frame Reid reported and the one the fix helps least, and a ladder that dropped its hardest case would be marking its own homework. Two floors, on the WORST site and on the MEDIAN site. **`lumaSd` and `lumaSpread` are deliberately NOT asserted on**, because they were measured and they do not work: a global spread cannot tell a relief map from a smooth gradient of the same range. **THE THRESHOLDS ARE DERIVED, NOT CHOSEN.** Each is the GEOMETRIC MEAN of the defect's score and the fix's score on that ladder, the point of maximum EQUAL log-margin: the fix clears the floor by exactly the factor the defect misses it by. Ladder, DW-37 one band: `2.251 3.355 1.863 4.609 2.599 4.983 1.533 0.853`. Ladder, two bands: `2.587 5.431 4.201 6.233 5.570 6.640 4.689 3.674`. Worst **0.853 to 2.587**, floor `sqrt(0.853 x 2.587) = 1.4855`, margin **1.742x each way**. Median **2.425 to 5.060**, floor `sqrt(2.425 x 5.060) = 3.5029`, margin **1.445x each way**. Two runs of the fixed build returned the eight numbers identically, so the floors are not straddling noise. **(d) THE NEGATIVE CONTROLS, which are the deliverable.** Reverting `relief()` to DW-37's single band takes the ladder to **min 0.853 / median 2.425 and both assertions go RED by name**; restoring takes it to min 2.587 / median 5.060 and both go GREEN. **(e) `mapwork.js` PASSED WITHOUT PROVING ANYTHING AND NOW CANNOT.** Its last check was `after.lumaStep >= before.lumaStep`: a frame-wide mean over 2,784 samples, asked to notice a pad that moves 4 of them. Measured on the run that passed, a level press of 315 dug and 119 filled cells plus 24 strikes carried lumaStep from 4.214 to 4.300, clearing by 0.086 on a number whose own noise is larger, and **any change of sign passes a `>=`**. A global statistic is the wrong unit for a local question. `MapContrast` now retains its per-sample tones (`lastLuma`, cleared wherever the painter reports ZERO_CONTRAST) and `__of.map('grid')` publishes them aligned sample for sample, so the probe reads the painter's OWN tones at exactly the samples whose height moved. Two more derived floors, each the geometric mean of the value at which the claim is EMPTY and the value measured: `toneMoved` empty at the frame's own mean adjacent step, `sqrt(4.214 x 28) = 10.8624`, cleared by 2.578x; `standOut` empty at 1.0, which is 'the work differs from its neighbours by as much as neighbours normally differ', `sqrt(1 x 9.07) = 3.0116`, cleared by 3.012x. `workContrast` is compared against the frame's own step rather than an absolute byte count, so the claim survives a change of palette, zoom or biome. **THE ACCEPTANCE BAR, MET AND LOOKED AT.** At a 129 m span the levelled pad and the dug trench move 52 of 2,784 samples, the worked sample stands **70 luma** from its neighbour against a natural mean step of **2.705**, a ratio of **25.88x**, and the pad reads as a bright disc inside a dark cut rim. **The ABI 13 negative control is the same work, the same site, the same zoom, and a featureless wash: 0 samples moved, 0 tone, five checks red by name.** Pictures: `docs/screenshots/WG34_{before,after}_{surface,regional,orbital}.png`, `WG34_after_mapwork_120.png` against `WG34_negctl_mapwork_120.png`, `WG34_{after,negctl}_contrast.png`. | Admin: an assertion without a negative control is not evidence, and a probe audit had just found one that passed BETTER when nothing was built. Both new assertions therefore ship with the reverted build measured beside the fixed one, and every floor carries its derivation in the file that uses it. | **Accepted** (DW-7, DW-37) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-35 | **THE BLUE BLOBS ARE NOT WATER, AND THE BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS WRONG. THEY ARE `Mtn_SnowPatch` ICE PROPS, AND THE ROOT CAUSE IS THAT SPAWN IS IN THE MOUNTAINS BIOME.** Reid: "idk what these blue blobs are but i assume water". The brief named `NodeKind::WaterPool` and its `col_WaterPool` proxy. **`NodeKind::WaterPool` places EXACTLY ZERO nodes anywhere in the browser**: `deposits.h` has no scatterer that ever chooses it, the only two kind lists in TS are `NodeField.plan()` (14 Trees) and `PATCH_KINDS` (iron, coal, copper, rock), `NodeArt.ART` has no entry for kind 5 so `NodeField.build` would silently drop one, and `water_pool.glb` ships on disk unreachable by any loader. **ISOLATION LADDER, three frames, same seed, same camera** (standing rule 7): default draws the blob at 19,281 props; `?props=0` REMOVES IT at 0 props; `?gameplay=0` KEEPS IT at 19,281 props with the trees and ore gone. So it is a prop. `props_mountains.glb` contains exactly one blue material, `OF_Ice` at linear (0.624, 0.7913, 0.8714), carried only by `Mtn_SnowPatch_LOD0/2`, contract dims **2.60 x 0.22 x 2.10 m**. **Measured off the frame by projecting the camera: the near blob spans about 2.5 m in depth and 1.75 m across, which is that asset and not a coincidence.** `Registry.ts` scatters it at 900/km^2 in biome index 5. **The world-gen finding underneath: the spawn's own site notes are stale by 1.7 km.** `Config.ts` documents the start as "a Hills valley floor at 2,963 m" and `spawn_probe.cpp` scored it DRY and explicitly "not a lake bed"; the live site reads **biome 5 (Mountains) at 4,667.79 m**. WG-24 to WG-27 rewrote the noise stack and nobody re-ran the spawn probe, so the home pad drifted uphill into the snow line. Snow patches at 4.7 km are CORRECT world generation; the defect is where the player starts. **NOT FIXED THIS PASS, and deliberately not**: the one-line fix is in `web/src/assets/Registry.ts`, which is the live vegetation lane's table, and the root fix is moving `homeDir`, which re-baselines the ground under five live lanes and every pad probe. Escalated to Admin with both options priced. | The instruction's premise was false and its intent was not. Reporting the false premise and delivering the intent where it does not collide is what the protocol is for, and DW-29a records a lane being right to flag rather than act silently. Worth recording HOW it was found, because reading could not settle it: three isolation frames and one projection measurement, against two plausible candidates that reading had produced (the ore-patch ground skin at 0x53687d, and Ocean-biome terrain at 0x14406e, which is dry blue-tinted ground you walk on). | **Accepted** (diagnosis; fix escalated, cross-lane) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-36 | **THE POND IS A BASIN CUT INTO `sampleDesignedHeight`, AND THE WATER LEVEL IS A SEPARATE PUBLISHED QUANTITY WITH ITS OWN BOUND (DW-26 applied to a third surface).** `BodyParams` gains `pondDir` / `pondRadiusM` / `pondDepthM` / `pondFreeboardM`; `biome.h` gains `pondBasinDropM` = `depth * (1 - smoothstep(0,1,dist/radius))`, and the old `sampleDesignedHeight` body is RENAMED `designedHeightNoPond` so the new one is `designedHeightNoPond - pondBasinDropM`. Because the basin lives in the ONE surface authority, the mesh, collision, the walker, voxel solidity, deposit snapping and build placement all see it with no special case. **Forge: pondDir a literal unit vector 55.000000 m from `homeDir` (DW-14 forbids runtime trig here and `test_water_field.cpp` pins both the unit length and the separation), 22 m basin, 4.0 m deep, 0.60 m freeboard.** Wholly inside the 150 m dead-flat pad, so the ground the basin is cut into is the pad's own bit-exact constant and the shoreline is a circle rather than a contour of the noise. **NEW HEADER `core/include/of/water_field.h`, and it is a separate file on purpose.** It publishes `levelM` (ONE scalar per body), `levelAt`, `depthAt`, `shorelineM`, `maxDepthM`, `submersionM`, `kNoWater`. There is no `surfaceHeight` in it and no water function on `surface_field.h`; a caller that wants the ground holds one, a caller that wants the water holds the other, and the separation is enforced by the type system rather than by discipline. **`levelM` reads `designedHeightNoPond`, never `sampleDesignedHeight`, and the circularity that would follow is a tested defect**: making it read the basined height puts the water 0.600 m BELOW its own floor and fails 6 tests / 8,651 checks. **`kNoWater` is a large NEGATIVE (-1e30) rather than NaN or 0**, so a caller who forgets the sentinel test reads "the water is unreachably far below you", which is the correct sense; NaN would silently take the dry branch and 0 is a legal height. **MEASURED**: pad 4667.788838 m, basin floor 4663.788838 m (cut exactly 4.000000), `levelM` 4667.188838 m, `shorelineM` **16.623148 m** solved by bisection from the profile and never stored, `maxDepthM` 3.400000 m, steepest grade **0.272727** against the analytic 1.5*d/r. **Bit-identity outside the radius is exact**: the basin term returns bitwise 0.0 at and beyond 22.0 m (last nonzero drop 21.9 m, first bitwise zero 22.0 m, 0 leaks), and `x - 0.0 == x`. The two `fieldWorldSig` memo fingerprints grew from 8 doubles to 13; a test asserts each of the three ground-affecting pond fields changes the key and that `pondFreeboardM` provably does NOT, because "forgot the field" and "excluded it deliberately" look identical in a hash. | Three answers to "where is the surface" is the exact ambiguity that cost this project the sinking deck and the sinking tunnel. DW-26's ruling is that when one authority must answer in two shapes you publish both shapes and test the bound between them; a third shape gets the same treatment or it becomes the sixth definition of the surface. Note also that the basin had to be terrain rather than decoration for a reason that is not aesthetic: a decal has no floor to stand on, no slope to wade down and no volume, so "see the bottom" and "depth increases as you wade in" are not achievable by any amount of shading. | **Accepted** (DW-26, DW-33, DW-14, standing rule 1) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-37 | **`water_field_tests`: 17 tests, 35,619 checks, and BOTH reversions verified by name.** Registered in `core/CMakeLists.txt`; full suite **34/34 ctest**. Asserts the basin (exact cut, bitwise identity outside the radius, monotone descent on 8 bearings, a TWO-SIDED grade bound so the test fails if anyone flattens the bowl back into a decal, Cinder untouched over 1000 directions), the water level (the DW-26 bound in both directions over 4,000 directions, freeboard exact at the rim, `kNoWater` on 512 rim directions and 512 beach directions, depth strictly increasing over 4,008 samples), and **the negative control that matters: `surfaceHeight` at the pond centre equals the basin FLOOR and differs from the water level by at least 3.0 m**, so if anyone ever makes a height function return the water level, that check goes red by name. **Standing rule 11, both reversions run in a scratch copy: `pondBasinDropM` returning 0.0 always fails 7 tests / 8,590 checks; `levelM` reading `sampleDesignedHeight` fails 6 tests / 8,651 checks.** Two existing `biome_tests` cases legitimately moved and were fixed at the property rather than at the threshold: the pad-flatness test now asserts the STRONGER exact statement that the set of directions differing from `padH` is EXACTLY the pond disc and the difference there is EXACTLY `pondBasinDropM`; and the pad-blend test switched its reading to `designedHeightNoPond`, which is precisely what the rename exists for, because three of its eight rays cross the pond and it was reporting the pad blend as broken because a different feature was working. **The suite contradicted three of my own header comments and all three were mine to fix**: the shoreline comment said 16.11 m and "a 32 m pond" from an arithmetic slip in a solve the code does correctly (16.623148 m, 33.2 m); the INVARIANT block used "inside the pond" and "inside the rim" interchangeably, which makes its left inequality FALSE over the whole dry beach annulus; and `submersionM`'s justification argued something the code does not do, since it never consults the ground and therefore reports positive submersion for a point below the beach surface (which is the water-table reading, and is now stated as that). | The header comments were wrong in the direction that reads as confident. A suite that PRINTS a derived quantity instead of asserting the number a comment claims is what caught all three, which is the general lesson: pin the property, print the value. | **Accepted** (standing rule 11) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-38 | **ABI 15 to 16, atomic across the bridge (standing rule 9).** `of_core_api.cpp` section 3b adds `of_water_no_value`, `of_water_level`, `of_water_level_at`, `of_water_depth_at`, `of_water_submersion`, `of_water_disc`. The EXPORT LIST is purely additive, but **the bump is load-bearing rather than cosmetic**: the basin changes `sampleDesignedHeight`, which is what `of_base_height` / `of_surface_height` / `of_surface_radius` / `of_solid_at` and every streamed chunk return, so an old wasm under this client would mesh a pond nothing collided with. Refusing to boot is the correct outcome. **What is deliberately NOT in the ABI: there is no `of_surface_water` and no water flag on any existing surface call**, so no caller can receive a water height while believing it asked for the ground. `of_water_no_value` hands JS the exact sentinel bits rather than letting the client transcribe -1e30. Rebuilt wasm 401,301 B, `sync-wasm` to `web/public/wasm/`, `expected.json` regenerated with **exactly one line changed (`abi` 15 to 16) and zero terrain values moved**. **That fixture is a NEGATIVE CONTROL for the pond, not a confirmation of it, and the distinction is now written down**: its eight sampled directions sit 370 km to 1,141 km from the pond, so it COULD NOT have moved, and a future pond change that does move it means the containment broke. Parity: TIER 0 self 126/0, **TIER A cross-toolchain exact 108/108**, TIER B 148/3 (the pre-existing documented `tan`/`asin` 1-ULP mingw-vs-musl delta, identical in count and identity to the ABI 15 baseline). Handshake quoted live from the running client: `[of] W0 handshake abi=16 ... worker instance agrees=true (mismatches 0)`, read from `_of_abi_version()` and not from a literal. **Found and fixed on the way: `web/wasm/test/enemyloop.mjs` pinned `=== 15` and broke the instant the shim moved, a file about enemies failing for a reason with nothing to do with enemies. It now reads the number from `expected.json`, so the pin says the true thing (the wasm on disk is the wasm the fixture came from) instead of rescheduling the same break for ABI 17.** | Standing rule 9's corollary, learned twice on 2026-07-26: the fix for a handshake refusal is never to flip the constant. The `enemyloop.mjs` case is the same lesson one level out, on a hard-coded number in a test rather than in a banner. | **Accepted** (standing rule 9) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-39 | **`WaterOracle` is a SEPARATE CLASS from `SurfaceOracle`, with no method name in common, and that is the whole design.** `SurfaceOracle.ts`'s first line is "THE surface answer"; bolting `waterHeight` onto it would have made water the sixth definition of the surface in all but name, which is exactly what DW-26 forbids. So there is no `waterHeight` on `SurfaceOracle` and no `surfaceRadius` on `WaterOracle`. `SurfaceOracle` CONSTRUCTS its `water` sibling (that is where the module handle and body already are) and gains nothing by it: there is still no way to get a water height out of a surface call. The debug API mirrors the split, `of.surface(dir)` and `of.water(dir)` and `of.submersion(point)`, so a probe cannot read one believing it asked for the other. `WaterDisc` publishes `shorelineM` AND `basinRadiusM` as different numbers, because between them is dry beach inside the bowl and a surface drawn to the wrong one is a disc of water climbing the bank. | The property that matters is not the ownership graph, it is that the two questions cannot be spelled the same way. | **Accepted** (DW-26) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-40 | **SWIMMING, AS ONE CONTINUOUS FUNCTION OF DEPTH WITH NO `isSwimming` THRESHOLD ANYWHERE.** New `web/src/player/Swim.ts`. Net radial acceleration is `-g * (1 - buoyancy * frac)` where `frac` is the submerged fraction of the capsule, so the float line is where `buoyancy * frac == 1`. At the shipped 1.35 that is **frac 0.7407, waterline 1.333 m up a 1.80 m capsule, leaving the 1.62 m eye 0.287 m clear**. A player who stops swimming BOBS UP TO THAT LINE from above it or below it, because it is a stable fixed point of the acceleration and not a state anything switched into: nothing has to decide when swimming starts and there is no frame where the answer flickers. **Wading is the SAME equation** below the float line, so the feet stay on the bed and the walker walks, just slower, with drag scaled by the same `frac`. Buoyancy is 1.35 and not a human's 1.02 for a stated reason: 1.02 floats with the mouth at the waterline and would put the camera 1.3 cm above the water. **One real change to the capsule tick, and it is a correction rather than a special case: a floor kills DOWNWARD motion, not upward.** Out of water the two are the same statement; in water they differ, and zeroing `vUp` on every landing tick would pin a swimmer to the bottom of the pond while the acceleration said otherwise. Held jump is SWIM UP rather than an impulse, because what it is for is climbing out at the bank. **"Am I in water" is answered in ONE place, `WaterOracle.submersionM(point)`, which takes a point and nothing else: no ground query, no capsule, no grounded flag.** `Swim.ts` never imports `SurfaceOracle`, so the code that swims has no way to name the ground. **THE DROWNING DECISION: NO, and the seam is left in and MEASURED rather than stubbed.** Three reasons, in order of weight: (1) buoyancy makes it nearly unreachable by accident, so the rule would fire on a player deliberately holding a dive, i.e. it punishes experimenting with a feature 55 m from spawn; (2) wrong genre pressure, the hazards here are logistics and a hostile planet, Factorio has no swimming and KSP lets a kerbal sit in the sea, and the nearest comparable that DOES drown you does it in an ocean you cross rather than a pond you wade into; (3) nothing to trade against it yet, no suit, nothing on the bed worth having, no deep water that is a real obstacle, so it would be cost with no decision attached, which is the test DW-30 sets. **`headUnderM` and `breathSecs` are computed and published every tick and consumed by nobody**, so reversing this is wiring a consumer to a number that is already correct and already probe-readable, not adding a system. | Reid asked for swimming and did not say whether depth drowns you. An unstated rule that kills the player is the kind of thing to decide explicitly and record, not to infer. | **Accepted** (DW-12, DW-30) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-41 | **`Capsule.ts` and `HeightfieldWalk.slopeGate` split out of `KinematicBody.ts`, which was at 399 of its 400-line cap.** The tunables table (with the measurement behind every constant) is not part of the solver that reads it, and `KinematicBody` re-exports `CAPSULE` so all six importers are unchanged. The slope rule had its SAMPLING delegated to `HeightfieldWalk.ts` and its DECISION inline, so that file owned half a rule; `slopeGate` now owns both halves and returns the uphill `cut` as a SCALAR rather than editing a vector argument, which keeps it allocation free on the tick path. Behaviour unchanged. **Water is deliberately not a slope exemption**: a swimmer is not grounded so it never runs on one, and a wader IS standing on the bed and should be refused by a bank too steep to climb exactly as on dry land. | The cap forced the question and the answer was one that improves the file regardless. Recording it because the alternative under time pressure is to shave a comment, and the comments in `Capsule.ts` are the measurements. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-42 | **`WaterSurface.ts` is the LEAST INTERESTING THING that can honestly be called a water surface, and that is the point.** World-gen owns the water's EXTENT and DEPTH; rendering owns its LOOK. What ships is only what comes out of the world data: the disc reaches the **shoreline** and not the basin rim, so dry beach is visible inside the bowl; it is a curved shell on the sphere rather than a plane; and per-vertex colour AND ALPHA are a function of the WATER DEPTH under each vertex, read from the oracle at build time, so it is translucent at the edge where the bottom is 13 cm down and dense in the middle where it is 3.4 m down. **That gradient is what makes it read as a body of water rather than a decal, and it is world-gen data, not a look.** It spends ZERO of DW-10's five custom shaders: a stock `MeshStandardMaterial` with a vec4 colour attribute. DoubleSide, so the surface is still there when the camera goes under it. Anchored per standing rule 6: float32 metres about the pond centre, engine transform re-derived from the f64 anchor on `OriginRebased`. Built inside `bootTerrain`, which cost the boot site exactly ONE line. | The brief's warning was that a water surface shipping as a flat blue plane would read as a bigger blob than the ones being removed. The answer is not to half-shade it in this lane; it is to ship the part that is genuinely world data, spend none of the shader budget, and hand rendering a clean list. See section 5 for that list. | **Accepted** (DW-10, DW-35, standing rule 6) | 2026-07-27 |
| WG-43 | **`probes/pondwade.js`: 16 checks, all green, driven on FOUR bearings with a negative control and a dive. R8 enforced.** A pond has a CIRCULAR shoreline, so every bearing is different ground and a single hand-placed wade would be the flat-clearing mistake for the third time. The probe drives the full crossing on 4 bearings and asserts on the WORST of them. **Geometry**: basin cut **4.000000 m**, worst uphill step walking inward **0** (bitwise), worst deviation outside the basin **0** (bitwise, the leak test), waterline measured independently by bisection on the sampled field at **16.623 m on all four bearings, agreeing with /core's derived value to 4.8e-9 m**. Depth ladder from the centre: 3.400 / 3.307 / 3.051 / 2.670 / 2.198 / 1.672 / 1.128 / 0.602 / 0.130 / 0 at 0 to 18 m. **Driven, per bearing**: 1,946 to 2,009 ticks, 125 to 130 m travelled, about 880 wet ticks, **300 to 338 FLOATING ticks**, passes within **0.005 m** of the centre, lands dry and grounded 96 to 101 m out the far side. **Sank below the bed: 0.000000 m on all four.** Eye clear while floating: **0.285 m**, against the 0.287 m the buoyancy constant predicts. **YAW IS CALIBRATED, NOT ASSUMED**: the probe walks briefly at two known yaws, reads the headings back off the actual displacement, and solves for scale and offset, which costs 1.2 s and removes the whole "the probe was aimed at the wrong ground" class that cost WG-33 a pass. **Swim speed is a CONVERGENCE, not a threshold**: the peak while floating is 2.448 m/s (momentum carried in off the bank, which drag is supposed to bleed rather than clamp), and the assertion is on the mean of the last quarter of the float ticks, **2.145 to 2.148 m/s against the 2.2 cap**. Asserting the peak would fail a correct implementation and pass one that clamped and then crept back. **THE DIVE, added because the wade never once put the camera under the water and "a water surface the camera crosses correctly" would have shipped on an assumption**: dropped in from above the middle, the eye goes **1.361 m** under, `breathSecs` reaches 2.083 s, and the capsule **surfaces unaided at tick 85 with no input at all**, ending floating at frac **0.741**, which is 1/1.35 to four figures. **NEGATIVE CONTROL**: the same driven walk 300 m away on the flat pad, 401 ticks, 30.6 m travelled, **0 wet ticks, 0 float ticks, maxFrac 0**. Without it a swim state that was simply always on would pass every assertion above. Pictures: `docs/screenshots/WG35_blue_yaw0.png` and `WG35_iso_{noprops,nogameplay}.png` before, `WG36_after_{shore,wide,swimming,aerial}.png` after. | The two checks that were RED on the first run were both the probe's fault and both were fixed at the property rather than the threshold: an 18 s drive was not long enough to cross and exit (so "got out" was measuring a player still standing in the shallows), and a peak-speed threshold was reading momentum as a cap failure. | **Accepted** (R8, DW-20, standing rule 11) | 2026-07-27 |

| WG-50 | **THE PER-CHUNK NOISE PHASE IS THE RIGHT SHAPE, AND IT IS NOT A CHUNK-FORMAT CHANGE. Confirmed on my own arithmetic, refuted on ownership and on cost.** Rendering's diagnosis is correct in every particular I could check. `pM` is formed at `TerrainShader.ts:185` as `(vWorld - uBodyCenter) * uMetresPerUnit`, and `uBodyCenter` is `-origin` uploaded as a GL `vec3`, so it arrives quantised at planet scale and `pM` inherits that quantum whatever `vWorld` does. **My measurement of the quantum is 0.03125 m, not 0.0625 m, and rendering's figure is the worst case rather than the typical one**: 0.0625 m is right for a component in [2^19, 2^20), but a player at a generic point on a 600 km sphere has components near 3e5 to 4e5, which sit in [2^18, 2^19) and quantise at 2^-5. The conclusion is unchanged and the ratio still bites: at the 1280x720 RN-45 camera the ground pixel is 3.56 mm at 2 m and 22.27 mm at 5 m, so the quantum is **8.77x the pixel footprint at 2 m, 3.90x at 3 m, 1.40x at 5 m and 0.55x at 8 m**, which is why the artefact is a near-field band that dies out around 6 m to 8 m rather than fading smoothly. **WHAT I REFUTE: "that is a chunk-format change, which is why it is yours."** It touches no byte of the 28 B/vertex packed layout, no `of_packed_*` export, no ABI and no parity fixture. The quantity is a pure function of `ChunkView.anchor`, which is already float64 on the main thread end to end (`ChunkView.ts:21,70` from `TerrainProtocol.ts:91-92` from `HEAPF64` from `centerUniverse.pos`, which is C++ `double`), the only narrowing happening later and only for the engine transform. **And there is a shipped precedent for exactly this shape that is not in the wire format either: `aFadeT0`** (`ChunkBatch.ts:50,122-129`), a per-chunk float allocated JS-side and `fill()`ed across the slot's vertex window, with no worker change and no `/core` change. **The refusal matters because the requested shape is the ONE version of this that endangers standing rule 4.** Putting the phase in the chunk format means changing `of_packed_*`, bumping the ABI under standing rule 9, moving the parity bytes and re-baselining 108/108 cross-toolchain. In the shape I am publishing, determinism is not protected by care, it is unreachable: the phase reads an anchor generation has already produced and produces a shading quantity generation never reads back. Terrain height, biome, solidity, deposits and the save are untouched by construction. | The brief asked me to satisfy myself on three things and all three came back clean, but only one of them the way I expected. The phase reduction IS exact (`cell + frac === anchor / L` bit-for-bit in float64 on every axis and octave). Determinism DOES survive, and the reason is stronger than "we were careful": the quantity is on the wrong side of the generation boundary to affect it. Chunk boundaries do NOT become visible, and that is the one I had to measure rather than argue. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-51 | **THE DEFECT REPRODUCED FROM ARITHMETIC ALONE, WITH NO SHADER, NO GPU AND NO THREE.JS: `web/tools/precision/artphase.ts`.** RN-45 was caught by a screenshot after every metric passed, so before spending anything on the cure I reproduced the disease independently rather than restating rendering's measurement. The tool renders one field three ways at the RN-45 camera (1280x720, fov 60, eye 1.8 m), taking screen derivatives on **2x2 quads exactly as hardware takes them**, which is the mechanism that turns a quantised position into runs of identical pixels. **It reproduces: `WG51_artphase_defect.png` has the concentric arcs centred on the eye and the quantisation lattice, and `WG51_artphase_{truth,fix}.png` are smooth and visually identical to each other.** **THE NUMBER RN-45 DID NOT HAVE: the fraction of quads whose screen derivative is EXACTLY 0.0 in both axes. truth 0.00%, defect 32.49% of 212,480 quads, fix 0.00%.** A histogram cannot see that and a band mean cannot see that; it is one cheap integer and it separates the defect from truth by two orders of magnitude.
