Field agreement against the ideal, which is the half the quad count cannot tell you (a mode can have a live derivative of the WRONG field): **defect max 1.341e-2 / mean 7.603e-4, fix max 2.041e-6 / mean 2.686e-7, a factor of 6,570 on the max.** **I GOT THE INSTRUMENT WRONG TWICE FIRST AND BOTH ARE RECORDED IN THE TOOL, because both are this project's own recurring failure.** (a) The first version put the ground patch at z = R with x and y small, and **the defect did not reproduce at all**: 0.00% dead quads, identical to truth. In that orientation two of three components of `pM` are small and therefore exact, so the quantisation had nowhere to land. A control that accidentally removes the thing it controls for reports success. Fixed by standing at a generic direction on the sphere, which is where a player is everywhere except on three great circles. (b) The second version reported truth and defect agreeing on **69,033 of 212,480 quads, to the digit**, because the truth path was reading the already-quantised position. Two numbers agreeing to the last digit is a wiring diagnosis, never a coincidence, and RN-45 had just published that same lesson about its own noise floor. (c) A third: truth ran the hash in float64 while the others ran it in float32, and truth and fix then drew visibly DIFFERENT terrain. That was not a precision result, it was a control changing two things at once: `hash13` is a chain of `fract` and is chaotic across precisions, so float64 gives an unrelated value for the same cell rather than a nearby one. All three modes now evaluate the identical noise function and differ ONLY in how the lattice coordinate is obtained, which is the one variable under test. | The shading gain is a declared contrast stretch applied identically to all modes and it is labelled in the tool, because at the shipped bump strength the effect is a few counts, which is exactly why RN-45's band statistics could not see it. A picture honest about amplitude would be a picture of nothing; a picture that hides its stretch is worse. | **Accepted** (DW-7, DW-20, standing rule 11) | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-52 | **THE SEAM PROOFS, THE LOD PROOF AND THE HEADROOM BOUND, AS ASSERTIONS THAT FAIL BY NAME: `web/tools/precision/phasecheck.ts`, 57/57.** Standing rule 11's corollary is that a probe reporting a number without asserting anything is a log line. Everything below is checked against **one bar, the 3.56 mm ground pixel at 2 m**, because an error under a pixel cannot become a visible artefact and any other threshold would be tuned. **Exactness**: `cell + frac === anchor / L` exactly in float64 on all 12 axis-octave pairs; `frac` in [0,1); `cell` exact in float32 with 189x to 10,131x of headroom under 2^24. **Reconstruction error over a whole depth-14 chunk: 3.3 to 12.8 micrometres, i.e. 1/279 to 1/1065 of a pixel.** **The positive control, on the same instrument: today's `pM` arithmetic reads 45.19 mm, i.e. 12.7 pixels**, so the test that passes the fix fails the shipped code by name rather than merely being satisfied by it. **Seam, neighbouring chunks at depth 14 over 2,048 samples straddling the shared plane: 2.8 to 11.1 micrometres.** **LOD cross-dissolve (DW-23 draws the outgoing parent and the incoming children in the SAME frame, so different anchors at different depths over the same ground are on screen together, and a per-chunk scheme that is merely self-consistent would shimmer): 8.0 to 11.3 micrometres.** **AND THE CONTROL THAT EXCUSED A CASE IT NEVER MET, caught in my own suite**: sections 3 and 4 both reported **0 cell-index splits over 8,192 samples**, so my claim that a split is harmless because the interpolant is C1 was reasoning and not measurement. Section 4b now lands samples on lattice planes to the ULP on purpose, **provokes 154 real splits, and asserts the reconstructed coordinate still agrees: worst 177.383 micrometres, 1/20 of a pixel.** | The bound is stated in metres of ground rather than in lattice cells throughout, because the only comparison that decides whether a screen derivative survives is against a pixel footprint, and a number in cells invites a threshold argument. | **Accepted** (standing rule 11) | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-53 | **EVERY OCTAVE THAT FEEDS THE DERIVATIVE MUST BE REDUCED, AND MY OWN HEADLINE STATISTIC FAILS TO SAY SO.** The payload size turns on whether the finest octave alone is enough, and the fine octave carries roughly 5.7x the gradient of the next one down, so it was a fair question. A fourth mode, `finefix`, reduces only the 4.2 m octave and leaves the macro ladder on `pM`. **It scores 0.00% dead quads, identical to truth and to the full fix**, because the fine octave's live derivative keeps the SUM off zero everywhere. **And `WG51_artphase_finefix.png` still has the arcs and the lattice plainly in it**, with field agreement 2.498e-3 against the full fix's 2.041e-6, a factor of **1,224**. So the dead-quad count I am handing rendering is **necessary and not sufficient**, the field agreement is the number that separates a partial fix from a real one, and the picture remains the arbiter. This is RN-45's lesson arriving from the other direction: it is not that numbers cannot see this defect, it is that each number sees only part of it, so a probe wanting to police it needs the pair. **Consequence, which is the deliverable: 6 floats per octave per chunk, 4 octaves, 24 floats.** | Recorded as a decision rather than a note because the tempting cheap version is the one that passes the test. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-54 | **THE DELIVERY MECHANISM IS RENDERING'S TO PICK, AND ONE OF THE TWO CANDIDATES IS DISQUALIFIED BY ARITHMETIC.** Terrain chunks are not meshes: the whole resident set is TWO `BatchedMesh`es (`ChunkBatch.ts:59`, `ChunkGeometryPool.ts:30-31,48-49`) sharing one `ShaderMaterial` whose own header states that per-chunk state is zero (`TerrainMaterial.ts:1-8`), with no `onBeforeRender` anywhere in the terrain path. **So a per-chunk uniform is impossible without giving back DW-11's draw-call collapse**, and that is worth saying plainly because "just pass a uniform" is the obvious suggestion. Two mechanisms remain and they are not equivalent. **(a) A vertex attribute `fill()`ed per slot, the `aFadeT0` pattern: 24 floats x 1,217 verts x about 320 near slots = roughly 37 MB, against a measured 93.7 MB frame VRAM. That is a 40% increase for six numbers a chunk and it is refused.** **(b) A `DataTexture` indexed by three's own batching id, the `MachineBatch.ts:131` pattern (`getIndirectIndex(gl_DrawID)`), already proven on r185 in this codebase: 24 floats x 320 slots = about 31 KB.** Three orders of magnitude apart, same information. The terrain vertex shader already includes `<batching_pars_vertex>` and `<batching_vertex>` (`TerrainShader.ts:30,46`), so the id is available there for free. **World-gen supplies the numbers, rendering owns the channel.** Re-stamp points: wherever the anchor changes, which is `ChunkView.refresh()` and the near/far slot swap, and NOT the rebase path, since the anchor is invariant under a rebase, which is the whole reason the field was keyed on `pM` in the first place. | I am naming (a) as refused rather than leaving both open, because a 40% VRAM increase discovered after the shader work is done is a wasted lane, and the arithmetic to rule it out is one multiplication. | **Accepted** (DW-11, DW-28) | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-55 | **THE SPAWN IS PREPARED AND NOT MOVED, and the survey found two defects in the instrument before it found a site.** Admin's item 2, deliberately stopping at a shortlist. **(1) `spawn_probe.cpp` COULD NEVER HAVE PRODUCED THE CURRENT SITE**: it hard-filters to Plains, Forest and Hills (`spawn_probe.cpp:134-136,425`), and the spawn is Mountains. It was never a probe output. Re-run at lat 2 / lon 144 it ranks the site **last of 21, score -85.84 against the winner's +32.81**, with 23.88% slope at 20 m (20x to 30x every candidate) and a 0.976 m worst step under a 4 m DW-32 module. It is also not the valley floor `Config.ts` claims: it sits **+130.7 m ABOVE the 6 km mean**, which is a tabletop, the exact failure the probe's own header warns against. **(2) `probePadHere` PREDATES WG-36's POND AND ITS PAD VERDICT IS WRONG BY 1.08 m.** It moves `homeDir` to the candidate (`spawn_probe.cpp:487-491`) and never moves `pondDir` or zeroes `pondRadiusM`, then measures with `sampleDesignedHeight`, which since WG-36 subtracts the basin. Its reported "worst 4 m step inside the flat radius 1.078601 m" at the current site is **entirely my pond**: inside the flat radius `designedHeightNoPond` is a bit-exact constant, so the basin is the only varying term, and the analytic value for a 4 m span across the basin's steepest point is 1.080 m. Candidate rows read 0.000000 m only because the pond is thousands of km away from them. Related: `printHomeDirLiteral` emits `homeDir` alone, so acting on its output as printed would orphan the pond and break `test_water_field.cpp:138-148`. **(3) THERE IS NO DESERT BIOME.** The set is Ocean / Beach / Plains / Forest / Hills / Mountains / Polar (`biome.h:53-67`). What Reid liked is partly the post grade (`PostConfig.ts:231`, `CompositeGlsl.ts:86-89`) and partly **Beach**, `0xc8b48a`, which with Mountains is one of only two biomes taking DRY_DETAIL props rather than grass (`Registry.ts:125-133`). Beach is invisible to the probe because `minAlt = 200.0` (`spawn_probe.cpp:544`) cuts the whole band. **(4) THE SNOW LINE IS THE MOUNTAINS BIOME BOUNDARY, not an elevation**: `Mtn_SnowPatch` is keyed on biome index with no elevation gate (`Registry.ts:143-144`), so the prop snow line is `kMountainsRel` 0.330 x 6000 m of raw relief, about 2,700 m designed. The shader's own snowfield is a separate thing at 5,160 m to 6,840 m (`TerrainShader.ts:263-265`, duplicated in `BiomePalette.ts:54-55`). The current spawn is **1,239 m above the prop line and 492 m below the shader one**, which is precisely why WG-35's blobs were props and not shader snow. **Shortlist in section 6.1. `homeDir` moves under 18 call sites** and I have the list; two ctests pin the current literal by name and will fail on any move, which is correct and is the reason this waits for a quiet checkout. | Not moved, as briefed. Reid picks the biome. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-56 | **THERE IS ALREADY A WELL-CONDITIONED LOCAL COORDINATE ON THE GPU, IT IS PAID FOR, AND THE TERRAIN SHADER USES IT ZERO TIMES.** Admin asked me to say plainly if the right answer were a different precision split or "not worth the chunk-format churn", so here is the third option, found while confirming the second. **Every chunk already carries a per-quad normalised UV**: `of_core_api.cpp:1445` documents it as `uv = v/65535, over the quad`, it is in the wire format's five sections (`ChunkFormat.ts`), it is in the batch's attribute list (`ChunkBatch.ts:40,47`) and it is written per slot (`:111`). **`TerrainShader.ts` contains the string `uv` exactly 0 times.** So it is uploaded, held in VRAM and interpolated every frame for nothing. **And it is well conditioned where `pM` is not, by construction, because it is local**: at a depth-14 chunk of 57.856 m one uint16 step is **0.883 mm, which is 0.248 of the 3.56 mm ground pixel at 2 m** (0.496 at depth 13), against `pM`'s 8.77 PIXELS. A tiling detail normal or albedo map sampled on `uv * N` therefore has a clean screen derivative with **no phase, no new attribute, no DataTexture, no chunk-format change and no CPU work at all**, and it is the ordinary way every other engine does this. Seams work out: `N = chunkEdge / tileM` is an integer at every depth if the base is chosen once, so neighbours at one LOD meet at a tile boundary. **The honest cost, and it is a real one: the detail's WORLD size doubles at each LOD step, because the UV is per quad rather than per metre.** That is tolerable here specifically because the bump is only well conditioned within about 20 m to 45 m of the eye by RN-45's own bound, and inside that band the player is at max depth, so the step happens outside the band. It also tiles, which is what the shipped macro colour field is already good at breaking up. **I am NOT recommending this INSTEAD of the phase, and the distinction matters.** The phase is the general fix: it repairs `pM` for the strata warp and for any future field that takes a derivative, it costs about 31 KB and 24 CPU ops a chunk, and it is now proven and asserted. The UV route is narrower, cheaper still, and may make the DETAIL BUMP SPECIFICALLY unnecessary. **Rendering should measure both before writing either**, and the reason I can hand them that choice at all is that neither is a chunk-format change. | Recorded because "the channel you need is already on the GPU and nothing reads it" is exactly the kind of fact that gets rediscovered expensively two lanes later. Note also the smell it represents: an attribute uploaded every frame that no shader samples is either a missing feature or dead weight, and nobody had asked which. **UNMEASURED, and flagged as such**: I did not build it, and it carries a DW-5 asset question the art lane owns. | **Proposed** | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-57 | **THE POND NOW MOVES WITH THE PAD, AND THE PAD METRIC MEASURES THE PAD AGAIN. `spawn_probe.cpp`, and it is the fix that makes the eventual spawn move SAFE rather than merely tidy.** Two defects, one cause: the probe had no concept of the pond at all, the word did not appear in the file. **(1) `probePadHere` moved `homeDir` to a candidate and left `pondDir` and `pondRadiusM` where they were**, then measured with `sampleDesignedHeight`, which since WG-36 subtracts the basin. So it charged my own pond to the candidate's terrain. **The proof is that the number is a CONSTANT: 1.078601 m now reads identically at sites of 436 m, 293 m, 1,643 m and 4,667 m elevation on wildly different ground, and a constant cannot be a property of terrain.** It matches the analytic 4 m span across the basin's steepest point, 1.080 m. It previously read 1.078601 m at the incumbent and 0.000000 m at every candidate purely because the pond was thousands of km from them, which is exactly why it looked like a site property. **Both numbers are now measured and BOTH printed** (DW-26: when one authority must answer in two shapes, publish both shapes): `worst 4 m step, pad alone` reads **0.000000 m at every site**, which is what the pad is FOR and was unreadable under a 4 m bowl, and `with the pond cut in` reads 1.078601 m, with the difference named as the basin. **(2) `printHomeDirLiteral` emitted `homeDir` ALONE**, so pasting its output moved the pad and left the pond at the old spawn, still referencing a water level for a pad that was no longer there, in whatever biome sits 55 m from a site nobody chose. It emits both literals now, with the separation printed against the tolerance the test pins. **THE CONSTRUCTION WAS IDENTIFIED BY BIT-IDENTITY, NOT BY TOLERANCE, AND THAT DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT.** `pondDir` is `homeDir` rotated 55 m along a heading 30 deg east of north, and I tried the obvious `offsetDir` tangent-offset-then-normalise first: it lands **154 nm** from the shipped literal with the separation at 54.999999827 m. A TRUE GREAT-CIRCLE ROTATION reproduces the shipped `pondDir` **BIT-FOR-BIT on all three components** at 54.999999981 m. **Both pass `test_water_field.cpp`'s `|sep - 55| < 1e-6`, so a tolerance check would have accepted the wrong one and quietly moved the pond 154 nm every time anyone regenerated the literal.** `pondDirSelfCheck` now reconstructs the shipped `pondDir` from the shipped `homeDir` on every run, prints BIT-IDENTICAL or a loud MISMATCH, and sets the exit code. The round trip is complete: run over the current spawn the tool re-emits both shipped literals byte for byte. | The candidates' honest defect is now visible too, and it is sharper than the score: at the current spawn **the pad ADDS 18.00 percentage points of grade** over the natural ground at a 59.70% blend annulus, while all three sampled candidates read NEGATIVE (-1.95, -0.12, -0.83), meaning the pad smooths ground that was already gentle. The incumbent is the only site where the pad makes the terrain worse, which is the cut-shelf failure the function was written to detect and could not report while the basin was drowning its other number. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-116 | **THERE ARE NO SCENERY TREES: every tree in the world is a streamed harvest node, and the canopy tier is RETIRED TO A DEFAULT rather than deleted** | Reid's ruling, and the retirement's shape is the measurable half: `?canopy=620` reproduces the WG-59 world in the SAME binary, so what the ruling cost and what it bought are one query parameter apart instead of two builds with two streamed chunk sets and two shadow states. The tier keys on `distance / scale`, not distance, because WG-119 makes a tree's size mean something and one absolute distance would either pop the big ones or pay LOD0 for the small ones. `geomAt` walks DOWN to a present tier, so the three assets that author only LOD0 and LOD1 behave exactly as before rather than vanishing at 165 m | **Accepted** | 2026-08-01 |

| WG-120 | **A streamed-out cell drops its UNTOUCHED trees from `known`**, and only a chopped one is kept for ever | `RockField` keeps everything it ever materialised, which is right at 49 rocks over a 5 km walk and unbounded at 2,226 trees per ring: the map and every autosave would grow with the walk. It is kept for the one case the census cannot reach, that the slope is sampled through `editsHandle` and a dug pit or levelled terrace makes steep ground under a live ring; that case is UNTESTED and owed. Lowering the constant until the counter moved was rejected: it would refuse trees on ground that is fine for trees, to flatter an instrument | **Accepted** | 2026-08-01 |

Stating bareness twice is the DW-26 failure in miniature, and it was also an instrument failure: with no Mountains density, `canopyBareCells` read 0 at all seven sites because the term's refusing case was unreachable | **Accepted** (WG-61) | 2026-07-28 |

And a cell's world position is a float64 anchor plus a float32 chunk-local offset, so the same ground arriving at two LOD depths differs in the last bits: through a smooth field that is about 1e-9 of weight, through a hashed tile boundary it is a coin flip and the stands would reshuffle every time a chunk changed depth. Same class as WG-51, and the cheap answer is to make the function continuous | **Accepted** (WG-62) | 2026-07-28 |
| WG-17 | **`CANOPY_LOD2_M` is pinned EQUAL to `DETAIL_RADIUS_M` (78 m)**, and the canopy adds no rebuild band | Measured, not chosen. | WG-18 | **Anything that caches a chunk's ENGINE position re-derives it from `TerrainStream.afterRebase`, not from a second `OriginRebased` subscription** | A subscriber that re-composes transforms from `ChunkView.pos` is correct only if it runs AFTER the streamer's own re-place loop, and `Events` delivers to a `Set` in insertion order, so a second subscription makes correctness a function of which file `Boot` constructs first. That is a dependency nobody would preserve across a refactor. The hook makes "after the views are re-placed" a property of the code. The defect it repairs (`Scatter.replace` documented as THE rebase path and never called, measured at 4,000.089191 m of displacement across 43 of 43 chunks) was itself an ordering-free omission that survived for months | **Accepted** (WG-65) | 2026-07-28 |

| WG-141 | **The moon height field is a NINE-RUNG CRATER LADDER, not a single crater layer** (`cubed_sphere.h::sampleHeightFieldMoon`). Frequencies 9 / 27 / 81 / 243 full-neighbourhood, then 270 / 810 / 2430 / 7290 / 21870 confined; amplitudes `14800/f` and `6080/f`. Plus a 4-octave base, a mare mask gated into the basins, and an 833-frequency regolith roll. | The old stack was the pre-WG-25 original: finest content 1,111 m, so nothing on the moon was smaller than a kilometre and a player could not walk into a crater. Craters are scale-free in two laws at once (population ~D^-2, depth/diameter ~0.12); tripling frequency while thirding amplitude reproduces BOTH in one construction, so the ladder is self-similar by design and every rung has the same wall grade. Amplitudes are derived from the ratio, not tuned. **Deliberately NOT LOD-gated**, so the field stays a pure function of (seed, dir) and both bitwise crack-free proofs survive untouched. Cost 2.21x (492,507 vs 1,086,656 verts/sec), which the confined variant is what makes affordable. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-142 | **`craterField`'s 3x3x3 neighbourhood was insufficient for its own radius; the radius is capped at 0.625 so reach is exactly 1.0 cell.** | A crater reaches `cr * kCraterReach` cells from a centre jittered anywhere in its cell, so the old 0.75 maximum reached 1.2 cells and a crater two cells away could be clipped part way down its rim. Not theoretical: **346 misses in 160,000 samples, worst clipped profile 0.1906, a 470 m step out of nothing.** 3x3x3 is sufficient iff reach <= 1.0, which the cap makes exact. Moves moon bits deliberately. | **Accepted** (defect fix) | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-143 | **The crater profile is continuous: `craterProfile(t)` is one shared function for both crater fields.** Bowl `-1 + 1.5t^2` to a crest of +0.5 at t == 1, then a SQUARED fade to zero slope and zero value at t == 1.6. | The spike profile was `-(1-t*t)` inside and `(1-rim)*0.5` outside: 0 approaching t == 1 from below, 0.5 AT t == 1. **A step of half the layer amplitude at every crater rim on every body with craters**, which on the old moon was a 1,400 m vertical wall and is why a 2 m-spacing sample on a crater floor could report a 1,589 m step. Sharing one function means the confined variant cannot drift from the full one. The squared outer fade removes a circular crease a linear fade leaves in the derivative. | **Accepted** (defect fix) | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-144 | **The moon's design layer uses a CONTINUOUS gain, `designedGainForMoonRelief(rel)`; `designedReliefFactor` is retired as a shaping input on both bodies.** | WG-25 fixed exactly this on the planet and the moon was never revisited, which makes it the THIRD thing about Cinder in that category. Switching gain on the discrete biome put a **120 m wall at the 1.00->1.15 highland threshold and a 40 m one at the 1.00->1.10 crater-floor threshold**, following contour lines around the body and showing up as gaps in the biome histogram no sample could land in. Measured after: worst step at a biome crossing 173.8 m against the retired form's 287.5 m. The BIOME stays discrete, because it is a material id and should be; only the height is made continuous. | **Accepted** (defect fix) | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-145 | **`makeCinder.maxReliefM` 4000 -> 5000, and the moon biome bands re-tuned to +/-0.070.** | The two are ONE decision and separating them is how I got it wrong first. `maxReliefM` is both the declared relief bound AND the denominator `biomeAtMoonH` expresses its bands in, so raising it for headroom widened every band at once and the split went to Regolith 89.9% / MoonHighland 2.0% / CraterFloor 8.1%. A biome carrying 2% of a body will essentially never be drawn. 5000 covers the worst of five seeds (-4565 to +3985) with margin while keeping the bands tight; the split is now **53.1% / 31.7% / 15.2%**. `moon_biomes_are_all_populated` and `moon_relief_bound_holds_across_seeds` pin both halves so the next person to move one has to look at the other. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-146 | **CURVATURE, not slope, is the instrument for "does this terrain have features".** | This nearly gave me a confident wrong answer in both directions. A crater wall is a continuous 24% to 48% grade at EVERY sampling scale, so the old moon reports a perfectly healthy 0.554 m median step over a 4 m baseline and the rewrite reads as a **1.3x tweak**. It was never a plane; it was tilted without being featured. The second difference `h(a-d) - 2h(a) + h(a+d)` is zero for a straight slope of any steepness and of order its own depth for a feature of size d: on that instrument the same two fields separate by **33x at 4 m and 18x at 40 m**. The MEDIAN is the gate, not the mean, because curvature over a crater field is heavy-tailed and a mean would pass on a handful of rims. | **Accepted** (method) | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-147 | **Body plurality in the client: one `BodyId`, one `createBodyHandle`, and `?body=cinder`.** `PlanetBody.ts` owns the rule; `TerrainInitMsg` and `OracleProbeRequest` each carry a `bodyId`; the two workers create their own handle from it. **No ABI change.** | The client assumed one body in exactly three places and every other call site already threaded a handle, so this is plurality rather than un-hardcoding. Putting the rule in `PlanetBody.ts` keeps it next to the file that already calls itself "the one body-constant reader (D-006)", and after the change `_of_body_create_*` appears at exactly two call sites in `web/src`. Workers build their own handle because heaps are never shared (DW-16). **Taking no ABI number was deliberate: physics held 22 uncommitted throughout.** | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-149 | **The cost meter counts crater cell evaluations as a SECOND unit, and refuses to present the valueNoise figure as the cost of a cratered body** (`OF_COUNT_CRATER_CELL`, `craterCells()`, `terrain_probe` §5). Closes R18. | The crater fields hash lattice cells directly and never call `valueNoise`, so `noiseCalls()` was blind to the dominant term. Measured on Cinder: **10 valueNoise calls against 113 crater cell evaluations per sample**, i.e. the meter was reporting about 8% of the work while looking authoritative. Admin's ruling: a cost meter blind to the most expensive thing on the body is worse than no meter. The two counts are deliberately **not summed**, because a cell evaluation and a valueNoise call are different units and the conversion is a measured property rather than a constant to hard-code; instead the probe prints both and, when the crater count is non-zero, states plainly that verts/sec is the cost authority for that body. Forge reports 0 crater cells and no note, so the refusal is body-specific rather than a blanket disclaimer. Still compiled entirely out unless `OF_NOISE_COUNT=1`. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| WG-148 | **The airless look is ROUTED TO RENDERING, not built here, and it is not a polish item.** | Stated rather than left to be discovered, per the brief. The evidence is a controlled pair at one camera: with `forgeAtmosphere()` the moon from 12 km is a white fog bank with no visible craters; with `--atmos=0` it is a black sky, stars, a hard terminator and four scales of overlapping crater. Aerial perspective was erasing the entire rewrite. Detail and the second finding (ambient collapse) in §5. | **Accepted** (routed) | 2026-08-02 |

| WG-200 | **RUINS ARE A PLACEABLE TYPE, AND THE TYPE IS `core/include/of/poi.h`.** `SiteKind` / `SiteSpec` / `FSite` / `SiteCatalog`, plus `core/tests/test_poi.cpp` (18 tests, 92 checks) and `core/tools/site_probe.cpp`. There is no `theRuin()` anywhere: a site is a row in `siteSpecsFor(bodyId)` and Forge's table has exactly one row today. **BOTH placement modes exist and are tested from day one**, `Anchor::Home` (golden-angle spiral in an arc band about `body.homeDir`) and `Anchor::Global` (Fibonacci sphere over the body, proven placing 12 sites at a 50 km separation), through ONE `admit()`, because a second mode added later is a rewrite and a second mode proven today is a table. The generator takes the **FIRST admissible candidate in a fixed hash order, never best-of-N**: scoring 20,000 candidates costs 740,000 height samples at world init and first-admissible at the measured 4.69% hit rate costs about 800, and a score drifts on any terrain change anywhere in the band while a first-admissible only drifts when a candidate BEFORE the winner changes verdict. `maxTries` exhausting is a published **refusal with a histogram**, never a fallback that drops the gates. | One ruin now, more scattered later, and Reid's ask was explicitly "we will add more scattered around the planet". Building the instance would have meant deleting it. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-201 | **THE FOOTPRINT INSTRUMENT IS A PLANE FIT, BECAUSE "ON A CLIFF" IS TWO FAILURES AND SLOPE SEES ONE.** (a) TILT, a uniform grade under the whole footprint. (b) BROKEN GROUND, a level mean plane with a step or a ledge INSIDE the disc, where a point slope at the centre reads healthy and the building still has a hole under it. `measureFootprint` least-squares fits a plane over 37 samples (centre + 3 rings x 12 spokes, even rings offset half a spoke so 37 samples do not leave 12 blind wedges) and reports `tiltDeg` and `residP95M` **out of one fit, so the two numbers cannot disagree about what they saw**. p95 rather than max (one boulder crest must not veto good ground) and rather than mean (WG-146's corollary: terrain second-order quantities are heavy-tailed). **THE BASELINE IS STATED BY CONSTRUCTION**: the disc radius IS the footprint radius and the ring spacing is r/3, which is the answer to "the same Cinder point reads 24.500 deg over 6 m and 26.627 over 1000 m". Sphere curvature over the disc is r^2/(2R) = 0.00027 m at r = 18 m and is not corrected for; at r = 300 m it is 0.075 m and would have to be. | WG-146 exactly, one scale down: slope saturates on a tilt, so it cannot separate "tilted" from "featured". | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-202 | **AND THE HONEST READING OF THAT MEASUREMENT, which is not the one it first looks like.** Against a span-only gate (max minus min, the obvious cheap alternative) **calibrated to admit the SAME fraction** so the comparison is about WHICH ground each admits: at an **18 m** footprint the pair admits 4.69% of the band and the two gates disagree about **79 sites each way, of which only 7 are residual refusals and 72 are tilt**; at a **30 m** footprint the pair admits 9.30% and the disagreement is **235 each way, of which 212 are RESIDUAL and 23 tilt**. **At the shipped 18 m footprint the residual gate does very little work.** Broken ground inside 18 m is genuinely rarer than inside 30 m, which is a property of the terrain rather than of the instrument. It is kept anyway and the reason is written where it can be read: it costs nothing (it falls out of a fit already being computed for the tilt), it is the only thing between a bigger site type and a building with a hole under it, and the 30 m column is what it will look like the moment anyone adds one. **Adding a gate after the fact means re-deriving a threshold nobody has data for.** | Recorded because the 30 m figure is the one that flatters the decision and the 18 m figure is the one that ships. A justification that only holds at a radius nobody uses is exactly the number NUMBERS.md says has no defender. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-203 | **THE ART SPEC IS DERIVED FROM THE GATES AND IS NOT A SEPARATE OPINION.** With `footprintM` 18 m, `maxTiltDeg` 4.0 and `maxResidM` 1.0, the worst admissible ground drops `18*tan(4)` = **1.26 m** from centre to rim with up to **1.0 m** of residual on top, so **a ruin needs a plinth, skirt or buried course that can absorb about 2.3 m at its rim without daylight under it**. Tighten the gates and that falls; loosen them and it rises. **Nothing in this project levels terrain under a static structure** and the launch pad's answer was to refuse to sit on soil at all, so this is the number that decides whether a ruin reads as a building or as a prop hovering over a slope. The SHIPPED ruin is far inside it (tilt 0.720 deg, residP95 0.115 m), which is what the margin is for. | The pad's own history is the precedent: a 24 x 24 m footprint on one plane was accepted at 3.7% with a 6.956 m median spread. A 36 m ruin on unprepared ground has the same problem, worse. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-204 | **THE ID IS KEYED ON THE ORDINAL, NEVER ON THE POSITION, AND THAT IS WHAT PROTECTS A SAVE.** `siteIdFor(body, kind, ordinal)`. A terrain change moves the winning candidate; an id derived from the chosen direction would **orphan every "I have been here" bit in every save on the planet, silently**, and the ruin would present itself as unvisited to a player who had cleared it. Proven rather than argued: tightening the tilt gate moves the ruin **689.0 m** and the id is unchanged, `0x9f051605d9480737` on both sides. **TWO MECHANISMS FOR MOVING IT FAILED FIRST and the second is instructive.** Growing the pad blend to 1100 m moves nothing (the winner sits at 1394.6 m and was never inside it). Scaling `maxReliefM` moves nothing either, because `sampleHeightField` ends in `h *= body.maxReliefM`, so relief is a **UNIFORM scale on every height**, which scales every candidate's tilt by the same factor and therefore preserves the ORDER in which candidates pass a tilt gate. **A change that moves every number and no verdict is not a terrain change as far as this code is concerned.** | WG-11's discipline for `FDepositNode::Id` applied to a scarcer object where the cost of getting it wrong is higher: there are hundreds of deposits and one ruin. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-205 | **THE GATE ORDER WAS WRONG AND THE BUG WAS A GATE NO INPUT COULD REACH.** The first draft of `admit` tested the pad blend before the water. Forge's only water is the home pond, **55.0 m from `homeDir` and therefore inside the 600 m pad blend**, so every wet candidate on the only body that has water was refused as `InsidePadBlend` before the water gate ran: **the water gate was unreachable by any input and read exactly like a working one from the pass rate.** It was found by a shoreline test that could not be made to go red, which is WG-116's slope gate reached from a different direction. Water now runs before the footprint, which is also the cheaper order (13 depth queries against 37 height samples). **What remains true and is stated rather than hidden: the shipped spec's band starts at 700 m, so the shipped GENERATOR still cannot reach the pond.** The gate guards future ponds, lakes and a second body's water; its reachable refusing case is driven through `admit` directly and is a genuine shoreline case (centre DRY at depth 0.000 m, deepest point on the 18 m ring 3.391 m), which a centre-only water test would have admitted. | INSTRUMENTS.md's whole thesis. The control failing is what found it; every green in that build was truthful and the gate was worthless. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-206 | **THE OCEAN GATE'S ONLY REACHABLE CASE IS A DIFFERENT SEED, AND LOOKING FOR IT FOUND SOMETHING ELSE: `?seed=` DOES NOT MOVE THE SPAWN.** `BodyParams::homeDir` is a LITERAL unit vector calibrated for the client's default seed `0x0bf00d01` (DW-14 requires a literal, so this is not fixable by computing it at runtime). On seed `0x7` that same point is OCEAN and **all 4096 candidates in the band are refused for it**, correctly and with a histogram that says so. The seed moves the world under a fixed spawn point rather than moving the spawn. Recorded as **R18** rather than fixed, because moving `homeDir` per seed re-baselines the pad, the pond, every screenshot and every VisitSites row. | It is the only way to reach the ocean gate today, and it is a fact about `?seed=` that nothing else in the project states. | **Accepted (finding)** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-207 | **THE SPAWN IS NOT 1.7 KM UPHILL. IT IS 1,704.8 M UPHILL, IN THE MOUNTAINS, AND THE NEAREST NATURAL TREE IS OVER 20 KM AWAY.** `Config.ts:31` says in HEAD, in words, "a Hills valley floor at **2,963 m**". Measured through the shipped oracle at HEAD: **4,667.789 m, biome Mountains**, which `VisitSites.ts:95`'s `groundM: 4667.8` independently agrees with to the decimal. Drift **+1,704.789 m** and a biome change nobody wrote down. **AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS BIGGER THAN THE DRIFT.** `ScatterTuning.ts` puts `TREELINE_BARE_M` at **1850 m**, so the spawn is **2,817.8 m above the bare treeline**. Swept through `site_probe` at 20,000 candidates a band: **0 of 20,000 below the treeline and 100% Mountains at 700 to 1400 m, at 1500 to 6000 m, and at 6000 to 20000 m.** The first sub-treeline sample appears at **24 to 28 km** (1 of 12,000) and reaches only 3.9% by 28 to 34 km. **The storyline's first goal is "gather wood", and the only wood within 20 km of spawn is the 14 hand-placed trees of the starter spiral.** `TreeTuning.ts:193` already says so in a comment ("the only wood at a spawn above the treeline, which the current Mountains candidate is") and nothing has acted on it. | The standing task Admin named, measured, and worse than its framing. Raised rather than fixed: moving `homeDir` re-baselines the pad, the pond, the probes, the screenshots and the saves, and that is Admin's arbitration. The WG-55 shortlist in section 6.1 is still the live option. **The ruin design is deliberately immune to it**: the anchor is read from `body.homeDir`, so if the spawn moves the ruin moves with it and `poi.h` needs no edit. | **Accepted (finding, NOT fixed)** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-208 | **THE SPAWN PAD'S BLEND IS A 42% GRADE AND ITS OWN COMMENT SAYS 1 PERCENTAGE POINT.** `makeForge` says the blend "adds ~1 percentage point of grade over the natural slope of the same ground, so it does not read as a cut disc". Measured over an 18 m disc along one bearing: at 150 m padded tilt **0.148 deg (0.3%)** against natural **13.300 deg (23.6%)**; at 300 m **7.408 deg (13.0%)** against 9.077 (16.0%); at **450 m 22.828 deg (42.1%)** against 20.399 (37.2%); at 600 m 18.971 against 18.634; at 900 m bit-identical. The pad turns a 13 degree hillside dead flat and then has to give **23.7 m** back over 450 m of blend, and at the steepest point it is **4.9 percentage points steeper than the natural ground it is smoothing**. Printed by `test_poi.cpp` rather than asserted, because the pad is not this header's to change. | Not a contradiction of the original claim, which used a different baseline, and worth having on the record beside it, because a comment that justifies a number is a number nobody is checking. Downstream of WG-207: the pad exists to make a Mountains spawn buildable and it is doing a lot of work. | **Accepted (finding)** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-209 | **THE SEAM TO GAMEPLAY, PUBLISHED. `SiteCatalog` is the whole of it and it answers exactly two questions plus a keep-out.** (1) WHERE ARE THE RUINS: `sites()`, `near(dir, cosHalfAngle)`, `nearest(dir, kind)`, `byId(id)`. `near` takes a **cosine of a CONE**, not a projection and not a rectangle, for WG-29's reason: a scan radius yields a cone, a camera frustum yields a cone, and a map's orthographic cell does not survive the limb. (2) HAS THIS ONE BEEN INVESTIGATED: `visited(id)` and `markVisited(id)`, which **returns true only the first time**, so a caller can tell "first visit" from "again" without keeping a second copy of the bit. (3) `insideAnySite(body, dir, marginM)`, the keep-out every other placement system has to ask, which **normalises its argument** so it answers correctly for a surface position as well as a unit direction (the exact bug that silently beat the tree field's 6 m clearing keep-out, where `pos` at `radius + height` differs from `dir * radius` by 27 m at Forest). **NOT built here and named as gameplay's: the scan, the research, the reveal, the reward, and what "investigated" means.** World-gen never learns what a ruin contains. | The charter line already said it: "ruins, anomalies, loot sites (content authored by gameplay; placement here)". | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-210 | **EXISTENCE IS NOT RENDERING, AND THERE IS NO SECOND STREAMING PATH.** A site's LOCATION is known to the world from the moment the world exists. The table is **56 bytes a row**: one ruin is one row, five hundred scattered ruins is 28 KB, so there is no streaming of the table, no residency, no cache and no query cost worth optimising. The GEOMETRY is a normal streamed prop and belongs to whoever draws it. **The path to reuse is the space station's, not the harvest node's**, and this is settled rather than open: `StationView.build/place/sync` already gates `group.visible` on `eyeDistM <= farPlaneM + boundM` and acquires its batch slot lazily on first approach, which IS the "render it when the player gets close enough" Reid asked for. `RockField`/`TreeField` are the wrong model because their handoff is `/core` node-index shaped (`addOutcrop` requires an `of_gp_node_add`ed sim node, and `NodeBatch` only admits `_Full`/`_Half`/`_Low` meshes), so reusing them means inventing a fake node kind and fake depletion variants. **Collision is NOT streamed**: one always-resident `Solid` through the shared `StructureBodies`, which costs one sphere reject per walker sample. | Reid: "you do not have to then render all of them, just the location where it would be rendered. Then once the player gets close enough you would render it." The cheap answer and the correct one are the same answer. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-211 | **THE SHIPPED RUIN, MEASURED.** Seed `0x0bf00d01`, Forge. lat **1.89775**, lon **144.08537**, **1,394.6 m** from spawn, ground **4,756.1 m**, biome Mountains, tilt **0.720 deg** and residP95 **0.115 m** over its 18 m footprint, id `0x9f051605d9480737`, found on candidate **21 of 4096** (19 tilt refusals and 1 residual before it). The walk from spawn: **1,394.6 m, max grade 19.39 deg at a 5 m arm and 17.86 deg at 20 m, net climb +88.3 m, total ascent 124.9 m, 0 wet samples of 280, 5.1 minutes at the shipped 4.6 m/s** (2.5 minutes sprinting). **The walk is asserted on the shipped instance and is deliberately NOT a generator gate**: the straight great circle is the WORST case for reachability, because a player may walk around an obstacle, so a walkable great circle PROVES the site is reachable while an unwalkable one merely fails to prove it. Gating on it would refuse good sites for a reason that is not true. | The band is 700 to 1400 m: the inner edge is the 600 m pad blend plus the footprint plus margin (and `admit` CHECKS the pad delta rather than trusting that arithmetic, so growing the pad fails loudly), and the outer edge is a ten-minute round trip on foot. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-212 | **CINDER HAS NO RUINS AND THE REFUSAL IS A VALUE, NOT AN EMPTY TABLE.** `siteSpecsFor` is keyed on `bodyId` and its **default arm refuses**; `refusalFor` returns "Cinder is ruled lifeless (WG-141): no ruins on the moon" and, for anything else, "unknown body: no site table, and no fallback to Forge's". Proven three ways: Cinder places nothing and says why, body 47 places nothing and says why, and **a body carrying Forge's exact terrain constants under Cinder's id still places nothing** while the same ground under Forge's id places one. That third test is the shape `seedNests` was missing: **not a wrong table, an absent question.** | WG-144: a bug class that crosses bodies will cross again. The tutorial spiral put 51 nodes on the moon regardless of body and nobody noticed until somebody drove it. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |

| WG-213 | **THE WG-55 SHORTLIST IS STALE FOR THIS QUESTION, AND ITS OWN DATA SAYS SO.** `spawn_probe` is not wrong and is not superseded: it answered FLAT / TEMPERATE / SCENIC / DRY, carefully, before the treeline was a gameplay constraint. Its two Hills candidates sit at **2,077.2 m and 1,897.2 m**, and the canopy survey in section 6.2 of this file measured **0 canopy trees and 100% bare cells** at the first and **1 tree and 79% bare** at the second. Beach, the "desert option" Reid liked, has `TREE_DENSITY_KM2` **0 by table**. Only `plains` (507 trees) and `forest` (4,637) had wood, and the shortlist itself flagged both as empty frames (26 m and 23 m of relief in a 6 km box). So `core/tools/spawn_site.cpp` adds the criterion that decides it and keeps the rest: **expected trees in the shipped 620 m ring**, reported at the pessimistic / nominal / optimistic treeline wander rather than at one number, plus the pad's own plane fit on NATURAL ground, the 6 km relief and the fraction above (a floor against a tabletop), the fraction of the tree ring that is one biome (a boundary site can be flipped by a tuning nudge, which is the shortlist's own "do not ship rank 1" warning), and the highest the sun ever gets at that latitude. **THE WANDER IS A BAND, NOT A SWALLOWED TERM:** reproducing `TREELINE_WANDER_M`'s noise field in C++ would be a second copy of a client rule in a second language, so the tool reports h-240 / h / h+240 and the pessimistic column is the one to read. | INSTRUMENTS.md's dominant failure exactly: a control that depends on something that moved. Nobody made a mistake; the question changed underneath a correct answer. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-214 | **THE SPAWN MOVED, from lat 2 / lon 144 to lat -3.41413 / lon 150.27984.** Reid ruled it after WG-207. A Hills valley floor at **797.6 m**: below `TREELINE_FULL_M` even with the wander's full 240 m pushed against it, **~1,296 trees in the 620 m ring pessimistically** (the old site: 0), **623 m of relief inside a 6 km box with 54.2% of it standing above** (a floor, not a tabletop), **its whole tree ring one biome** so no tuning nudge can flip it, and **noon sun 63.8 deg** against the old 69.2, the closest of the candidate set so the lighting regime barely shifts. **The pad's work drops from 10.517 deg / 8.424 m p95 residual to 2.429 / 3.492.** Both DW-14 literals re-emitted and pinned: `homeDir` bit-agrees with its lat/lon to 0.000000 m, `pondDir` is **55.000000 m** away on a heading 30 deg east of north (WG-57: move one without the other and the basin is orphaned) and sits wholly inside the 150 m dead-flat disc with its rim. **THE RUIN NEEDED NO EDIT AND MOVED WITH IT**, which is WG-200's anchor design paying off inside the hour: it is now **753.8 m out at 772.8 m in Hills, a 2.7 minute walk with a worst grade of 6.96 deg at a 20 m arm**, against the old site's 1,394.6 m and 17.86 deg. **41/41 ctest green.** | Reid is testing in sandbox and has no playthrough to protect, so the cost of moving `homeDir` is at its minimum today and rises the day he starts one. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |
| WG-215 | **THE CLAIM IS NOW ASSERTED, NOT DESCRIBED: `core/tests/test_spawn.cpp` (8 tests, 23 checks).** `Config.ts:31` described a place the spawn was not, and **that comment was the only thing making the claim**, which is how 1,704.789 m of drift and a biome change went unseen for months. Eight gates: the `homeDir` literal agrees with `Config.ts`'s lat/lon (two encodings of one place, and nothing checked they agreed); the biome bears trees; the site is below the treeline **with the wander's own margin** (`BARE - WANDER`, because being under BARE is not enough when the threshold is displaced); there is wood in the shipped ring; the pad sits on already-flat ground; the pond moved with the pad; the land is rugged AND is a floor rather than a tabletop (**both halves needed: relief alone passes a hilltop**); and the ruin still places and is still a walk. **EVERY BOUND IS LOOSER THAN THE CHOSEN SITE ON PURPOSE**: a gate tuned to the site it was written for fails on any change and gets deleted. **AND THERE IS A NEGATIVE CONTROL: all four headline gates are proven to REFUSE the old spawn**, in the same suite, in the same run, so none of them is a rubber stamp on whatever ships. | The rule this project wrote down twice this week: prose is for the reason, never for the constraint. A relationship that matters enough to explain matters enough to fail. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-03 |

Deriving it per seed means shipping a cross-toolchain-exact trig or a fixed-point direction construction, **with its own parity suite**, and cross-toolchain exactness is a thing this project already maintains a 106-case suite for. **(2) The spawn is not a formula, it is a SEARCH.** `spawn_site` sweeps 400,000 directions and then measures 185 of them; that cannot run at world init.
