## 4. Architecture & approach
- **Terrain:** noise stack (continents → mountains → detail) evaluated per cubed-sphere quad at the LOD needed by camera distance; quads stream in/out. Everything derives from `(bodySeed, faceId, quadPath)` → fully regenerable, so the natural world costs ~zero to save.
- **Deformation:** when a player digs, instantiate a voxel/SDF patch seeded from the heightfield at that location; store the *delta* only. Rendering & physics query "is there a patch here?" before falling back to heightfield. (v1 may stub this; keep the seam in the API.)
- **Deposits/POIs:** a deterministic placement pass over each body (Poisson-disk / hashed-grid by seed) yields node positions + types; gameplay binds content. Depletion/looted flags are diffs.
- **Floating-origin aware:** all queries accept/return data in core-engine's coordinate/frame terms; chunk origins are 64-bit, local meshes are near-origin.

## 5. Interfaces & dependencies
**Depends on (inbound):** core-engine's **pinned** contracts (spike1-core-engine.md §5) — `FUniverseCoord` (now carries `FFrameId`), `FFloatingOrigin`/`ToEngine`, `IFrameGraph`/`FReferenceFrame` (μ/radius/SOI/spin), and the events `OnOriginRebased`/`OnSOIChange`. Chunk centers are `FUniverseCoord`, **not** `FVector`. The seed comes from [persistence](persistence.md)/new-game config.
**Provides to (outbound) — published contracts (now pinned concretely in [Spike 1 §4](../spikes/spike1-worldgen.md#4-pinned-data-contracts)):**
- `SampleTerrainHeight(FBodyId, lat, lon)` + collision queries `GetGroundContact` / `RaycastTerrain` — all `FUniverseCoord`-based, resident-free, with the `bFromVoxelPatch` voxel seam → [physics](physics.md). **`FTerrainContact` + `FTerrainHit` now also carry `SlopeRad` (terrain slope, radians, from the contact `Normal`) + `SurfaceHardness` (normalized `[0,1]` `double`, from `MaterialId`/biome) for physics tip-over + friction/restitution (RC-7, append-only).** *(Spike 1 §4.3, WG-10)*
- `FTerrainChunk` (chunk center as `FUniverseCoord`, body-center-relative verts, skirts, `MaterialId`) + `ITerrainProvider`/`OnChunkReady`/`OnChunkEvicted` streaming → [rendering](rendering.md). *(Spike 1 §4.1–4.2)*
- **`FDepositNode` deposit catalog** (C-1, **PINNED** Spike 1 §4.5) + query surface `GetDeposits`/`QueryDepositsNear`/`GetDeposit`/`ExtractFromDeposit` → [gameplay](gameplay.md) (mining UX), [factory-sim](factory-sim.md) (miner extraction), [persistence](persistence.md) (depletion). Per node: `FDepositId Id` (stable hash), `Position`(`FUniverseCoord`+`FFrameId`), `Lat`/`Lon`, `SurfaceNormal`, `FBodyId Body`, **opaque gameplay-owned `ItemId Resource`** (C-3 — referenced not defined; collapses gameplay's `DepositTypeId`), `Grade`, `InitialAmount`, mutable `RemainingAmount`. Catalog deterministically seed-generated per body (WG-6 discipline). **Cinderite `ItemId` placed only on Cinder** (WG-4 off-world hook). *(Spike 1 §4.5, §5.1–5.2, WG-11)* **Cross-domain dep: needs gameplay's C-3 `ItemId` registry (Ferrite-ore, Cinderite) to exist with stable ids — flagged to Admin.**
- **`FBodyParams`** (radius, μ, surface g, SOI, spin, atmosphere profile) → physics, rendering. **`FAtmosphereProfile` now carries Mie terms (`MieScaleHeightM`/`MieScatteringScale`/`MieAnisotropy`) alongside the Rayleigh fields for rendering's UE Sky-Atmosphere horizon haze (RC-2, append-only); Forge gets concrete Mie values, Cinder is airless → N/A.** *(Spike 1 §5, §5.1–5.2, WG-10; numeric values shared with core-engine `FReferenceFrame` — Admin to confirm single source of truth.)*
- **`WorldDiscovery` (WG-29, NEW, `core/include/of/discovery.h`)** → the map ([gameplay](gameplay.md)), [persistence](persistence.md). Two named questions, `surveyed(dir)` and `explored(dir)`, over two sparse cube-face grids fed by ONE `observe(dir, altM)`. Published across the bridge as `of_disc_*` (ABI 12) and typed in `web/src/sim/wasm/discabi.ts`; `of_disc_window` hands the map four CORNER directions per cell rather than a centre and a size, because the map's projection is orthographic and a cell near the limb foreshortens to a sliver. Serialises as a delta-varint key set at **1.04 bytes/cell** through the persistence.h cursors WITHOUT including that header (leaf, as `voxel_field.h` is). The lattice is deliberately `PollutionField`'s ([enemies](enemies.md) §4) and `discovery_tests` asserts the two agree cell-for-cell over 560,000 directions; **open cross-domain ask: enemies.h should adopt `SurfaceCellGrid` so there is one copy rather than two agreeing ones.** Consumers must ask `ModeRules.fullMapRevealed` (DW-31) rather than this field when the MODE decides visibility.
- **WHAT THE DW-37 3D MAP LANE REPLACES, AND WHAT IT INHERITS (WG-29, written FOR that lane).** DW-37 turns the map into a rotatable camera over the scene the engine already renders. Sorted so nobody has to re-derive it. **VIEW-INDEPENDENT, transfers intact, do not rewrite:** `discovery.h` entire (it answers "has this direction been seen", at two resolutions, and contains no projection); every `of_disc_*` export (`of_disc_window` takes a direction and a cosine, which is a CONE and not a projection; a camera frustum yields one too); `of_map_sample` (deliberately specified in view-REGION terms, so a far-field ground overlay reuses it; **SIGNATURE CHANGED at ABI 14, WG-33**: an `edits` DensityField handle in second position and the height is `surfaceHeight` rather than `sampleDesignedHeight`, so the player's own terrain reaches the map. `edits <= 0` is bit-identical to ABI 13 by the oracle's own undug overload. One caller, `web/src/world/MapTerrain.ts`; every other export untouched); `web/src/world/Discovery.ts` entire, including the 1 Hz interval, the generation-keyed cache and the save round trip; `MapWorld.ore()`, which is THE discovery gate and hands over plain world data; `ModeRules.fullMapRevealed`; the persistence field and `RestoreLedger.discovery`; and `MapTypes`' `MapOre` / `MapDiscovered` / `MapDiscoveryReadout`, none of which carry a screen unit. `MapFocus` splits: the ORIGIN half (`centreM`) transfers exactly, because a camera orbits a focus point, and the AXIS half becomes the camera's orientation reference rather than a projection plane; the *rule* that the axis rides on the FOCUS and never on the zoom is the part worth keeping, since it is what stops a conic foreshortening differently at different zooms. **PROJECTION-SPECIFIC, expect to replace:** `MapDraw.ts` (`Proj`, `toPx`, the body arc, the conic polyline and its Liang-Barsky clip, the apsis rings and leader labels, the scale bar); `MapPaint.ts`'s `toPx` / `discCoversCanvas` / `place` / `hits`; `MapLayers.ts`'s quad fill and glyphs; and the sampled-terrain blit, which is the half of the terrain work that a real camera makes redundant. **`MapPaint.sizeAlpha` is the interesting middle case and should be KEPT**: it is expressed in pixels per metre, which a perspective camera also has (projected size), so the legibility ramp and DW-36's "no zoom threshold anywhere" property survive a change of projection even though every call site moves. **Is `MapScene` still the seam? Yes, and more so than R17 thought.** `centreM`, `spanM`, `focusName`, `axisName`, `discovered`, `ore`, `revealAll` and both conics are all producer-side world data; only `planeU`/`planeV` are projection-specific and they become an orientation. `MapMode.readout` barely changes, and `MapView`'s READOUT COLUMN (Position / Focus / Discovery / Maneuver) is DOM over plain numbers and does not change at all.
- **`IPersistable` (C-6, world-gen side — CONFIRMED** Spike 1 §4.6**)** → [persistence](persistence.md). World-gen implements `IPersistable` (chunk-scoped, `DomainId=WorldGen`) over **deposit depletion only**: `(depositId, remaining)[]` per region, `depositId = hash64(bodySeed, region, localIndex)` (the stable id persistence §2.1 specified). Terrain + deposit *placement* regenerate from seed (PS-7/WV1) — **not serialized**; voxel-patch diffs are Phase-4 (seam empty, WG-2). *(Spike 1 §4.6, WG-12)*
- **THE WATER LEVEL (WG-36 to WG-39, NEW, `core/include/of/water_field.h`)** -> the character controller ([physics](physics.md)), [rendering](rendering.md), and eventually the pump ([factory-sim](factory-sim.md)). **A THIRD surface answer, published as its own named authority with its own bound, per DW-26.** `levelM(body)` is ONE scalar per body, metres above the datum. `levelAt(dir)` returns it or `kNoWater`. `depthAt(dir, edits)` is the only function that reads a ground authority and it reads the EDITED surface, so digging the bed deeper genuinely makes the water deeper. `submersionM(pos)` takes a POINT and nothing else and is the ONE place "am I in water" is answered. `shorelineM` is DERIVED by bisection from the basin profile and never stored, because a constant copied from the thing it describes is standing rule 11's worked example. Across the bridge as `of_water_*` at **ABI 16**; typed in `heap.ts`; on the client as `WaterOracle`, a SEPARATE class from `SurfaceOracle` with no method name in common. **There is no `of_surface_water`, no water flag on any existing surface call, and no `waterHeight` on `SurfaceOracle`. If you want water you ask for it by name, or you do not get it.**

- **WHAT THE WATER SURFACE STILL NEEDS FROM RENDERING (WG-42, written FOR that lane, and this is the honest list rather than a half-shaded handoff).** `web/src/world/WaterSurface.ts` ships the part that is world DATA and stops. **It spends ZERO of DW-10's five custom shaders**, deliberately, so the lane that gives water a look can spend one knowingly rather than inherit a compromise. What is there: a curved shell on the sphere reaching the SHORELINE (not the basin rim, so dry beach shows inside the bowl), tessellated finest at the waterline, with per-vertex RGBA where **both the colour and the ALPHA are a function of the measured water depth under that vertex** (0.14 alpha at the edge where the bottom is 13 cm down, 0.82 in the middle at 3.4 m), a stock `MeshStandardMaterial`, `DoubleSide`, `depthWrite: false`, `renderOrder` 2, anchored per standing rule 6. **WHAT IS MISSING, in the order it would most improve the read:**
  1. **AN UNDERWATER CAMERA LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE DRY LAND.** `docs/screenshots/WG36_after_aerial.png` is the camera 1.72 m below the water surface and it is indistinguishable from standing on the bed: no tint, no depth fog, no colour extinction, no surface seen from beneath. The quantity that pass needs already exists, is correct, and is published every tick: `SwimState.headUnderM` (positive = the eye is that far under), reachable from a probe as `of.swim().headUnderM`. This is the single biggest gap and it is entirely rendering's.
  2. **No surface motion at all.** No normal animation, no ripples, no flow. A perfectly still 33 m pond reads as glass or as a decal depending on the angle.
  3. **No specular sun glint and no reflection.** Water without a highlight is the main reason a translucent plane reads as a filter rather than as a liquid.
  4. **No refraction**, so anything crossing the waterline (the player's own legs, a prop, a future pipe) is straight through it.
  5. **No shoreline treatment.** No foam, no wet-sand darkening on the beach ring, no soft alpha against the terrain intersection. The 5.4 m dry beach between `shorelineM` (16.62 m) and `basinRadiusM` (22 m) is currently the same material as ground 300 m away.
  6. **Nothing in the far scene.** The pond exists only in the near pass, so it disappears rather than fades at range.
  **AND ONE THING THAT IS NOT RENDERING'S, flagged here because it is visible in every screenshot:** the prop scatter does not know the pond exists, so `Mtn_SnowPatch`, grass cards and pebbles are placed ON THE POND BED and at the waterline (see `WG36_after_swimming.png`, ice slabs floating mid-pond). The scatter needs to read `of_water_depth_at` and refuse, or bias to submerged props, wherever depth is positive. That is one query and it is now available; the file is the vegetation lane's.

- **THE PER-CHUNK NOISE PHASE (WG-50 to WG-54, NEW, `web/src/world/ChunkPhase.ts`)** -> [rendering](rendering.md). This is the answer to RN-45's open dependency, and it is **deliberately NOT in the chunk wire format**: it changes no byte of the 28 B/vertex packed layout, no `of_packed_*` export, no ABI and no parity fixture. It is a pure function of `ChunkView.anchor`, which is already float64 on the main thread.
  - `reduceOctave(anchor, wavelengthM) -> { cell, frac }` and `packPhases(anchor, wavelengthsM) -> Float32Array`, laid out `[cell.xyz, frac.xyz]` per octave, 6 floats each. `cell` is `floor(anchor / L)`, an exact integer with 189x to 10,131x of headroom under float32's 2^24; `frac` is `anchor / L - cell` in [0, 1). The division and the floor happen in float64 **here and nowhere else**, which is the entire content of the fix.
  - **THE SHADER-SIDE HALF OF THE CONTRACT, stated in one place because a phase computed one way on the CPU and consumed another way in a shader is exactly where a bit-exactness guarantee gets quietly lost.** Per axis, with `rLocal` the vertex's chunk-local offset in metres (that is the `position` attribute, already float32 relative to the anchor): `t = frac + rLocal / L`, then `i = cell + floor(t)` in exact float32 integer arithmetic and `f = fract(t)`. Hash `i` exactly as the shader hashes `floor(pM / L)` today and interpolate on `f`. **`cell` is what makes the field global rather than tiled per chunk, and `frac` is what makes it resolvable.** Dropping `cell` gives every chunk the same noise patch, which is a visible grid.
  - **Every octave that feeds a derivative must be reduced. A partial fix passes the dead-quad statistic and is still visibly wrong (WG-53).** At the shipped ladder that is 4 octaves, 24 floats per chunk.
  - **The delivery channel is rendering's to choose and one candidate is already ruled out (WG-54):** the `aFadeT0` vertex-attribute pattern costs about 37 MB against a 93.7 MB frame and is refused; the `MachineBatch.ts:131` batching-id `DataTexture` costs about 31 KB. A per-chunk uniform is not available at all, because chunks are instances in two `BatchedMesh`es (DW-11).
  - **Re-stamp wherever the anchor changes** (`ChunkView.refresh()`, the near/far slot swap) and **not on the floating-origin rebase**, because the anchor is invariant under a rebase. That invariance is the whole reason RN-45 keyed the field on `pM` in the first place, and it is preserved rather than given up.
  - **Two instruments ship with it and rendering should adopt both, because neither is sufficient alone:** `web/tools/precision/phasecheck.ts` (57/57 assertions, including a positive control that fails the shipped arithmetic by name) and `web/tools/precision/artphase.ts` (the offline reproduction, the dead-quad statistic, the field-agreement statistic, and the pictures). **The dead-quad count is necessary and not sufficient and the picture is still the arbiter (WG-53).**

- **AND BEFORE RENDERING BUILDS ANY OF THAT, read WG-56.** Every chunk already carries a per-quad normalised UV that is uploaded, held in VRAM and interpolated every frame, and `TerrainShader.ts` samples it **zero times**. Its quantum is **0.248 of a ground pixel at 2 m** where `pM`'s is **8.77 pixels**. A tiling detail map on `uv * N` may deliver sub-metre detail with no phase and no CPU work at all. The phase is still the right general fix and is the only one that repairs a derivative taken on a WORLD-space field; the UV route is narrower and may make the detail bump specifically unnecessary. Measure both.

- **WHAT AN AIRLESS BODY REQUIRES OF RENDERING (WG-148, NEW, written FOR that lane, and it is a consequence of a world-gen FACT rather than a taste).** Cinder is airless by design (D-006): `atmosphere.h::makeCinderAtmosphere()` returns an empty profile and every query returns exactly 0. The client does not read that. `web/src/app/Boot.ts:120` calls `forgeAtmosphere(body.radiusM)` unconditionally, and `forgeAtmosphere` is the ONLY producer of `AtmosphereParams` in the codebase; there is no airless counterpart. `docs/web/ARCHITECTURE.md:2553` already anticipated this ("Cinder needs an airless profile (`uAtmosOn` 0) at W9").
  - **THE MEASUREMENT, because "it would look better" is not an argument.** One camera, 12 km over the spawn meridian, everything else identical: `docs/screenshots/WG141_moon_air12k.png` (atmosphere on) is a **white fog bank in which not one crater is visible**; `docs/screenshots/WG141_moon_air12k_noatmos.png` is a black sky with stars, a hard terminator, and overlapping craters at four scales. **Aerial perspective was erasing the entire WG-141 rewrite.** That pair is the whole ask.
  - **What is needed, in order.** (1) An `airlessAtmosphere()` sibling, or a body-kind branch at the one decision site, so `uAtmosOn` is 0 on a moon: no Rayleigh, no Mie, no aerial perspective in `TerrainMaterial`. (2) **A sky that is black but not empty**: stars must be visible AT NOON, which is the single strongest "there is no air here" cue and the opposite of Forge's rule. (3) **AN AMBIENT SOURCE THAT IS NOT THE SKY INTEGRAL, and this one is load-bearing rather than cosmetic.** `SkyIbl` derives ambient from the sky, so with the sky off the shadowed side of every crater and boulder collapses to near-black: see `docs/screenshots/WG141_ground_craterfloor.png` and `WG141_ground_highland.png`, both at `sundot=0.28`. Real airless bodies genuinely do have brutal terminator contrast, so this is not simply wrong, but at these values the moon is unplayable in shadow and a player landing near the terminator sees nothing. An explicit low ambient or a bounce term is a rendering decision I am flagging, not making.
  - **NOT asked for: ejecta rays.** They are albedo, not topography, so they are deliberately absent from the height field (WG-141) rather than faked into it. If they are wanted they are a material effect and they need an "ejecta proximity" scalar this lane can publish; say so and I will add one.
  - **One thing in these screenshots that is NOT rendering's:** thin bright streaks follow the terrain contours in `WG141_ground_craterfloor.png`. Unattributed as yet, possibly the detail-card layer at grazing light. Recorded as R17 rather than blamed.

- **THE POI / SITE SEAM (WG-200 to WG-212, NEW, `core/include/of/poi.h`)** -> [gameplay](gameplay.md) (the scan, the reveal, the reward), [rendering](rendering.md) (the prop), [persistence](persistence.md) (one bit), and every placement system in the client (the keep-out). **The world says WHERE a site is and never what is inside one.**
  - **`SiteCatalog::ForBody(body)`** is the whole entry point. `sites()` returns `FSite` rows: stable `SiteId id`, `SiteKind kind`, `uint16_t body`, `uint32_t variant` and `ordinal`, unit `dir`, body-frame `pos` on the BASE surface, `up`, `latRad`/`lonRad`, `yawRad`, `footprintM`, `arcFromAnchorM`, and the `tiltDeg`/`residP95M`/`groundM`/`biome` that admitted it, **published so no caller re-measures**.
  - **"Where are the ruins"** (for the scan to reveal): `near(dir, cosHalfAngle)` -> indices, `nearest(dir, kind)` -> index, `byId(id)`. **`near` takes the COSINE OF A CONE**, deliberately, for WG-29's reason: a scan radius yields a cone and a camera frustum yields a cone, while a map's orthographic cell does not survive the limb. One dot product per site and no trig at the call site.
  - **"Has this one been investigated"**: `visited(id)` and `markVisited(id)`. **`markVisited` returns TRUE only the first time**, so gameplay can distinguish a first visit from a repeat without keeping its own copy of the bit. `visitedCount()` for the HUD.
  - **THE KEEP-OUT, and it is an ASK of the client rather than only an offer**: `insideAnySite(body, dir, marginM)`. `TreeField`, `RockField`, `OreField`, the scatter and player building all need it or a 14 m conifer grows through the roof, and none of them consults it today. It **normalises its argument**, so it answers correctly for a surface `pos` (at `radius + height`) as well as for a unit direction; the tree field's clearing keep-out shipped broken for exactly that reason and read `refusedClearing: 0` while arithmetic said it must fire. **Whoever wires it must add a refusal COUNTER**, because a gate that cannot be distinguished from an unwired gate is not a gate.
  - **The save surface is ONE BIT per site.** `serialize`/`deserialize` are templated on the persistence cursor style so the header stays a leaf (it never includes persistence.h) and write the visited ids as a sorted delta-varint list: **11 bytes for one visited site, 1 byte for a world where nothing has been visited.** Everything else regenerates (WG-3, C-6). `deserialize` DROPS an id with no matching site rather than failing, so a table that shrank because a spec changed still loads.
  - **THE BRIDGE WAS SPECIFIED HERE AND IS NOW BUILT: ABI 24, WG-151.** The nine exports below shipped with their layout unchanged; the calling convention (scratch arena, not a caller pointer) follows this shim's own established precedent rather than this bullet's `outPtr`/`cap`/`ptr`/`len` prose, which predates a working build. A second bit (`known_`, unknown -> known -> visited, monotone) shipped in the same pass, Admin-approved: see WG-151 for the full account. Left below for the record. It needed an ABI number and I did not take one at the time: a lane is live in `flight.h` and the docking bridge, and WG-141's lane set the precedent of taking none while physics held an uncommitted ABI. The proposed exports, so Admin can allocate and a settled rebuild can verify them without reading diffs: `of_poi_count(body) -> i32`; `of_poi_row(body, i, outPtr) -> i32` writing **18 f64 words** [idLo, idHi, kind, ordinal, dirX, dirY, dirZ, posX, posY, posZ, lat, lon, yaw, footprintM, arcM, tiltDeg, residP95M, biome], with the id split into two 32-bit halves because a `uint64` id does not survive a single f64; `of_poi_near(body, dx, dy, dz, cosHalf, outPtr, maxN) -> i32`; `of_poi_nearest(body, dx, dy, dz, kind) -> i32`; `of_poi_inside(body, dx, dy, dz, marginM) -> i32`; `of_poi_visited(body, idLo, idHi) -> i32`; `of_poi_mark_visited(body, idLo, idHi) -> i32`; `of_poi_save(body, outPtr, cap) -> i32`; `of_poi_load(body, ptr, len) -> i32`. **Nine exports, one 18-word scratch row.** No lane commits `of-core.wasm` or `expected.json`.

- **WHAT THE RUIN NEEDS FROM AN ASSET LANE (WG-203, written FOR that lane; `tools/blender/**` is theirs and a `.glb` is an unmergeable blob, so this is a specification and not a model).**
  - **Footprint:** the site guarantees a disc of **radius 18 m (36 m across)** whose ground is flat to **tilt <= 4.0 deg** and **p95 |h - plane| <= 1.0 m**. The building sits inside that.
  - **THE PLINTH IS THE DERIVED NUMBER AND IT IS THE ONE THAT DECIDES WHETHER THIS READS AS A BUILDING.** Worst admissible ground drops `18*tan(4)` = **1.26 m** centre to rim with up to **1.0 m** of residual on top, so the ruin needs a plinth, skirt, buried course or rubble apron that absorbs about **2.3 m at its rim with no daylight under it**. **Nothing in this project levels terrain under a static structure**; the launch pad's answer was to refuse to sit on soil at all, and a 36 m ruin has the same problem worse.
  - **Height:** 6 to 12 m, so it breaks the skyline from a few hundred metres. That is what makes "the scan revealed something over there" land as a destination rather than as a map pin.
  - **Enterable: YES, and that is the whole point** (the player investigates it). It needs an interior volume the capsule fits in and a threshold with no step taller than the walker's climb.
  - **Collision: `col_*` proxy meshes, and this is not optional.** They go through `proxiesOf` into ONE `Solid` on the shared `StructureBodies`. **`col_Ruin_1` DELETES `col_Ruin_2` and everything after it**, because every proxy reader in the client does `mesh.name.replace(/_\d+$/, '')`: name them `col_Ruin1` to `col_RuinN` with NO underscore before the digit. `check-proxies.mjs` is the gate.
  - **LOD naming: `Ruin_LOD0/1/2`**, the building convention. **NOT** the harvest-node `_Full`/`_Half`/`_Low` convention, which is for depletion variants a ruin does not have.
  - **Variants: 1 is enough today**, and `SiteSpec::variants` already hashes a choice from the site id, so an art set of 3 or 4 needs one number changed and nothing else. Ask for more when the scattered ruins land.
  - **A placeholder that is obviously a placeholder is fine for proving the placement.** The placement is provable without any art at all: `poi_tests` asserts it headlessly today.

**Contracts negotiated:** chunk format (`FTerrainChunk`) + height/collision queries are **pinned in Spike 1 §4**, confirmed consistent with core-engine's `UniverseCoord`/`FFrameId`. **`FDepositNode` (C-1) + deposit-depletion `IPersistable` (C-6) pinned/confirmed in Spike 1 §4.5–§4.6 (Phase 1).** Open negotiation with physics: analytic queries vs baked per-quad collision meshes (Spike 1 WR5).

## 6. Task backlog / roadmap
- **[Phase 0 / Spike 1 — Wave-0 core BUILT + GREEN (WG-13); UE streaming pending]** Minimal cubed-sphere heightfield for one planet (atmo) + one moon (airless). **WG-Build 0 DONE in headless C++** (`cubed_sphere.h` + `world_gen_tests`): `FQuadKey` + quad math, tangent warp (WG-5), position-hashed noise stack (WG-6/WG-7), `generateQuadMesh`, `SampleTerrainHeight`; **gates WV1/WV2/WV3 pass BITWISE** (neighbour + parent/child shared edges bit-identical; deterministic regen), plus cube→sphere-on-sphere, warp-uniformity, WV7 query agreement, planet≠moon. Full design + build plan + pinned chunk/height/collision contracts + 2-body params in **[spike1-worldgen.md](../spikes/spike1-worldgen.md)**. Next: **WG-Build 1→2** — `UTerrainStreamer` (LOD/hysteresis/skirts/async/budget, §3), `FTerrainChunk` emission + floating-origin hookup (`OnOriginRebased`/`OnSOIChange`), wire the query layer's `bFromVoxelPatch` stub, then integrate at core-engine Wave-1 step 8 (replace placeholder sphere colliders) and run gates WV4–WV6/WV8 in-engine.
- **[Phase 1 — DEPOSIT GEN BUILT + GREEN (WG-14); depletion `IPersistable` pending]** Deposit catalog **`FDepositNode` pinned (C-1, Spike 1 §4.5, WG-11) and now REALIZED** — `core/include/of/deposits.h` + `deposits_tests`: `GenerateDeposits` (deterministic position-hashed Fibonacci-sphere placement, surface-snapped), the full query surface (`GetDeposits`/`QueryDepositsNear`/`GetDeposit`/`ExtractFromDeposit`), and the **Cinderite off-world hook (WG-4)** all built; determinism + Cinder-only + on-surface + extraction/depletion (seed+diff) proven bitwise. **`IPersistable` deposit-depletion confirmed (C-6, Spike 1 §4.6, WG-12)** — NEXT: wire the depletion `(depositId, remaining)[]` `IPersistable` over the catalog (the diff surface is exactly `ExtractFromDeposit`'s `RemainingAmount` decrement). Also Phase 1: biomes, full body-parameter tuning feeding physics/rendering; edge-stitch index buffers (Spike 1 WG-8 deferred polish). **Dep: gameplay C-3 `ItemId` registry — world-gen currently references Ferrite `0x0001` / Cinderite `0x0004` as bare `uint16_t` (WG-11); gameplay confirms the canonical ids.**
- [Phase 2] Diff persistence integration (deposit-depletion design done — C-6); resource identity tuning (the WG-4 rule is pinned for the 2 bodies; tune density/grades).
- [Phase 4] Voxel deformation patches (fill the `bFromVoxelPatch` seam, Spike 1 §4.4); POI/loot placement at scale.

### 6.1 WG-55 spawn shortlist, awaiting Reid's pick

Not moved, deliberately. `homeDir` re-baselines the ground under every pad,
level, build and terrain probe, and `pondDir` must move with it or the pond is
orphaned. It goes when the checkout is quiet and Reid has picked.

#### Four things that are true and were not known before this survey

Reid picks the site. The job here is to make sure he is picking from true
statements, and four of the statements we were working from were false.

**1. There is no Desert biome, and the desert he liked is Beach.** He asked for
"the desert biome" by name. The biome set is Ocean, Beach, Plains, Forest,
Hills, Mountains and Polar (`biome.h:53-67`), and that is all of it. What reads
as desert is partly the post grade and partly **Beach**, whose palette entry is
the sand colour `0xc8b48a` and which is one of only two biomes that take dry
scrub props instead of grass. `docs/screenshots/WG53_site_beach.png` is a Beach
site and it is unmistakably the look he meant: pale sand, dry tussocks, low
scrub, open sky. **So the answer to "can I start in the desert" is yes, and it
is called Beach.** Beach was invisible to the spawn probe because the probe's
`minAlt = 200.0` (`spawn_probe.cpp:544`) cuts the entire coastal band.

**2. The spawn probe could never have produced the current spawn.** It hard
filters to Plains, Forest and Hills (`spawn_probe.cpp:134-136,425`) and the
current spawn is Mountains. This is not a scoring near miss: the probe and the
world disagree about what a legal spawn even is. Re-run over the current site it
ranks it **last of 21, score -85.84 against the winner's +32.81**, with 23.88%
slope at 20 m, which is 20x to 30x every candidate. It is also not the "Hills
valley floor at 2,963 m" that `Config.ts` still documents: it is Mountains at
4,667.79 m and it stands **+130.7 m above the mean of the surrounding 6 km**,
which makes it a tabletop rather than a valley floor.

**3. The probe's pad verdict is currently wrong, and my own pond is what broke
it.** `probePadHere` moves `homeDir` to the candidate (`spawn_probe.cpp:487-491`)
but never moves `pondDir` or zeroes `pondRadiusM`, and it measures with
`sampleDesignedHeight`, which since WG-36 subtracts the pond basin. Its reported
"worst 4 m step inside the flat radius 1.078601 m" at the current site is
**entirely the pond**: inside the flat radius the un-ponded height is a bit-exact
constant, so the basin is the only varying term, and the analytic 4 m span across
the basin's steepest point is 1.080 m. Candidate rows read 0.000000 m only
because the pond is thousands of km away from them. A feature of mine is
degrading an instrument of mine, which is the shape worth remembering.

**4. The snow line is a biome boundary, not an altitude.** `Mtn_SnowPatch` is
keyed on the biome index with no elevation gate at all (`Registry.ts:143-144`),
so the prop snow line is exactly the Mountains boundary, `kMountainsRel` 0.330 of
6000 m of raw relief, about **2,700 m** of designed height. The shader's own
snowfield is a separate thing entirely, 5,160 m to 6,840 m
(`TerrainShader.ts:263-265`). The current spawn sits **1,239 m above the prop
line and 492 m below the shader one**, which is precisely why WG-35's blue blobs
were props rather than painted snow.

#### And one thing the control picture settles

`docs/screenshots/WG53_site_current.png` is today's spawn in daylight, and **the
blue blobs are plainly in it and they read as puddles of water.** They are flat,
saturated blue, and lying in the ground. They are `Mtn_SnowPatch` ice props, so
the WG-35 diagnosis is confirmed on the picture as well as in the counts, but the
useful half is the other direction: **Reid calling them water was the correct
reading of what is on screen.** An ice prop that reads as standing water is an
art defect independent of where the spawn goes, and moving the spawn out of
Mountains hides it rather than fixes it. Flagged for the art and rendering lanes;
not mine to change.

#### A selection criterion nobody knew about: this planet has no seasons and a fixed sun declination

Forge's sun direction is `normalize(cos 2*pi*t, 0.42, sin 2*pi*t)`
(`SkyPass.ts:105`). The `y` component is **constant at +0.387 for all time**, so
the sun has a permanently high northern declination and the planet has no axial
tilt and no seasons. **A far-southern spawn therefore cannot ever get a high
sun.** Measured at each candidate's own local noon:

| site | lat | best sun elevation, ever |
|---|---|---|
| hills2 | +22.29 | 89.5 deg |
| current | +2.00 | 69.2 deg |
| plains | -7.97 | 59.3 deg |
| forest | -19.85 | 47.4 deg |
| hills | -31.17 | 36.1 deg |
| beach | -35.60 | 31.6 deg |
| **beach2** | **-57.94** | **9.3 deg** |

**This is a real choice and not a rendering artefact.** If Reid picks `beach2` the
spawn is in low golden evening light permanently, at every hour of every day,
because that is the highest the sun ever gets there. Its frame reads warmer and
moodier than `beach` for exactly that reason. Some people will want that. It
should be a decision rather than a surprise, and it is the sort of thing that
would otherwise be discovered a week after the spawn moved.

`beach` at 31.6 deg is the better sand option on this criterion as well as on
the flatness numbers, which is convenient rather than a coincidence: the probe's
own latitude band stops at 60 degrees.

**Two instrument notes from the same pass**, both worth keeping:

1. **The first batch of shots came back at night, and the cause was a real
   misuse rather than a wrong flag.** `__of.setTime(t)` sets one sun vector in
   the BODY frame, so a fixed `t` is a fixed direction in space and therefore a
   *different local time at every longitude*: `t = 0.32` is mid-morning at lon
   144 and the middle of the night at lon -86. There is no `?time=` or `?sun=`
   flag; `setTime` was already the right handle, used wrongly. The probe now
   sweeps `t` and takes each site's own local noon, and solves the camera yaw so
   the sun is over the same shoulder in every frame. Comparable frames of
   different longitudes need per-site solar time, not a shared constant.
2. **`snowBatchesDrawn` is unreliable and should not be quoted.** It reads
   `OF_Ice:744` at every site including both beaches, which contradicts the
   pixels and the biome rule. Every run boots at the Mountains spawn before
   teleporting, so it is almost certainly a batch allocated at boot and never
   released rather than live geometry at the destination. **A counter that
   reports geometry which is not on screen is the DW-28 class**: it would happily
   confirm snow at a site that cannot place a single flake. The biome index and
   the pixels are the evidence; that counter is not.


#### The candidates

Every site below is under the prop snow line. `rawRel` is the number `biomeAt`
classifies on, and 0.330 is the Mountains boundary. Pictures are
`docs/screenshots/WG53_site_<name>.png`, all shot in daylight at eye height with
the same camera and settings, with the HUD left on so the biome index, latitude,
longitude and altitude can be read back off each frame.

**Both owed repairs are DONE (WG-57).** `probePadHere` now moves `pondDir` with
`homeDir` and reports the pad's own flatness and the ponded flatness as two
named numbers, and `printHomeDirLiteral` emits both literals with the separation
checked against the tolerance the test pins. Running the tool over the current
spawn re-emits both shipped literals byte for byte, so the output is
paste-ready.
`homeDir` is encoded in 18 places and two ctests pin the current literal by name

against the shipping default, at a settled frame with the scatter backlog
drained at every site. `?canopy=0` reproduces the pre-change build's triangle
count to the unit at all seven sites, so the control is not merely a flag, it is
verified equal to the world before this tier existed.

| site | biome | ground m | calls | triangles | canopy trees | what the treeline does |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rn15 (invariant camera) | Hills | 860.9 | 70 to 78 | 989,052 to **983,882** | 462 | below it: 0% of cells bare |
| current spawn | Mountains | 4,667.8 | 65 to 65 | 619,445 to **619,445** | 0 | above it: 100% bare |
| forest | Forest | 27.3 | 78 to 78 | 1,024,404 to **1,157,625** | 1,560 | below it: 0% bare |
| plains | Plains | 331.8 | 78 to 86 | 2,652,309 to **2,613,292** | 260 | below it: 0% bare |
| hills | Hills | 2,077.2 | 70 to 70 | 838,677 to **838,677** | 0 | above it: 100% bare |

Programs (37 or 50, order dependent within a session and identical row for row
across the pair), geometries (52) and VRAM (104.2 MB) do not move anywhere.

**What this does at whichever site Reid picks**, which is the question section
6.1 leaves open:

- **Beach** (his desert option, and the one the survey said to show him first):
  nothing changes. Zero trees, zero triangles, zero draw calls, bit-identical
  frame. The pale sand stays pale sand. If he wants a treeline BEHIND the beach
  it is one density row and it is deliberately not there today.
- **Plains**: 260 trees as isolated copses in open grass, and the frame gets
  1.47% CHEAPER, because the understorey those copses shade costs more than they
  do. This is also the site the survey flagged as the most expensive frame of
  the set at 2.66 M triangles, so the direction is the useful one.
- **Hills at 1,897 m (`hills2`)**: the spawn lands ON the treeline. 79% of
  offered cells are above it, so the picture is open ground with stragglers and
  wooded slopes falling away below. This is the most dramatic of the seven and
  it is the one to show him if he wants a view.
- **Hills at 2,077 m (`hills`, the survey's own recommendation on the numbers)**:
  bare, bit-identical frame. A spawn there has no forest in it at all and the
  nearest trees are downhill. That is correct and it is worth him knowing before
  he picks, because it is the one site where this pass is invisible from the pad.
- **Forest at 27 m**: the closed forest, and the only site that pays, at +13.0%.
- **The current Mountains spawn**: bit-identical. Nothing here moves the spawn or
  depends on where it ends up.

**Cost decomposition**, from `?canopyshade=0` (trees on, the understorey they
shade left at full density):

| site | trees alone | understorey shade | net |
|---|---|---|---|
| forest | +206,600 | -73,379 | **+133,221** |
| rn15 | +57,974 | -63,144 | **-5,170** |
| plains | +35,078 | -74,095 | **-39,017** |

**What is NOT delivered, stated plainly.** The canopy reaches 620 m. That puts a
treeline on the near ridges and fills the middle distance, and it does not reach
a horizon two kilometres away on a body where that much ground is visible. No
arithmetic gets it there: at 2 km the same density is fifteen times the
instances, and every tree is drawn four times because a prop batch has both
frustum-culling flags off and casts into three cascades. **A horizon treeline
needs a far-field impostor layer and this client has none** (there is no
billboard, impostor or card mechanism anywhere in `web/src/render`; the only
authored impostors in the repo are the harvest trees' 16-triangle `_LOD2` nodes,
which `NodeBatch.ts:190` never loads because its regex matches `_LOD0` only).
That is a rendering-lane ask and it is recorded as one rather than half built.

**Two other findings logged unfixed:**

- **`Scatter.replace` has no caller.** It is the documented rebase path
  ("re-derive every instance matrix from its chunk's anchor, THE rebase path"),
  `TerrainStream.onOriginRebased` re-places every `ChunkView`, and nothing tells
  the scatter. Chunks inside the scatter radius survive a rebase rather than
  being dropped, so their instance matrices should be stale by the rebase delta.
  `FloatingOrigin.thresholdM` is 4,000 m and every probe teleports rather than
  walking, which is exactly why nothing has seen it. **NOT confirmed by
  measurement and therefore not fixed here**; it needs a driven 4 km walk.
- **Both slope gates refuse nothing.** `CANOPY_MIN_SLOPE_COS` (44 degrees)
  refused 0 of 271,815 offered cells and `MIN_SLOPE_COS` (57 degrees, in force
  for every prop since RN-7) refused 0 as well, across all seven sites including
  the Mountains spawn. Counters for both now ship, so the next reader inherits
  the measurement rather than the comment.

### 6.3 WG-64 to WG-66: the dead rebase path, and the assumption that hid it

Section 6.2 logged this as suspected and explicitly NOT confirmed. It is now
confirmed, fixed and controlled. `probes/scatterrebase.js` is the instrument and
it is the first probe on this project that WALKS to its condition rather than
teleporting to it.

**The measurement**, one binary, driven sprint at the Forest site, input tape
held for the whole run, 88 slices of 5 sim seconds:

| t (s) | metres | rebases | staleMaxM | staleChunks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | 44.8 | 2 | 0.000000 | 0 |
| ... 86 slices, every one 0.000000 ... | | | | |
| 430 | 3,954.8 | 2 | 0.000000 | 0 |
| **435** | **4,000.8** | **3** | **4,000.089191** | **43 of 43** |
| 440 | 4,046.8 | 3 | 4,000.089191 | 23 |

The displacement equals the rebase delta to six decimals, and it decays instead
of snapping back because a chunk is only re-placed when it is next rebuilt, at
one chunk per frame.

**The generalisable finding is not the bug.** It is that the entire probe suite
shares a locomotion assumption. A teleport moves the player 4 km in one step,
every resident chunk falls outside the scatter radius, and the stale matrices
are dropped before a frame is drawn, so the defect is unreachable by the only
motion any probe uses. `probes/walk.js` does walk, and does read `rebases`, but
reads it as streaming churn and never asks where the props went. Any subsystem
that is only ever exercised through a shortcut has this shape available to it.

**Two instrument notes worth keeping:**

- The first capture came back at NIGHT, because seven sim minutes of walking
  moves the clock, and nothing in the frame could be read. The probe now solves
  local noon before the shot using `stats()` calls that advance zero frames,
  which matters here more than usual: any frame it ran would let the scatter
  rebuild a chunk and heal the very state the picture is of.
- The correct value is a hard 0.000000 and is not a threshold anybody tuned.
  `Scatter.write` and `ChunkView.place` both go through the same f64 `toEngine`
  subtraction, so a re-placed chunk is exact; `STALE_EPS_M` is 1 mm and exists
  only so a float32 round trip cannot trip the counter, against a failure
  measured in kilometres.

**Still open, and it is now a question for other domains rather than for me:**
the scatter was one of several things that cache an engine transform. The other
`OriginRebased` subscribers (`zfight`, `voxelMesh`, `levelRing`, the water
surface, the streamer itself) are all wired; nothing here audits whether every
consumer that composes from `ChunkView.pos` is on the new hook. A driven walk is
now cheap, and the same probe shape would answer it for buildings, belts and
machines.

### 6.5 WG-91 to WG-100: cashing in the new art, and the two rows I refused to write

All numbers from ONE binary: a `git archive HEAD web` scratch tree (`web/dist-wgwire`) carrying HEAD plus exactly nine of my files and NOTHING else, verified file by file against HEAD by sha256 before every run. That isolation was not ceremony: two sibling lanes were live in `Config.ts`, `Boot.ts`, `run.mjs` and `NodeField.ts` while this ran, and the first measurement attempt died on a `TREE_RADIUS_M` import from a `TreeTuning.ts` that does not exist at HEAD. Dev-served on port 5231 (`--strictPort`, my own process; Reid's 4200 untouched), each site at its own local noon, chunk set converged and both backlogs drained.

**The invariant table both ways, and the pass is NET CHEAPER.** Each control is a one-binary URL flag except `Plains_PebbleA`, whose control restores its row in the scratch tree (WG-67's precedent).

| site | biome | HEAD tables | shipped | delta | calls HEAD/ship |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rn15 | Hills | 1,123,074 | 1,123,074 | **0** | 80/80 |
| current | Mountains | 619,981 | 627,981 | **+8,000** | 59/59 |
| forest | Forest | 1,591,650 | 1,587,471 | **-4,179** | 76/76 |
| plains | Plains | 2,701,128 | 2,673,936 | **-27,192** | 88/84 |
| hills | Hills | 922,124 | 922,124 | **0** | 72/72 |

Net **-23,371 triangles and -4 draw calls**. Programs (38 or 50, order-dependent), geometries (52), textures (99/101/105) and VRAM (104.2 MB) do not move anywhere, row for row, in any configuration measured this pass, including the one that adds a whole new 167 kB node file.

**WG-94, THE SPIRE, AND THE CONTROL IS THE PROOF.** `rock_spire.glb` is a second `NodeArt.ART[NODE_KIND.Rock]` entry with `weight` 0.5 and `biomes: [Mountains]`, both new optional fields on `NodeArt`. `?spires=0` splices it OUT of the list rather than skipping it at pick time, so the control also removes the fetch (`assets.files` 42 with, 41 without), which a pick-time skip could not. With spires off, **rn15, forest, plains and hills are bit-identical to the triangle** and Mountains reads `BoulderStone=49`; with them on the same 49 rocks read `BoulderStone=39 + RockSpire=10`, drawn 2.04 m to 3.34 m. The weighted draw is bit-identical to the uniform floor it replaces wherever every weight is absent and no biome gate is set, which is why the two trees and every ore boulder still pick the file they picked before. `hitUpM` 1.15 is derived from the player's arm and not from 0.55 of the asset: at 3.9 m placed, 0.55 of its own height is 2.15 m, over the player's head. Driven proof: `probes/wgart.js` walked to one, stood 2.88 m off, and the crosshair returned `Stone, 100% remaining` (`WG94_spire_after.png`).

**WG-98, THE RAISED ROCK BUDGETS VERIFIED IN THE CLIENT, AND THE ROCK LANE'S ARITHMETIC IS LOW BY 4x.** RN-243 priced the stone raise (142 to 426) at "about 16.5k triangles, 1.7% to 3.0% of frame" and said plainly it was unmeasured. Measured: **a harvest node's LOD0 is drawn FOUR times**, once in the near pass and once in each of three shadow cascades (`NodeBatch.makeBatch` sets `castShadow = true`). The proof is exact rather than inferred: `?rocks=0` at four sites gives **1,704 triangles per stone boulder, which is 426 x 4 to the unit**, and the spire pair moves Mountains by exactly `10 x (626 - 426) x 4 = 8,000`. The whole rock layer therefore costs 4.70% (rn15), **11.6% (Mountains)**, 1.07% (forest), 1.15% (plains) and 4.99% (hills) of frame triangles, and the boulder raise alone is about four times its estimate. Not a defect in the raise, which ART-DIRECTION.md licenses; a correction to its price. The shadow multiplier is the single biggest lever anyone has on node cost.

**WG-91, THE FOREST FLOOR, AND THE FRAME DISAGREES WITH THE PATCH ARITHMETIC IN SIGN.** `FOREST_DETAIL` gives Forest its own understorey mix at the same pre-scale sum (538,000, verified to the unit) over the same seven species. **`propsPlaced` is IDENTICAL at 25,711 both ways**, which is the instance-neutrality claim measured rather than argued. The understorey lane predicted +0.16% of patch triangles; the arithmetic on the shipped per-card counts (18/40/18/42/30/36/30) gives **+0.2022%** (+124.9 triangles on 694 m2, and no rounding convention reproduces +0.16%); the running frame gives **-4,179, i.e. -0.26%**. Both are right about different things: the patch is LOD0 cards at full density, the frame is the whole graded 78 m ring where most cards draw LOD2 and the LOD2 counts are nearly uniform, so re-weighting is almost free there while `Detail_FlowerSprig`'s drop removes real two-primitive instances (`propInstances` -229). The four unchanged sites reading bit-identical across four independent runs is what says -4,179 is signal rather than noise. Visible in the pair `WG91_forestfloor_{before,after}.png`: upright strokes with bare ground between them become low broad cover with litter.

**WG-92, THE TWO ROWS I REFUSED TO WRITE, AND THIS IS THE FINDING OF THE PASS.** RN-245's three Mountains stems (`Mtn_ScreeSheet` 0.155 m, `Mtn_TalusFan` 0.168, `Mtn_FrostShards` 0.160) get NO registry row. Wired at the retired pair's own density (1,820 pre-scale, exactly what WG-68 retired) they cost **+218,096 triangles (+34.7%) and +8 draw calls** at Mountains, and the standing frame at 1,820, at 1,050, at 520 and at ZERO is the same picture. The near-ground pair settles it: pitched 38 degrees at the dirt, **360 placed instances and +59,936 DRAWN triangles produce a frame I cannot tell from the frame without them** (`WG92_mtnclose_{none,520}.png`; the density ladder is `WG92_mtn_{after,1050,520,none}.png`). The triangle delta proves they are drawn, so this is not "they failed to place".

The mechanism is the part worth keeping: a 1.75 x 1.50 m plate that is 0.155 m tall has almost no projected area from a 1.66 m eye, and the size band it occupies is ALREADY filled by `Detail_PebbleScatter`, which Mountains runs at 64,000 pre-scale in `DRY_DETAIL`, i.e. **70x the scree ask**. **RN-247's corrected 0.174 m decoration cap and the visibility floor have collided**: under the cap a rock decoration cannot be seen, over it it lies to the crosshair, and the band between them is empty. Mountains' rock read therefore has to come from the NODES and from the terrain material, and the three stems are an asset-lane question rather than a client row. This is INSTRUMENTS.md's shape with the sign flipped: not a term measured where it cannot work, but a term ADDED where its read was already provided.

**WG-93, `Plains_PebbleA` DROPPED, and it is the cheapest win in the pass.** Authored 0.18 m and drawn to 0.279 m by `ScatterLook.scaleFor`'s 1.25 x 1.24, i.e. above the smallest harvestable silhouette, so it lies to a crosshair that cannot catch it. Dropping the row is **-27,192 triangles and -4 draw calls** at Plains, the most expensive site in the survey. The read survives on `Detail_PebbleScatter` at 26x the density. Re-authoring it under 0.174 m restores the row in one line, and that ask is Admin's to route.

**WG-97, THE SEEDED-SIBLING EVALUATION, ANSWERED WITH THE SPIRE AS THE EXPERIMENT.** Adding `rock_spire.glb` WAS the test of what a sibling file costs, because it is exactly that: a new node .glb whose materials already exist in the node batches. Measured both ways: **draw calls +0** (59/59 at Mountains; `nodeBatches` is 4 with and without), **geometries +0** (52, because a `BatchedMesh` is one geometry however many sub-geometries it holds), **VRAM +0.0 MB** on the published estimate (104.2 either way; the true figure is about 0.12 MB, under its rounding), **asset load +0 measurable** (3,105 ms against 3,140 ms, the difference in the wrong direction, which is the tell that it is noise), and **frame triangles move only by the DIFFERENCE between the two forms**, never by their sum, because a sibling is drawn INSTEAD of the original. **So the answer is yes, and the only real cost is download bytes.** One extra seed across all four ores is +470,860 B on a 743,412 B node payload; three extra seeds is +1.41 MB against 8.49 MB of total assets. **Recommendation: N=2 (one sibling per ore) unconditionally; N=4 only after the dead payload below is stripped**, because 35% of every sibling's bytes would be geometry nothing can select.

**WG-96, THE DEAD LOD PAYLOAD, MEASURED AND CLASSIFIED HONESTLY.** `NodeBatch.build` matches `_LOD0` only and admits only Full/Half/Low, so every `_LOD1`, `_LOD2` and `Stump_*` mesh in a node file is downloaded, parsed into a `BufferGeometry`, and never selected. Across the seven node files: **270,758 B of BIN, 29.73% of the shipped node payload and 35.45% of its geometry bytes**, plus about 84 kB of JSON descriptors (estimated; the BIN figure is exact, and the LOD0 and LOD1/LOD2 bufferView sets are disjoint in all seven files, so the subtraction hides nothing). For the six files actually fetched today it is 206,066 B, 27.72%. **The cost is NOT VRAM and must not be claimed as such**: three.js r0.185 creates a GL buffer in `WebGLAttributes.update`, reached only from `renderBufferDirect`, and these geometries are never submitted, so no buffer is ever allocated. It is network bytes, parse time, and JS heap retained for the session (`NodeField.templates` holds a strong reference and there is no `dispose`). **Owner note: the sibling tree lane landed `NODE_LOD1_M`/`NODE_LOD2_M` in `NodeBatch` mid-pass**, which if it selects those tiers converts dead payload into live payload and closes this item. That is theirs; it should be confirmed rather than assumed.

**WG-99, AN INSTRUMENT DEFECT FOUND BY USING IT.** `PropLibrary.stats().perMaterial[].live` is published as a per-role live count and is in fact a **monotonic high-water mark**: `release()` pushes the slot onto `free` and decrements `instancesLive` but never decrements `b.live`, and `acquire()` only increments it when it does not reuse a free slot. The tell was two different biomes reporting byte-identical per-material strings while `instances` differed by 3.3x. It is correct for the DW-28 question it was written for (what capacity does this role need) and wrong for "what is on screen here", which is INSTRUMENTS.md's "a value published for rendering is not automatically fit to measure with". The valid live counters are `props.instances` and `props.propsPlaced`, and this pass uses only those. Also noted: `stats().assets.bytes` reads 0 and is not populated.

**WG-100, `bush_scrub` IS NOT A SCATTER PROP AND MUST NOT GET A `Registry.ts` ROW.** `contracts.json` says `node_kind: "Tree"`, its `lod_nodes` are Full/Half/Low variants, its sockets are `socket_hit` and `socket_item_pop`, its clip is `Tree_Sway`, and it ships a `col_BushScrub`. Every one of those is a harvest-node fingerprint and none is a prop's. A biome-prop row would register `BushScrub_Full`, `BushScrub_Half` and `BushScrub_Low` as three unrelated stems and scatter the depleted ones as scenery. It belongs as a third `ART[NODE_KIND.Tree]` entry, which is the sibling tree lane's table, and the `weight`/`biomes` mechanism WG-94 added is exactly what stops that entry turning one tree in three into a 0.9 m bush.

**A property of the tier sampler, recorded because it cost a confused minute:** changing ANY biome prop density re-rolls which spec every draw in that biome lands on (`ScatterEmit.pickClustered` walks cumulative density), so a snow patch can move when a scree density changes. Two frames at different densities are not registered pictures of the same world.

**Known debts, stated:** the three Mountains stems and their cap collision are handed up rather than solved; the `Plains_PebbleA` re-author is an asset ask; `Registry.ts` was brought back under the 400-line cap by moving rationale into this file rather than by a file split, deliberately, because splitting the one file two world-gen lanes are both editing is the worst possible timing. Repo-wide `check-limits` is unchanged at 25 violations, none of them new.


### 6.6 WG-116 to WG-122: every tree is a node, and the LOD that made it affordable

All numbers from ONE binary (HEAD `cb160d6` plus this lane's files), a scratch
tree frozen with `git archive HEAD web` and served on a lane-private port, each
site at its own local noon with the scatter and tree backlogs drained. The
settle is 660 frames and that is a correction: `StatsProbe` keeps a 600-frame
ROLLING ring, so a 60-frame settle read a p50 that was nine tenths queue-drain
frames and made the tree world look twice as expensive as its control AT SITES
WITH NO TREES IN THEM. Frame time on this headless harness is still too noisy at
the 3 to 15 ms scale to attribute a few ms to anything, and it is reported
below only where the sign is unambiguous; the triangle and draw-call invariants
are the measurements this pass rests on.

**THE CRUX: what the LOD and the culling are worth** (Forest, 2,226 tree nodes
in a 620 m ring, one binary, four flag combinations):

| node path | triangles | vs LOD0-only | calls |
|---|---|---|---|
| `?nodelod=0 --nodecull=0` (what HEAD's node path does) | 7,558,472 | | 76 |
| `?nodecull=0` (LODs on) | 1,895,584 | **-74.9%** | 76 |
| `?nodelod=0` (culling on, one tier) | 1,871,556 | -75.2% | 71 |
| **shipping** (both on) | **1,365,739** | **-81.9%** | 71 |

Measured at the SHIPPING scale band. The same four rows at the band this pass
first shipped (WG-119, before the heights were re-derived off the bytes) read
7,574,172 / 1,718,204 / 1,747,331 / 1,313,024, i.e. taller trees cost 52,715
triangles and moved the saving by 0.8 of a point; the shape of the answer does
not depend on the correction and both sets are recorded so neither can be quoted
as the other.

The LOD does the heavy lifting and **it is an asset the project already paid
for**: every node `.glb` ships `_LOD0`, `_LOD1` and `_LOD2` meshes (the trees
ship twelve meshes each) and `NodeBatch.ts:191` matched `_LOD0` only, so a
791-triangle harvest tree drew its near geometry at 600 m while the 16-triangle
impostor in the same file was dead bytes. Loading them is a second axis on the
geometry id that the depletion variant already switches, so a tier change is one
integer write and no re-snap. Per-instance culling is the second, smaller half,
and the comment it replaces was RIGHT WHEN WRITTEN ("per-instance culling costs
more than it saves at this object count", written of the clearing's two dozen
nodes). **This closes R10 and it pays at sites with no trees at all**: the rocks,
the WG-94 spires and the spawn clearing gain 111,734 (Mountains spawn), 134,574
(hills) and 135,113 (beach) triangles from tiers they always had.

**The invariant table both ways, at all eight sites** (HEAD = `?trees=0
--canopy=620`, the WG-59 world; ship = the default):

| site | biome | ground m | trees | HEAD tris | ship tris | delta | calls HEAD/ship |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rn15 | Hills | 860.9 | 799 | 980,891 | 965,364 | **-15,527** | 67/65 |
| current | Mountains | 4,667.8 | 0 | 518,847 | 518,647 | -200 | 57/57 |
| forest | Forest | 27.3 | 2,226 | 1,478,045 | 1,365,739 | **-112,306** | 63/71 |
| plains | Plains | 331.8 | 232 | 2,555,711 | 2,565,183 | +9,472 | 71/69 |
| hills | Hills | 2,077.2 | 0 | 787,550 | 787,550 | **0** | 59/59 |
| hills2 | Hills | 1,897.2 | 1 | 808,101 | 807,869 | -232 | 67/59 |
| beach | Beach | 12.2 | 0 | 780,148 | 780,148 | **0** | 59/59 |
| beach2 | Beach | 8.3 | 0 | 739,272 | 739,272 | **0** | 59/59 |

Programs, geometries and VRAM (104.2 MB) are IDENTICAL across HEAD, bare and
shipping at every site. Textures read 99/95/95 at the first two sites, which is
`props_canopy.glb` no longer being fetched, and converge once the other atlases
load. The +8 calls at Forest are node batches appearing in shadow cascades that
previously held no instance; that attribution is INFERRED from the pattern (the
same batches, more spread) and is not separately measured.

**THE NEGATIVE CONTROL, and it is the strongest one this lane has run.** A
second scratch tree frozen at pure `HEAD` was served beside the first and both
were driven by the same probe (`probes/treectl.js`, which reads nothing this
pass added). `--trees=0 --canopy=620 --nodelod=0 --nodecull=0` against pure HEAD:
**identical on every column at 8 of 8 sites** (calls, triangles, programs,
geometries, textures, VRAM, node pool count, instances, batches, refused, prop
instances, canopy props). An earlier run of the same pair differed by exactly
+2,048 at two sites, which is one terrain chunk's interior (32x32x2) and was
streaming variance between two browser sessions, not code; it disappeared on the
re-freeze and is recorded because the number looks like a bug and is not.

**What Reid sees at each candidate spawn** (trees live in the ring / realised per
km2; delivery is placed over expected over the SAME cells):

| site | trees | /km2 | delivery | read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| forest (Forest) | 2,226 | 1,843 | 1.0000 | closed forest, the densest of the seven, `WG117_forest_trees.png` |
| rn15 (Hills, 861 m) | 799 | 662 | 0.9891 | woodland below the treeline; 13 trees inside 50 m, 16 more to 100 m |
| plains (Plains) | 232 | 192 | 0.9608 | isolated copses in open grass |
| hills2 (Hills, 1,897 m) | 1 | 0.8 | 1.61 | ON the treeline: open ground with one straggler |
| current (Mountains, 4,668 m) | 0 | 0 | n/a | bare, and bare BECAUSE the altitude term ran (4,633 cells) |
| hills (Hills, 2,077 m) | 0 | 0 | n/a | above the treeline; frame bit-identical to HEAD |
| beach / beach2 (Beach) | 0 | 0 | n/a | THE DESERT is untouched; frames bit-identical to HEAD |

Delivery at hills2 is 1.61 over an expectation of 0.62 trees, which is one tree
against a fractional ask and is quoted rather than hidden. Every other site with
trees sits inside its fair-draw 4-sigma band with both ratio sides over the same
cells (RN-15's lesson).

**THE REFUSALS, and one of them was broken until a count caught it.**

| gate | reachable? | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| treeline | YES | 4,633 cells emptied at the Mountains spawn, live 0 |
| standing water | YES | 4 cells refused at the spawn pond |
| clearing keep-out | YES | 14 refusals at a Forest spawn |
| slope | **NO, and it cannot be** | see below |

The clearing keep-out read 0 at a spawn where arithmetic said it must fire about
six times, and the cause is worth keeping: it compared a candidate expressed as
a unit direction times the body radius against a placed node's position ON THE
SURFACE, which at Forest is 27 m further out, so every comparison beat a 6 m
keep-out before the two were even in the same place. A zero from a gate that
cannot fire reads exactly like a zero from a gate with nothing to catch.

**THE SLOPE GATE CANNOT FIRE, and this time that is proved rather than
observed.** `TREE_MIN_SLOPE_COS` refused 0 at all eight sites, which is the same
reading WG-63 got from the canopy tier's copy of the same constant. Eight gentle
sites cannot tell "the gate works" from "the gate is not wired", so
`probes/treeslope.js` went looking: a coarse global pass hill-climbed to a sample
spacing finer than the gate's own 3 m arm, 26,120 oracle samples. The steepest
ground ANYWHERE on Forge is **57.92 degrees** (lat 58.0, lon 16.5, 2,865 m) and
the steepest ground BELOW THE TREELINE is **29.15 degrees** (lat -77.0, lon 2.6,
1,676 m), against a 43.95 degree gate. Everything steep is bare because it is
high. The constant is KEPT, for one case the census cannot reach: the slope is
sampled through `editsHandle`, so a dug pit or a levelled terrace makes locally
steep ground under a live tree ring. **That case is untested and is owed.**
Lowering the constant until the counter moved was rejected outright: it would
refuse trees on ground that is fine for trees, to flatter an instrument.

**SIZE IS YIELD, and the band came off the bytes on the second attempt.** A
tree's art scale is a linear map of /core's grade, and grade is the same number
`InitialAmount` is multiplied by, so the world holds trees from 20.0 to 39.98
wood and the bigger one is worth more. The first band was derived from
`NodeField`'s header calling a harvest tree "a 6.5 m tree"; measured off the
shipped glb the conifer is 6.50 m and the broadleaf is **5.00 m**, against the
retired canopy species' 12.00, 16.50 and 10.50 m, so the median drawn tree was a
third too short and `WG117_rn15_trees.png` showed a hillside of scrub while
every counter read 0.9891. Re-derived to [1.25, 2.45] the conifer spans 7.0 to
18.2 m (median 12.0) and the broadleaf 5.4 to 14.0 m. Cost of the correction:
+8,383 triangles at rn15 and +45,943 at forest, both still well under HEAD.

**PERSISTENCE, cell-keyed, proven by a real reload.** `SaveSlot.trees` is
`[latCell, lonCell, slot, remaining]`, additive and optional, and SAVE_VERSION is
deliberately unmoved (WG-29's rule: the check is `!==`, so a bump orphans every
existing world). It rides the `writeSlot` choke point that PS-13 to PS-15
established, so both save routes carry it and no second writer was introduced.
`reload.mjs --setup=probes/treereload.js` walks 70.1 m to a STREAMED tree (the
70 m floor is asserted, because chopping one of the clearing's spiral trees would
prove the OLD index-keyed path and pass while this one was broken), swings three
times for 18 wood, saves, and does a real `page.reload()`. **The tree comes back
at the same body-frame position to the bit and at `remaining`
12.358138084411621 exactly**, and it comes back through the PENDING path
(`trees: 0, treesPending: 1` in the ledger, `drainedOnRestore: 1`), which is the
harder half: the diff was parked at load and applied at the moment the tree
materialised. `--params=` was added to `reload.mjs` for this, because the default
spawn is above the treeline and a tree probe there would have had nothing to chop.

**SCALING, and the debt this pass pays and the one it leaves.** `known` is
bounded by the ring rather than by the walk: a cell that streams out drops its
UNTOUCHED trees (`forget`), because a full tree carries no diff and regenerates
identically, and only a chopped one is kept for ever. Measured over the eight-site
tour: `known` equals `live` at every site and `forgotten` climbs to 3,465, so a
long walk does not grow the map or the autosave. **`RockField` still keeps every
rock it ever materialised and should inherit this**; at 49 rocks it does not
matter and at tree densities it would. `NodeField.update` still pays one WASM
state read per PRESENTED node per frame (about 2,200 at Forest), which is WG-72's
named debt now much larger; it did not show above the harness noise and is left
measured rather than fixed.

**Pictures beside numbers** (DW-7, and this pass needed it twice):
`docs/screenshots/WG117_{forest,rn15}_{trees,head}.png` are the pairs at the two
sites that change, and `WG117_forest_trees.png` is the feature in one frame: a
closed forest with the accent prompt reading "Left click Harvest Wood, 100%
remaining" on a tree that used to be scenery. `WG117_forest_bare.png` is the
`?trees=0` control.

### 6.8 WG-150: the runtime body-switch seam, and why this lane should not own it end to end

Admin asked me to establish the seam before building anything and said that "this is a core-engine job and here is exactly where the boundary is" would be a completed brief. **That is the answer.** The terrain half is mine and is well bounded; the lifecycle half is not mine, and the highest-value pieces of it are **owned by nobody**.

**Four structural facts decide it, and none of them is about terrain.**
1. **`chunkKey` carries no body.** `web/src/world/ChunkFormat.ts:109-111` is `` `${faceId}:${depth}:${qx}:${qy}` ``. Forge chunk `2:8:130:44` and Cinder chunk `2:8:130:44` are **the same string**, and that string is the map key for `TerrainStream.views`, the worker's evicted list, and `Scatter.placed`. This one is mine to fix.
2. **Nothing in this client has ever taken anything apart while the loop is running.** `TerrainStream.dispose()` (`TerrainStream.ts:412-419`) exists and **has never had a caller**. The only existing answer to "change the world" is `window.location.reload()` at `MenuBoot.ts:48`.
3. **`Events` has no unsubscribe.** Seven objects subscribe to `OriginRebased`; rebuilding any of them leaks the old handler. `FloatingOrigin.ts` itself is clean and body-agnostic (it imports only `Events` and `Vec3d`), so the class is right and the *lifecycle around it* is the problem.
4. **`SaveSlot` has no body field at all** (`SaveGame.ts:162-277`), `VoxelEdits` is one global set created by `_of_edits_create()` with no body argument, and the rock/tree depletion rows are keyed by a **body-radius-derived lattice cell**. A Forge tunnel would reappear as a hole at the same absolute cell on Cinder.

**Mine, entirely inside `web/src/world/**` and `web/src/workers/**`:** a re-init message on `ToTerrain` (with the three WASM handles actually destroyed first, since `terrain.worker.ts` destroys none of them today); making `TerrainStream.onMessage` stop silently dropping a second `inited`; a drop-all-residents operation beside the per-chunk `evict`; a body discriminator in `chunkKey`; re-seating `SurfaceOracle.body` / `WaterOracle.body` and rebuilding `WaterOracle.disc`; `MapTerrain`'s cache key and handle; `RockField.forget` (already owed as R14) and body-tagging both lattice keys.

**Not mine, and the escalation list in priority order:** `Services.body` is a `readonly` scalar on a 45-field all-`readonly` record with **no teardown path of any kind** (unowned; core-engine by subsystem). The origin re-seat and the seven-subscriber rebase contract (core-engine by subsystem, **unowned by file**: `FloatingOrigin.ts` carries zero decision tags and is named in no controller doc). `uMaxRelief` and the wet band are **baked into two `ShaderMaterial`s at creation** and a `BatchedMesh` binds one material for its lifetime (rendering). About a dozen gameplay, factory-sim and physics collaborators hold copied `bodyHandle`/`bodyRadiusM` snapshots. The voxel-edit and save-schema question is persistence's. And `MapBoot.ts:133,171` runs a **second body-id convention** (`kind === 'moon' ? 1 : 0`) that would have to move in lockstep with `cfg.bodyId`.

**Recommendation: core-engine leads a lifecycle pass (Services mutability, teardown, event unsubscribe, frame ordering) and I do the terrain half underneath it.** I can start on the parts that are unambiguously mine and are useful regardless of who leads, above all the body discriminator in `chunkKey` and the worker handle leak, because both are latent defects today rather than only obstacles to switching.

### 6.9 WG-220 to WG-227: the canopy table was an order of magnitude low, and no density reaches forest cover one-instance-per-tree

`lane/canopy-density`, base `origin/main` at faa31238, 2026-08-20. **WG-220 to
WG-227 used; WG-228 and WG-229 surrendered unused, abandoned per NUMBERS.md
rule 4.** Frames `docs/screenshots/WG2220_*`. Real Windows D3D11 (ANGLE, RTX
4060 Ti), 1600x900.

**THE RAILS.** TWO owned `vite preview` servers, `--strictPort`, because this
lane's before-arm is a different BUILD and not a page param: the after build on
`127.0.0.1:5312` out of this worktree's own `dist` (sentinel
`dist/of-sentinel-wg220.txt`), the pristine `faa31238` build on
`127.0.0.1:5313` out of `web/dist-before-wg220` (sentinel
`of-sentinel-wg220-before.txt`, gitignored by the repo's own `web/dist-*/`
rule). Both sentinels written locally and **fetched back over their own port**
before any measurement, and the two builds proved distinct by their entry
hashes over the wire (`index-CIDdYmTJ.js` against `index-CBoaczWD.js`).
Ownership confirmed by `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 5312` (PID 35268),
killed by that PID. `web/wasm/dist` and `test/expected.json` untouched; no file
in `web/src/render/` touched.

#### 6.9.1 WG-222, the ask: 38 stems a hectare is savanna

`C()` figures in `Registry.ts` are instances per square kilometre after
`DENSITY_SCALE`, and a square kilometre is a hundred hectares. The shipped
FOREST row was 3,840 per km2, i.e. **38 canopy stems a hectare**. Real mature
temperate forest carries 200 to 400 and open woodland 60 to 120. RN-2225 built
the whole far tier correctly and then photographed a savanna, and its own
judgement routed this ("what limits the picture is the density the planet asks
for. That is world-gen's table").

The ask is **x6** across the four canopy biomes: FOREST 23,040 per km2 (230 a
hectare), HILLS 7,200 (72), PLAINS 2,520 (25, wooded grassland), MOUNTAIN 2,880
at the foot, nearly all of which the treeline deletes. **These are now
CLOSED-STAND figures, which is a change of meaning and not only of magnitude**
(6.9.2): the row is the density on ground inside a closed stand of a closed
wood, and the biome average is 48.7 per cent of it, measured. Written that way
because "how thick is the forest where there is forest" is checkable against a
forestry table and "the average over a landscape including its fields" is not.

**AND THE MIX MOVES TO BROADLEAF, WHICH IS COVERAGE FOR FREE AND IS HALF THE
RESULT.** The eye reads crown cover, and `contracts.json` gives the three
species wildly different crowns: pine 3.85 x 2.55 m (7.71 m2), fir 2.9 x 2.2
(5.01 m2), broadleaf 8.4 x 10.5 (**69.27 m2, nine times the pine**). The old
Hills mix averages **22.56 m2** a tree; the same TOTAL density led by broadleaf
averages **44.38 m2**. That is 1.97x the crown cover for the identical instance
count, the identical triangle count and the identical frame cost. Forest goes
31.38 -> 44.01 m2 the same way. MOUNTAIN deliberately does not (no broadleaf
survives at the treeline, so its 6.87 m2 crown and its sparse flanks stand, and
the treeline stays observable there for WG-59's own recorded reason).

#### 6.9.2 WG-221, the grove mask: distribution, not just density

`STAND_M` is 165 m and was right for a 620 m ring seen from 1.6 m. Seen from
1,200 m over a 3.5 km disc it is FINE GRAIN, and a field whose only feature
size is 165 m averages to uniform speckle at exactly the range this tier exists
to fill. `GROVE_M` = **760 m** is a second, coarser field MULTIPLIED over the
stand field, not a fourth octave inside it, and the distinction is arithmetic:
folding another octave into `standAt` REDUCES total variance (a weighted sum of
independent fields concentrates about its mean), which is the opposite of what
patchiness needs. A product of two ramped fields has the variance of neither -
closed canopy needs every term to agree, open ground needs one to dissent, so
`canopyWeight` is now `stand x grove x treeline`, three terms each of which can
independently say "no trees here". `GROVE_FLOOR_W` 0.12, `GROVE_LO/HI`
0.40/0.62. Sampled in body-frame metres by the same `octave` the stand field
uses, so every word of WG-62's determinism argument carries: smooth rather than
hashed, LOD-independent, a pure function of position.

**THE HARVEST RING DOES NOT TAKE THE MASK, and the call site says so with a
literal 1** (`TreeField.buildCell`, `groveWeight(1) === 1`, so the two-term
weight is bit-for-bit what that line always computed). It is a design refusal
and not only a regression guard: the grove is 760 m and the harvest ring is
620 m, so a player stands inside about ONE grove cell, and masking this layer
would mean a spawn that rolls a clearing has no wood within walking distance
while the storyline's first hour is gathering wood.

**OWED AND NAMED:** the two layers now disagree about groves across the 550 to
690 m crossfade, so a player standing where the grove field says "open" sees
the harvest ring's normal density hand over to a thin far tier. RN-2228 built
that seam on the two densities being about equal across it. The fix is either a
harvest-side mask with a spawn guarantee, or a grove floor raised until the
disagreement is under the noise; both are decisions with consequences outside
this lane.

#### 6.9.3 WG-225, and the arithmetic that says a density alone cannot do this

Crown cover is density times crown area, and closing 40 per cent of the ground
with this world's 44 m2 crowns needs `-ln(0.6)/44e-6` = **11,600 trees per km2
standing at once**, which over the flyover's 43 km2 of visible canopy is half a
million instances, i.e. **115 ms of CPU in a 16.6 ms frame** at the measured
0.21 us a tree. **There is no density that reaches forest-credible cover
one-instance-per-tree at this range**, and that is the finding this lane exists
to write down.

What is available is crown SIZE, and it is available honestly because
`CANOPY_NEAR_M` (550 m) is already past `CANOPY_LOD3_M` (420 m): **every tree
this tier draws is already an impostor card**, a trunk stub and two crossed
quads, twelve triangles, no crown geometry at all. A card is a picture of
canopy, and past the near seam a card standing for nine trees is what every
impostor forest ever shipped has been. `CANOPY_FAR_GROW` = 1.6, i.e. 1.0x at
690 m rising to 2.6x at 3,500 m, uniform rather than horizontal-only
(`DETAIL_FAR_GROW` went horizontal because a grown 0.60 m card at the player's
feet became a 1.34 m one; nothing here is nearer than 690 m and a card
stretched in width alone reads as a mushroom the moment it has pixels).

**THE RAMP IS AGAINST THE FIXED RADIUS, NOT THE REALISED REACH, and that took a
second attempt.** Tied to the reach, a standing player at the 1,400 m ground
reach would see the FULL growth on their own treeline: 31 m trees beside 12 m
harvest trees, the seam WG-116 and RN-2228 spent two lanes making invisible.
Tied to `CANOPY_FAR_RADIUS_M`, the ground pose only reaches 0.25 of the ramp
(1.40x, a 17 m tree) while the aerial far field gets the whole 2.6x, and the
growth stays a function of the ground rather than of the observer.

#### 6.9.4 WG-224, the reach pays for the density, and the ground pose pays nothing

Cost is linear in the realised tree count, which is `density x admitted area`,
so at a fixed frame the two are exchangeable. `CANOPY_FAR_RADIUS_M` 4,200 ->
**3,500** (the annulus given up is 31 per cent of the disc and is where a 12 m
tree is 3.1 px through the deepest haze column in the frame);
`CANOPY_REACH_PER_ALT` 3.5 -> **2.92**, which is 3,500/1,200 and therefore
keeps that constant's own property that the altitude rule binds exactly at the
flyover pose it was derived from; `CANOPY_GROUND_REACH_M` 2,000 -> **1,400**,
which RN-2234's own table is the argument for (0 -> 2,000 m from a standing eye
moved the forestfloor box 22.82 to 23.05, a quarter of a luma count for 1.9 ms).
All three are budget numbers at today's card cost and **RN-2240's
single-material impostor divides the per-tree cost by four and buys every one
of them back.**

#### 6.9.5 The coverage arithmetic, stated

Crown area per species is `pi/4 * w * d` off `contracts.json` LOD0 dims, mixed
by the row's own density weights, times `E[w^2] = 1 + jitter^2/3 = 1.00653` for
the width jitter (`scaleFor` puts the height band on Y alone, so only `jitter`
widens a crown). Cover is the Boolean-model `1 - exp(-lambda A)`, the fraction
of ground under at least one crown, which is what "crown cover" means in
forestry and what the naive sum-of-crowns overstates once crowns overlap.

| flyover (Hills) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| canopy trees | 22,940 | 46,575 |
| canopy ground `canopyM2` | 62.63 km2 | 43.54 km2 |
| realised density | 366.3 /km2 | 1,069.7 /km2 |
| mean crown | 22.71 m2 | 44.67 m2 |
| `lambda A`, no far-grow | 0.00832 | 0.04778 |
| far-grow crown multiplier | 1.000 | **3.390** |
| **crown cover** | **0.83 %** | **14.95 %** |

**RN-2225's own 1.8 per cent was measured with a flat 8 m circular crown; the
density-weighted figure for the mix that was actually shipped is 0.83 per
cent**, so the picture was thinner than the record said. The 18-fold move
factorises exactly: **x2.92 realised density (the x6 ask through a 0.487 grove
mask) times x1.97 crown mix times x3.39 far-grow**, and the middle factor costs
nothing at all. Without the far-grow term the after arm is **4.67 per cent**,
published so the density claim stands on its own and cannot be flattered.

The far-grow multiplier is the area-weighted `E[(1 + 1.6t)^2]` over the density
fade, `1 + 0.9886 G + 0.3157 G^2` at G = 1.6, with the moments taken over
`r w(r)` on 690 to 3,500 m.

Forestfloor (Forest, standing eye, reach 1,400 m): 1,118 -> **3,464 trees per
km2**, mean crown 31.58 -> 44.30 m2, far-grow multiplier 1.390, **cover 3.47 ->
19.2 per cent**, and see the budget row: it costs nothing.

Three fresh processes per arm at the flyover returned `canopyProps` 46,575,
