#### 6.9.9 WG-223, the stand weight exposed for rendering's parked term

rendering.md 2.14.7b judged `CANOPY_SHADE` worse and named the fix as
world-gen's: "a `shadeW` that varies over tens of metres (crown-scale) instead
of one that saturates over a whole biome. That is a world-gen question about the
stand field, not a rendering constant."

`CROWN_M` = **34 m**, measured rather than picked: the broadleaf's crown is
8.4 x 10.5 m and the conifers 3.85 and 2.9, so a clump of three or four
adjacent crowns plus the gap between clumps is a feature about thirty metres
across. `crownWeightAt(x, y, z, altM)` is exported as the named accessor and is
what `sampleChunk` now hands the parked term. **NOTHING IS WIRED:**
`Config.canopyShade` is still default OFF and Admin's ruling is still open;
what changed is the SHAPE of the arm being ruled on.

Measured over the 21,416 understorey cells the term actually multiplies (the
78 m floor, not the whole disc), at forestfloor: the crown-scale field reads
**0.2441 +/- 0.3210, max 1.0** against the pre-WG-221 field's **0.6507 +/-
0.4068** over the identical cells. Armed at `CANOPY_SHADE` 0.45 that is an 11
per cent mean thinning instead of 29 to 38, which is 2.14.7b's own prescription
(patchiness rather than magnitude). **STATED HONESTLY: the standard deviation is
not the patchiness claim**, the old field's spread is large because a 78 m
floor straddles one 165 m stand boundary, which is one edge and not patchiness.
The claim is the field's characteristic LENGTH, 34 m against 165, and it is
authored rather than judged. **OWED to whoever takes the ruling: the
`?canopyshade=1` frame pair at forestfloor, which is the only instrument that
can settle it.**

**AN INSTRUMENT DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED INSIDE THIS LANE'S OWN COUNTER.** The
first version accumulated at every canopy-offered cell out to the whole reach
and read **0.32 against 0.16**, a real-looking pair about a region the shade
term never multiplies. 2.14.7b's claim is about the floor the player is looking
at, and the understorey exists only inside `DETAIL_RADIUS_M`. The comparator was
wrong too: quoting `planetW` (which already carries this lane's grove mask) as
"what 2.14.7b measured" would have credited the grove field to the field it is
being compared against; it is now `canopyWeight(altM, stand, 1)`, the
pre-WG-221 function exactly.

#### 6.9.10 WG-227, the aerial pose over forest, and the two judgements

RN-2225's owed item 3 ("the `vista` pose is blind to vegetation ... a new aerial
judgement pose over the Forest biome is owed") is paid: `artframe.js` gains
**`forestair`**, every field `flyover`'s to the digit except lat/lon, at
RN-2065's own surveyed forest site (-19.85, -72.7853). A shot and not an
`--evalargs` override, on `mtnslope`'s recorded reason that "an override is a
pose that exists in one shell history". It is registered in the pose block as
well as in `SHOTS`, which that block's own comment says is what stops a shot
photographing wherever the scenario spawned.

**FLYOVER, JUDGED** (`WG2220_flyover_{before,after}.png`). The before frame is
RN-2225's speckle: a green-grey plate with a scatter of dark grit on it, evenly
spread, carrying no structure at any scale, the eye reads it as noise on a
texture rather than as objects on ground, and the only shape in the lower half
of the frame is the cloud shadow. The after frame is a different KIND of
picture. There are woods with edges: a dense mass running off the bottom-left
corner, a second across the centre with a ragged margin, bare ground between
them wide enough to read as fields rather than as gaps, and a thinner stippling
carrying the wooded read out to where the haze takes it. Nothing in the frame
is a circle and there is still no line where the trees stop. **Honest limit: it
reads as WOODED COUNTRY, not as forest.** The site is Hills, whose table is 72
stems a hectare on purpose, and at 15 per cent cover the ground between crowns
is still the majority of the picture. The clumps also read a little as scrub
rather than as canopy, which is the crown asset and not the density: nine
pixels of a green blob has no canopy texture in it, and that is RN-2240's card
plus an asset pass, not another multiplier here.

**FORESTAIR, JUDGED** (`WG2220_forestair_{before,after}.png`). This is the
stronger pair and it is the one that answers the brief's question. Before: the
Forest biome from 1,200 m is a faint even sprinkle of single dark specks over a
dark blue-grey ground, indistinguishable at a glance from sensor noise, and
there is no place in the frame where you could point and say "that is a wood".
After: two large wood masses with real shapes, one filling the bottom-left
corner, one a rounded block through the lower centre with a distinctly
undercut edge and a clearing bitten into its middle, and open ground around
them that is unambiguously open. **That reads as a forested world with
clearings**, and the difference between the two frames is entirely structure
rather than brightness. **Honest limit on this pair too: the frame is under a
large cloud shadow**, so both arms are dim and the sunlit canopy is only
visible in the bottom-left corner, where it does show a proper closed texture.
A brighter phase at this site would be a better judgement frame and the pose
carries `flyover`'s sun pin rather than a chosen one; recorded rather than
re-rolled, because changing the sun to flatter the after arm is the one thing
a hero-frame pair may not do.

**VISTA** (`WG2220_vista_{before,after}.png`): identical, correctly, and
measured so (6.9.6). The site is a 4.7 km ridge above the treeline.

#### 6.9.11 Owed

1. **RN-2240 is the next move on this tier and this lane is sized against it.**
   Dividing the per-tree cost by four takes `CANOPY_FAR_RADIUS_M` back past
   4,200 m and `CANOPY_GROUND_REACH_M` back to 2,000 at the same frame, or buys
   another 2 to 3x of table at the shipped reach. Cover in the 30 to 40 per
   cent band is reachable from there and is not reachable today.
2. **The crown asset, not the count, is what limits the read now** (6.9.10).
3. **The grove seam at 550 to 690 m** (6.9.2), and it is a decision, not a fix.
4. **The `?canopyshade=1` frame pair** for Admin's open ruling (6.9.9).
5. **`MAX_PER_CHUNK` was not reached and was not raised.** `chunksCapped` and
   `cellsCapped` read 0 at every pose measured, but `want` is already pinned at
   the 14,000 cap for essentially every chunk (`full.total` counts the
   understorey over the whole chunk area rather than over its ring), so the
   headroom is unmeasured rather than large. A further density raise must
   re-read those two counters before it is believed.

### 6.10 WG-230 to WG-234: the per-chunk float64 world phase reaches the shader

Branch `lane/wg-phase`, 2026-08-21, base `origin/main` at `e7b8f037`. Frames
`docs/screenshots/WG230_*`. Numbers block WG-230 to WG-239 allocated;
**WG-230 to WG-234 used, WG-235 to WG-239 surrendered unused.**

#### 6.10.1 The finding that reshaped the lane: half of it was already written

`web/src/world/ChunkPhase.ts` has existed since 2026-07-28 (WG-50/WG-51), with
the float64 reduction, the shader contract, the error model and two precision
tools under `web/tools/precision/`. **Nothing in `web/src` has ever imported
it.** `TerrainArt.glsl.ts`'s header still names the phase as owed, and
`TerrainSplat.ts`'s does too, because the CPU half shipped and the path to the
GPU never did. This lane is that path, not a redesign; WG-50's arithmetic is
reused unchanged and its `reduceAxis` is the one place the float64 division
happens.

#### 6.10.2 The shape, and where it departs from WG-50 on purpose

WG-50 published a `(cell, frac)` pair per octave, six floats, because its
intended consumer was a value-noise octave that hashes an integer lattice cell
and repeats without a globally correct one. The consumer that actually arrived
is the far-field **splat** rung (RN-2160's phase 2), and **a tiling texture
fetch never sees an integer cell** — a wrapping sampler consumes only the
fractional part. So the shipped attribute is `frac` alone:

`aPhase`, **vec3 float32, per-chunk constant replicated across the slot's
vertices**, equal to `frac(anchor / PHASE_PERIOD_M)` per axis. The vertex shader
forms `vPhase = aPhase + position / PHASE_PERIOD_M`.

Three floats rather than six is not a micro-optimisation at this vertex count:
it is 6.4 MiB of pooled vertex memory rather than 12.8. `cell` stays exported
in `ChunkPhase.ts` for the day a lattice consumer wants it.

**It is an ATTRIBUTE and not a uniform because a `BatchedMesh` draws every
resident chunk in one multi-draw** (DW-11), so a per-chunk uniform does not
exist inside one. That is RN-2265's `aCanopy` precedent and `aFadeT0`'s before
it.

**It is deliberately absent from `ChunkBatch.stitched()`**, which is the one
interesting asymmetry against `aCanopy`. The canopy index is a function of each
vertex's own position and must be re-derived when `EdgeStitch` snaps one; the
phase is a function of the CHUNK, and the per-vertex half of the coordinate is
`position` itself, which the stitch has already rewritten. `aPhase + position/P`
follows a snapped vertex for free.

#### 6.10.3 The wire format is untouched, and versioning is therefore moot

No byte of the 28 B/vertex packed layout moves, no `of_packed_*` export changes,
no ABI bump, no parity fixture re-baseline. The quantity is a pure float64
function of `ChunkView.anchor`, which the chunk already carries and which
`ChunkBatch` has retained beside every slot since RN-2265, so the entire
plumbing cost outside the shader is one private method. This is WG-50's own
refusal held to: putting the phase in the wire format is the one version of it
that would move the parity bytes and force a 108/108 cross-toolchain
re-baseline.

Determinism follows from **where** the quantity lives rather than from care: it
reads an anchor generation has already produced and produces a shading value
generation never reads back. Terrain height, biome, solidity, deposits and the
save are untouched by construction.

#### 6.10.4 PHASE_PERIOD_M = 256 is a precision budget, and the seam rule

The shader forms `t = aPhase + position / P` in float32, so the quantum of the
reconstructed coordinate is `ulp(t) * P`, i.e. **the period divided by about
2^23. Doubling the period doubles the quantum everywhere.** 256 m holds the
quantum at 30.5 um for every chunk whose own half-extent is under the period,
which is every chunk resident inside about 600 m — the whole of the band phase 2
exists for. The other end of the trade is stated rather than hidden: 256 m is
the coarsest world-locked field a consumer can build on this attribute, and one
wanting a kilometre-scale wavelength needs a SECOND reduction at that period
rather than a larger value here.

**THE SEAM RULE IS THE ONLY RULE AND IT IS ONE LINE.** Two chunks reaching the
same ground hold `vPhase` values differing by an exact INTEGER, so
`fract(vPhase * n)` agrees across the boundary for integer `n` and for nothing
else. A consumer picking 3.0 m out of a 256 m period draws a line along every
chunk boundary in the frame, and draws it only at range where the boundaries are
far apart and the cause is least obvious. `assertPhasePeriod(periodM, who)`
throws on it at module load; `phaseState()` publishes `divides` for the period a
probe asks about.

#### 6.10.5 The precision arithmetic, computed and then measured

`web/tools/smoke/wg230phase.ts`, esbuild-bundled, fresh node process. `pM`'s
quantum is `2^(18-23)` = **31.25 mm**, WG-50's measured typical case for a
generic point on a 600 km sphere (the 62.5 mm in TerrainArt's header is the
worst case). Footprint is 3.56 mm at 2 m on the 1280x720 fov-60 camera. The
resident quad edge comes from the shipped `splitRatio` 1.4 and `maxDepth` 15.

| view | resident quad | local radius | pixel footprint | `pM` quantum | phase quantum | ratio | `pM` quanta/px | phase quanta/px |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 m | 28.93 m | 20.5 m | 3.56 mm | 31.25 mm | **30.5 um** | **1024x** | **0.114** | 117 |
| 75 m | 57.86 m | 40.9 m | 0.1335 m | 31.25 mm | **30.5 um** | **1024x** | **4.27** | 4,375 |
| 600 m | 462.88 m | 327.3 m | 1.068 m | 31.25 mm | 61.0 um | 512x | 34.2 | 17,500 |
| 37,947 m | 29,624 m | 20,948 m | 67.55 m | 31.25 mm | 1.95 mm | 16x | 2,161 | 34,580 |

**The measured worst reconstruction error, float32 at every step over 768 real
quadtree anchors, tracks the predicted quantum:** 17.9 um at the max-depth quad,
52.2 um at 600 m, 1.902 mm at the horizon.

**READ THE `pM` quanta-per-pixel COLUMN, NOT THE RATIO COLUMN.** The ratio is
largest where it matters least. What decides whether a screen derivative
survives is quanta per pixel, and `pM` carries **0.114 at two metres** (one
quantum spans 8.77 pixels, WG-50's own figure, which is why the surface-art bump
has to fade out) and **4.27 at 75 m** — four discrete levels for `dFdx` to pick
a mip from, organised into arcs of constant range from the eye. That is the
acute band, and it is the near end of phase 2's own 75-to-600 m charter. By
600 m `pM` is at 34 quanta a pixel and the precision argument alone would not
justify the attribute.

**THE PRECISION IS NOT THE WHOLE REASON AND SHOULD NOT BE SOLD AS IT.** The
blocking defect `TerrainSplat.ts` states is structural: the splat rides
`vChunkUv`, which normalises over the quad, so a layer's world scale DOUBLES at
every LOD ring. A far rung on the chunk UV would put a scale step at every
quadtree ring boundary inside the band phase 2 has to make seamless. `vPhase` is
the only coordinate in this material that is both world-locked and resolvable.

The invariant worth keeping: **the phase pins the shading coordinate's quantum
to the CHUNK's own extent instead of the BODY's radius, which is the same
precision the vertex positions the chunk is built from already carry.** You
cannot do better than the geometry, and now you do not do worse. It is also why
the improvement shrinks with range: the chunk grows as the quadtree coarsens.

#### 6.10.6 The alternative that is free, and the arithmetic that refuses it

A per-frame `frac(origin / P)` UNIFORM would need no attribute, no varying and
no per-vertex bytes at all: `pM = vWorld - uBodyCenter`, and `uBodyCenter` is
the planet-scale term, so reducing it on the CPU in float64 and adding
`vWorld / P` reconstructs the same world-locked coordinate. It was refused on
one number. `FloatingOrigin.thresholdM` is **4,000 m**, so `|vWorld|` for the
ground under the player's feet ranges over `[0, 4000)` m between rebases, and
the near-field quantum would therefore breathe by up to 2^11 across one rebase
cycle — a shading resolution that is a function of how far the player has walked
since the last rebase. The per-chunk anchor's is a constant of the chunk. That
alternative is recorded rather than discarded because it is the cheap answer and
someone will propose it again.

#### 6.10.7 The consumer stub, and why a stub lane ships a probe

`vPhase` is declared, `ofPhaseWrap(repeats)` and `ofPhaseMetres()` are the two
documented helpers (`TerrainPhase.glsl.ts`), and `uPhaseProbe` gates a checker
whose **shipped amplitude is 0**, so the committed frame multiplies albedo by
exactly 1.0.

A declaration nothing reads proves nothing: the GLSL linker strips an unused
varying, the attribute goes unbound, and the whole path is dead while every
counter reads correct — RN-2268's scar exactly. `?phaseamp=` and `?phaserep=`
(both registered in `run.mjs`'s `PAGE_PARAMS` in the same commit, RN-152's scar)
photograph the wire instead. **The repeats are a parameter and not a constant
because the first version fixed them at a 2 m checker and the aerial frame
returned moire** — an instrument that gives the same picture for "working" and
"broken" at the range the term is for.

- `docs/screenshots/WG230_forestair_probe.png`, 1.2 km altitude, `?phaserep=4`
  (a 64 m check): the pattern holds **one world size from under the aircraft to
  the 38 km horizon, across every LOD ring, with no line at any chunk
  boundary.** That is the property `vChunkUv` cannot have and is the frame the
  L1 lane should read.
- `docs/screenshots/WG230_mtnslope_probe.png`, bare rock at the standing eye,
  `?phaserep=16`.
- `docs/screenshots/WG230_forestfloor_after.png` is the shipped (amplitude 0)
  frame.

#### 6.10.8 The no-op proof

One flag is not available here because the default IS off, so the control is a
genuine **two-build pair**: `origin/main` at `e7b8f037` against this branch,
three fresh processes an arm, one owned `vite preview` on 127.0.0.1:5713 with a
sentinel written into `dist` and fetched back over the wire before each arm,
killed by PID.

| | forestfloor before | forestfloor after | meadow before | meadow after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `propsPlaced` / `cellsScattered` | **41,300 / 25,655** | **41,300 / 25,655** | **52,139 / 26,124** | **52,139 / 26,124** |
| `box` luma / p05 / p50 / p95 / iqr / loFrac | 22.82 / 4.64 / 18.29 / 54.93 / 18.78 / 0.677 | identical | 85.59 / 25.44 / 84.21 / 155.5 / 55.05 / 0.05 | identical |
| `skyHz` / `hzBand` / `mid` / `nearG` / `shade` | -- | -- | 198.80 / 207.71 / 118.96 / 80.58 / 89.14 | identical |
| draw calls / programs | 75 / 60 | 75 / 60 | 75 / 45 | 75 / 45 |

Every published figure is identical to the digit in all six pairs. `skyHi` reads
175.68 or 175.69 and jitters **within** both arms, which is the same one-count
sky jitter 6.9.7 already records; triangles carry the same pose-own jitter both
arms (1,280,886 / 1,280,910 at forestfloor). **Programs identical is the
statement that the isolator is a uniform and not a define**, so there is no new
shader permutation and the pair is one build.

**THE COST IS NOT ZERO AND IS NOT REPORTED AS IF IT WERE.** Pool preallocation
**29.2 -> 37.8 MiB** and `render.vramMB` **106.3 -> 114.8** in every pair; per
pooled chunk 79,771 -> 103,097 B over 460 near+far slots. Of the 16 B/vertex,
**12 are the attribute (6.41 MiB)** and 4 are a correction to a byte count that
had been attributing `aCanopy`'s four bytes to padding (2.14 MiB). RN-2166 warns
that `vramMB` does not count textures; it evidently does count buffers, and the
delta reproduces the arithmetic exactly, which is why it is quoted here.

**OWED, AND VISIBLE FROM THIS LANE:** `aFadeT0` (4 B), `aCanopy` (4 B) and
`aPhase` (12 B) are all per-chunk constants replicated across 1,217 vertices —
2.5 KB of real information carried in 3.1 MB of buffer. The consolidation is one
slot-indexed data texture read through three's own
`getIndirectIndex(gl_DrawID)`. Refused here because it would rewrite two other
lanes' shipped attributes to land one new one.

#### 6.10.9 Determinism

`reduceAnchorPhase` over 768 real quadtree chunk centres (an 8x8 block at every
depth 4 to 15 at the `forestfloor` site) returns FNV-1a32 **`642e0671`**, mean
0.487939604617989, min 0.000270147953415, max 0.999728322029114, **identical
across three fresh node processes**. The spread is published beside the digest
because a digest of an all-zero field is also perfectly stable.

The stronger claim is structural and is 6.10.3's: the reduction is an exact
float64 function of an anchor the parity fixture already pins, and generation
never reads it back, so it cannot be a new determinism risk. No texgen tile was
added, `assets/textures/dist` is untouched, and `web/wasm/dist/*` and
`test/expected.json` are untouched.

#### 6.10.10 What the L1 far-ground lane inherits

- `vPhase`, world-locked, resolvable, seam-free for integer repeats, live and
  photographed.
- `ofPhaseWrap(repeats)` -> `[0,1)^3` at period `256 / repeats` m;
  `ofPhaseMetres()` -> the same in metres modulo 256.
- `assertPhasePeriod(tileM, who)` at module load, returning the integer repeats
  to multiply by. **Call it.** A non-dividing tile is the one input that makes
  this attribute draw a seam.
- `phaseState()` on `window.__ofTerrainArt`, and the `phase` block in
  `artframe.js`'s report.
- **A note this lane cannot act on:** `TerrainArt.glsl.ts`'s header and
  `TerrainSplat.ts`'s UV-scheme note both still say the phase is owed. Those are
  the far-ground lane's files and the prose is left for whoever consumes the
  attribute to correct in the same commit that consumes it.

## 7. Open questions & risks
- **R21 (new, WG-217, 2026-08-03, MINE by domain, routed to its own pass): a handful of surface-nets vertex normals disagree with their own triangle winding at a levelled pad's cut rim.** Measured at four sites: 0 of 2,362 and 1 of 2,288 at the WG-214 spawn, 0 of 2,778 at the old spawn's pad, and **10 of 2,156 with 1 triangle facing INTO the rock** at a Mountains hillside 3 km off the old pad on HEAD's own terrain. **Pre-existing, and it reproduces at HEAD with no change of mine.** A disagreeing normal means that triangle is lit as one surface and drawn as its opposite. `test_voxel_field.cpp` now carries a ceiling of 4 on both scenes so it cannot get worse silently, and `inward == 0` is strict. The hunt belongs in `surface_nets.h` and wants its own pass: the likely shape is an ill-conditioned gradient on a near-degenerate sliver, which is a numerical-robustness question rather than a winding one.
- **R19 UPDATE (WG-214, 2026-08-03): CLOSED by moving the spawn.** Reid ruled it. The spawn is now a Hills valley floor at 797.6 m with ~1,296 trees in the shipped ring, and `core/tests/test_spawn.cpp` is the gate that would have caught the original drift. What remains open from it is the CLIENT-SIDE re-baseline, which needs a browser and is deliberately not done: see the owed list in the status line.
- **R20 UPDATE (WG-218, 2026-08-03): ASSESSED AND LEFT, with the cost written down.** Deriving `homeDir` from the seed needs cross-toolchain-exact trig (DW-14), a cheap runtime spawn SEARCH (the offline one sweeps 400,000 directions), and the same treatment for `pondDir` and the pad. Not a bounded add-on to a spawn move. `?seed=` still moves the world under a fixed spawn point.
- **R19 (new, WG-207, 2026-08-03, ADMIN ARBITRATION, and it blocks the storyline rather than this lane): the spawn is in the Mountains 2,817.8 m above the bare treeline, and the first goal of Reid's progression spine is "gather wood".** Measured at HEAD through the shipped oracle: ground **4,667.789 m**, biome **Mountains**, against `Config.ts:31`'s written claim of "a Hills valley floor at 2,963 m". `site_probe` at 20,000 candidates a band finds **0 below `TREELINE_BARE_M` (1850 m) and 100% Mountains** at 700 to 1400 m, 1500 to 6000 m and 6000 to 20000 m; the first sub-treeline sample is at 24 to 28 km (1 of 12,000) and only 3.9% of the band is below it by 28 to 34 km. **The only wood within 20 km of spawn is the 14 hand-placed trees of the starter spiral**, and `TreeTuning.ts:193` already says so in a comment that nothing acts on. Not fixed here: moving `homeDir` re-baselines the pad, the pond, every probe, every screenshot and every save, and the WG-55 shortlist in section 6.1 is still sitting unpicked. **Whichever way it goes, `poi.h` needs no edit**: the ruin's anchor is `body.homeDir`, so the ruin moves with the spawn.
- **R20 (new, WG-206, 2026-08-03, low priority, recorded because nothing else in the project states it): `?seed=` does not move the spawn.** `BodyParams::homeDir` is a LITERAL unit vector (DW-14 forbids computing it, since a 1-ULP difference hashes the pad to an unrelated height) calibrated for the default seed `0x0bf00d01`. On seed `0x7` that same point is **Ocean**, and `generateSites` correctly refuses all 4096 candidates with a histogram saying so. A seed moves the world under a fixed spawn point. Anyone using `?seed=` for a screenshot or a probe should know the spawn may be at sea.
- **R15 (new, WG-141, 2026-08-02, ADMIN SEQUENCING, and this is the one to read first): the moon is live in `/core` and NOT YET in the browser, on purpose.** `web/wasm/dist/of-core.wasm` and `web/wasm/test/expected.json` are regenerated in the working tree (and every screenshot in this pass was taken against them) but are **deliberately NOT COMMITTED**. Two physics commits, `9e19ddc` and `ebdeed8`, landed after my build and touch `core/include/of/orbital.h` and `core/include/of/transfer.h`, which the wasm build reads; committing my binary would ship a wasm missing their Cinder-transfer work, which is a revert wearing a binary. The committed header and the committed binary therefore disagree until **someone rebuilds from a settled tree**, at which point the fixture moves by exactly 24 leaf values, all of them moon values, and Forge does not move at all. That rebuild is an Admin sequencing call, not mine to make alone.
- **R16 (new, WG-147, 2026-08-02, gameplay via Admin): 51 starter harvest nodes place on Cinder regardless of body, and 14 of them are trees.** The streaming fields are correct and prove it: `trees.live` 0 and `rocks.live` 0 at all four probed sites with 5,984 and 632 `biomeZeroCells`, so `TREE_DENSITY_KM2`'s moon zeros are working exactly as written. The nodes that DO appear are the fixed golden-angle starter spiral (`NodeField.ts`, WG-59), which is placed once at world init and consults no biome and no body: measured `TreeBroadleaf` 8, `TreeConifer` 6, plus 37 ore boulders, unchanged across four teleports. **Broadleaf trees on an airless moon.** Whether Cinder should carry a starter set at all is a gameplay decision, not a world-gen one (Reid arrives by rocket having already done the tutorial on Forge), so this is routed rather than patched.
- **R17 (WG-141, 2026-08-02, STILL OPEN with two eliminations and one vacuous test): thin bright streaks follow the terrain contours** at grazing light (`docs/screenshots/WG141_ground_craterfloor.png`). **Strata is ELIMINATED by a controlled pair**: `WG141_r17_pair_s1.png` against `WG141_r17_pair_s0.png` differ on 1.21% of pixels, but the streaks are present in BOTH, and the probe confirms the terrain underneath is bit-identical with strata on and off (biome 9, relief -796.3198 m, curvature equal to six decimals), so it is shading and it is not the bedding layer. **Geometry is eliminated** by the same equality. **The grazing-incidence hypothesis could not be tested, because the test was vacuous and I am recording that rather than the conclusion I wanted:** `--sundot=0.28` and `--sundot=0.92` produce visually identical frames at this site, same sun disc, same shadows (`WG141_r17_highsun.png`). So `sundot` is registered in `run.mjs`'s `PAGE_PARAMS` and does not move the picture in the walk scenario, which is its own small finding and probably means the sun is being solved from `t` instead. Next candidates: the detail-card layer, chunk skirts, or the moon rows of the micro-detail tables. **One correction worth keeping: I nearly reported a determinism anomaly here that was me misreading a HUD value off a screenshot.** The strata=0 frame appeared to say "1.34 km relief biome 8" at a site the probe reports as biome 9 at -796.3 m. The probe was right and the reading was wrong. Reading a number off a picture is not measuring it; the picture is the arbiter for the LOOK and a probe is the arbiter for a NUMBER.
- **R18 (WG-141, CLOSED 2026-08-02 by WG-149 as to the instrument; the cost itself stands as a watch item.)** The meter is fixed: crater cell evaluations are counted as their own unit and `terrain_probe` refuses to present the valueNoise number as a cratered body's cost. The measured blindness was worse than the estimate in the original text below: **10 valueNoise calls against 113 crater cell evaluations per sample**. What remains open is not the instrument but the number it now reports honestly, and it still needs measuring in the BROWSER rather than natively, because nothing has yet exercised the streaming budget on a body whose 200 km radius makes quads physically smaller at the same depth. Original text:  **the moon costs 2.21x what it did and 2.93x what Forge does** (492,507 verts/sec against Forge's 1,442,346), measured by `procgen_bench`. Nine crater rungs is the reason and the confined variant is already what keeps it from being far worse. Not yet a problem, because nothing has measured the streaming budget on a body this small, where a 200 km radius means quads are physically smaller at the same depth. It needs measuring in the browser before anyone calls it fine. Related instrument note: `OF_COUNT_NOISE` counts `valueNoise` only, and the crater fields hash cells directly, so **the noise counter understates moon cost by roughly ten valueNoise-equivalents per sample** and must not be used to budget this body.

- **R11 (new, WG-121, 2026-08-01, owed by this lane): the tree slope gate has never been seen to fire.** Proved unable to fire on the DESIGNED surface (steepest sub-treeline ground 29.15 degrees against a 43.95 degree gate, 26,120 samples). Its only reachable case is the EDITED surface, because the sample goes through `editsHandle`: dig a pit or level a terrace under a live tree ring and the ground under a candidate becomes steep. Needs a driven dig probe. Until then the gate is carried on an argument, not a measurement.
- **R12 (new, WG-122, 2026-08-01, rendering, and it is WG-63's ask restated with a second customer): the tree ring's 620 m edge pops.** Nothing in the client can draw a tree past that, and a distance fade is now structurally refused (WG-122), so the only remaining answer is the far-field impostor layer. This lane has no billboard, impostor or card mechanism to build on.
- **R13 (new, WG-119, 2026-08-01, Blender via Admin): `props_canopy.glb`'s three tall species cannot become node art.** They have no `Full`/`Half`/`Low` depletion variants, so the parasol and fir forms the RN-271 flora pass authored are currently unreachable by the harvest path and the world's big trees are scaled harvest trees instead. Three variants each (plus the existing LOD tiers) would let `NodeArt` carry them and would put those silhouettes back.
- **R14 (new, WG-120, 2026-08-01, owed by this lane): `RockField` never forgets a rock.** `TreeField.forget` drops untouched entries when a cell streams out; the rock stream keeps every rock it ever materialised, and `serialize` pays a WASM read per entry. Immaterial at 49 rocks; the same shape at any density increase is unbounded. `RockField.ts` belongs to the sibling world-gen lane this pass, so this is routed rather than done.
- **R9 (new, WG-92, 2026-08-01, needs the asset lane via Admin): the decoration cap and the visibility floor have collided in Mountains.** RN-247's corrected authored cap is 0.174 m; the measured floor for a rock decoration to be distinguishable from the `Detail_PebbleScatter` layer the biome already runs at 70x the density is ABOVE it. So no Mountains rock decoration can both be seen and be honest, and the three RN-245 stems are unwired. Resolving it needs ONE of: a Mountains-specific `DRY_DETAIL` with less gravel, a decoration form whose read is vertical relief rather than coverage, or an argued exemption. Not a client change on its own.
- **R10 (WG-98, CLOSED 2026-08-01 by WG-118): a harvest node's LOD0 is paid FOUR times.** Answered in this lane rather than routed: the node batch now loads the `_LOD1` and `_LOD2` meshes the assets always shipped and culls per instance, so the four draws are of the tier the distance deserves. Worth 111 k to 135 k triangles at sites with NO trees in them, which is the rocks and the spires and the clearing collecting on it. Original text:  `NodeBatch` sets `castShadow = true` and there are three cascades, measured exactly (1,704 = 426 x 4 per stone boulder). At the Mountains site the rock layer is 11.6% of frame triangles. Taking nodes out of the far cascades, or giving them a shadow-only proxy, is the biggest single lever on node cost and it is rendering's call, not mine.
- **R1:** Heightfield↔voxel-patch seam (rendering + collision continuity at dig boundaries). **API seam now built** (Spike 1 §4.4: `bFromVoxelPatch` in every result struct, `ApplyPatchOverride` no-op stub — callers branch on it today); implementation deferred to Phase 4 (D-005). *(Spike 1 WR4)*
- **R2:** Noise cost vs streaming budget at low LOD over large bodies. **Mitigated by design:** LOD octave-gating (Spike 1 §2) + per-tick generation budget (§3.2); validate gate WV8. Still open until measured in-engine.
- **R3 (Admin Q4):** keep voxel door open without paying for it in v1. **Settled** by R1 seam above.
- **R4 (new, Spike 1 WR3 → Admin):** numeric body constants (radius/μ/SOI/spin, Spike 1 §5) are **shared** with core-engine's `FReferenceFrame` but core-engine pinned only the *shape*, not values. World-gen proposed Forge (planet) / Cinder (moon) numbers; **Admin to confirm single source of truth** so the two domains don't diverge. No contract conflict — value agreement only.
- **R5 (new, Spike 1 WR5 → physics):** physics collision against heightfield via **analytic queries** (built) vs **baked per-quad collision meshes** — negotiate with physics. Contract supports both; collision-mesh emission can be added to `FTerrainChunk` later without breaking callers.
- **R7 (new, 2026-07-26, the FIFTH instance of the surface class — Admin asked for a position):** the ONE oracle answers with **two SHAPES**. `solidCell` is a binary 1 m occupancy grid (a cell is solid when its CENTRE is at or below the designed surface); `surfaceHeight` is a smooth continuous field. A binary lattice can only approximate a smooth surface to within half a cell diagonal, so the shell stands up to **0.87 m proud** of the walkable ground (measured: air 0.15 m above the surface reads solid on 60.6% of walking ticks — ARCHITECTURE 15.2 item 93). **Position: neither offered option is right.** Changing the quantisation in `surface_field.h` CANNOT succeed, because the mismatch is not a bad threshold, it is inherent to the representation: any rule leaves a boundary layer up to one cell thick that one shape claims and the other does not, so moving the threshold moves WHERE the disagreement is and not WHETHER it exists (and it would re-baseline every voxel pin for nothing). Per-consumer reconciliation is what produced five surfaces: `VoxelCollision.solidForWalker` is correct for the walker and is the SIXTH definition unless it is promoted. **The third option: name both shapes in the oracle and make the choice a parameter rather than a rediscovery.** `surface_field.h` should publish `solidCell` (the VOXEL shell — what the mesher draws and what a dig carves; you must draw whole cells) alongside a `solidBelowSurface` (the shell INTERSECTED with the smooth surface — what a body occupies), with a header note stating the bound explicitly, and a ctest that ASSERTS it: for a sample of directions the two answers may differ by at most half a cell diagonal. A property test on the SIZE of a known disagreement is what turns "silently 0.87 m" into "loudly, at most 0.87 m". **WG-22 hit the same class from the other side:** `levelArea` decides per cell CENTRE while `derivedRaisingAt`/`derivedLoweringAt` probe per COLUMN in 1 m steps, so a rim column is legitimately half cut and reports its original height (15.2 item 99). Same root, opposite sign. Not changed this pass (Admin did not ask for a core change); proposed for the next.
- **R6 (new, Spike 1 WR1):** cube-face-corner seam determinism under tangent-warp float error — mitigated by canonical-edge ownership (lower faceId owns a shared edge so both faces read identical doubles); validate WV2.
